Kate doesn't know when 'Kate Bishop' becomes a term of endearment in their friendship, but she's not willing to question it.

    Chapter 1

    As long as it means that Yelena is here to stay, Kate is more than happy to hear her call her by her full name for the rest of their lives.

    talking to the enemy It doesn’t take long for Kate to discover that she has a bad habit of talking to the enemy.

    Clint has been on her ass about it since the moment it started. It’s always, focus on the job Kate and stop asking people how their mothers are Kate and, her all-time favorite: if you hand out skincare tips one more time I will kill you myself, Kate.

    She doesn’t understand how he can be so serious all the time. Granted, she’s seen her mentor smile at some of her jokes, and - if he’s feeling extra peppy on the day - he sometimes makes some of his own. His humour is dry, and his sarcastic quips play off of Kate’s perfectly. It always makes Kate grin from ear to ear as Clint grumbles about her being a bad influence on him.

    (Honestly, the fact that she can have any kind of influence on the Hawkeye is disorienting in its own right.)

    That being said, she will reluctantly admit that this bad habit is getting a little out of hand.

    So out of hand that when she hears, “Kate Bishop~” being drawled out from behind her, she barely has half the mind to whip around and lift the butter knife in what is half-assed protection. Her mind doesn’t even have time to process the situation as dangerous.

    Though the surprise does hit her head-on.

    Kate resists the urge to let her jaw hang open. Which is an impressive feat when a Black Widow is standing in front of her.

    Yelena, in all her high-quality fashion-clad glory, is standing in her living room. And Kate doesn’t know how to feel about that, because her heart is beating a hundred miles an hour but there’s a faint sensation of relief and excitement.

    If Yelena notices her eagerness, she doesn’t let her know. The assassin is calm, casual and attractive as ever with her hands in the pockets of her white jacket like the last time they had seen each other they hadn’t been duking it out at the Rockefeller Centre.

    Is she here to kill me?

    I mean, if she were I would be dead.

    An upside of her bad habit, Kate thinks, is being able to recover quickly when she’s caught off-guard.

    “Yelena~,” She throws back with a grin. “If it isn’t my favorite assassin.”

    Said assassin grins and hums as she moves towards her, taking her surprised welcoming tone as an invitation to make herself comfortable.

    Kate places the knife back on her table when Yelena brushes past her to take a seat at the table.

    “What brings you ‘round this part of the world?” Kate slinks around the table to her chair, dropping into it unceremoniously. “Are you back hoping to get your hands on the new Hawkeye?”

    She makes a show of fixing her shirt - Hawkeye merch from when she was fourteen - and tries to exude the same aura of confidence that Yelena has across from her. The woman’s eyes glint mirthfully, a knowing twist at the corners of her mouth like they’re sharing an inside joke. Which Kate supposes they are. It’s not every day someone encounters a Black Widow and lives to tell the tale.

    “Oh god no, I have had enough of hawks,” she says, face souring as she waves her words away.

    Kate blinks. She can feel her mouth make a perfect ‘oh’, her voice shaky as she says, “is that so…”

    “Yes. Clint Barton has ruined them for me.” Yelena’s head shakes, gold hair spilling into her face. “Now I look at a hawk and I think of an old man.”

    A laugh tumbles out of Kate before she can catch it. She schools her expression quickly.

    “Clint isn’t that old- he’s not! And-”

    There’s a satisfied smirk on Yelena’s face that sends a shiver down Kate’s spine.

    “Don’t change the subject.”

    “You’re right,” the assassin agrees swiftly. “I don’t want to talk about him.”

    Kate jumps on the opportunity. “Yes, we should be talking about the new Hawkeye in town.”

    When Clint had brought up the idea to her on Christmas Day, Kate had felt like everything that had happened that week was a figment of her imagination. Everything felt so surreal, it was almost impossible to believe it. Kate didn’t really meet Hawkeye, and she didn’t destroy the Christmas tree at Rockefeller Centre, and she didn’t send her mother to prison. Nothing that had happened was real.

    Except Kate definitely slipped on the walk back to Clint’s house in her excitement, and she definitely had that bruise on her ass for weeks after.

    It was real. Everything was.

    So of course she’s been telling anyone who will listen that she is the new Hawkeye in town.

    Second Hawkeye.

    (God, since when did Clint get in her head?)

    Yelena, on the other hand, doesn’t seem to share her excitement.

    “‘New’? Another one?” Yelena slumps in the chair, tilting her head back with a loud, guttural groan that has Kate raising her eyebrows in amusements. “You know, this is exactly the last thing that the world needed - right after the garbage TV shows you like so much.”

    “How do you know what shows I like to watch?”

    And Kate realizes how stupid the question is as soon as she’s done asking it, because then she’s saying “You know a lot about me,” at the same time Yelena says “I know a lot about you, Kate Bishop.”

    “Yeah, yeah.”

    “So defensive…” Yelena grins, smile wide and eyes impossibly bright.

    She has a pretty smile… are all Black Widows attractive? Is it in their job description to be attractive?

    Clearing her throat - and thoughts - Kate straightens in her seat. “Well, I hate to rain on your parade-”

    “What parade?”

    “What- It’s… what?”

    “What a strange thing to say, Kate Bishop.” The assassin frowns almost pensively, her hand coming to rest on the table in front of her.

    Her rings look cool… she has pretty hands- I should ask her where she gets her nails done - oh my god, stop staring at her hands.

    “It’s a saying- to rain on someone’s parade is a saying-” Kate shakes her head. “Whatever, what I’m trying to say is-”

    “It is a silly saying.”

    “Yes, thank you for your input.”

    “It does not even sound cool.”

    Kate stares at the assassin, unsure of where the conversation is going. Nothing in Yelena’s face hints at what her hidden intentions are, so she plays into whatever game the Black Widow has planned for her. “Would you like to rate it while you’re at it?”

    “Two stars. One and a half because you made it sound weird.” Yelena ignores the sarcasm dripping from her voice, settling her with an empty look that starkly contrasts the lively expression she had before.

    “What? How did I make it sound weird?”

    “You looked excited, like raining on a party is a good thing.”

    “It’s not a good thing, that’s why I said it.”

    “Why would you say it so excited if it is not a good thing?”

    For the first time in a while, Kate is at a loss for words. She feels like she’s being spun around in circles - like Yelena is leading their conversation but she’s not leading them in any particular direction and it’s throwing her for a loop. Because she’s the one that usually does the leading. She’s the one that spins people around until they realize that they have been giving up personal information to their enemy, and then it’s just that little bit harder for them to throw a punch or fire a gun in her direction.

    But if Kate is anything, it’s stubborn. “I said it because it’s not a good thing for you, but it is a good thing for me.”

    “You want me to suffer bad things?” Yelena knows how to play the game. Maybe even better than she does.

    “You want my party to be ruined by rain?” Her head tilts to the side, an innocent frown on her face that has Kate’s heart jumping in her chest.

    “Don’t put words in my mouth,” she says, barely keeping the croak from her voice.

    “I do not put anything in your mouth,” Yelena says in confusion. At this point, Kate doesn’t know if she’s genuine or if she’s playing pretend. And then sharp eyes are snapping upwards and meeting hers and her breath catches in her throat as the woman’s voice drops a few octaves. “I will though.”

    There’s a small, wicked twist to the corner of Yelena’s mouth that Kate doesn’t know what to do with. It’s gone as soon as it appears.

    Yelena is pushing herself away from the table with a less than graceful movement. “Maybe some food - I am starving…” She saunters into Kate’s open kitchen with a familiarity that should be unnerving. “I know you have not been eating well - it is not good for you, Kate.”

    “My eating habits are none of your business.”

    The words go in one ear and fly out of the other as Yelena opens her fridge, leaning back as if to scan its contents altogether.

    Kate already knows what her reaction is going to be before she gets it.

    “ Kate , what is this?” The blonde whips her head over her shoulder to shoot her a judgemental look, eyes wide in alarm. “Where is your food?”

    “First it’s the cutlery, now it’s the fridge…” Kate slides down her chair, tucking her chin into her chest.

    She watches as the assassin haphazardly throws her kitchen cupboards open, head following the movement of her eyes as she looks at everything. Or - well, not everything - more like a half-empty box of Lucky Charms, untouched pancake mix that she hasn’t had the time to get around and use, and some spices that Jack had gifted her in February.

    Judgement permeates the room.

    “You don’t even have the mac and cheese I liked last time,” Yelena whines. She can hear the pout in her voice.

    “I don’t know what to tell you.” Kate shrugs helplessly as the assassin turns to look at her with exaggerated disappointment.

    poof! Gone. In your stomach.”

    “Mhm…” Yelena hums.

There’s a calculating look in her eyes as she takes in Kate’s renovated apartment, most likely trying to find the best exits and entrances and any hidden threats like Clint always tells Kate to do. Her head twitches in the direction of any foreign, faint sounds.

    It would be impossible to catch this woman off-guard.

    Not that Kate would try.

    The silence gets to her and she breaks first.

    “Seriously though, what brings you to New York?” She asks as Yelena drops down onto her couch, propping her feet up onto her coffee table. Kate notes the way she is careful of not touching the mountains of paperwork sitting on the surface with her boots.

    For the first time in this strange conversation, Yelena looks stumped. And Kate wants to think that the surprise that colors her green eyes is genuine because the satisfied feeling in her chest is very pleasant.

    The assassin’s voice is dubious as she asks, “you can order food on Amazon?”

    Kate laughs freely at her innocence. “Yeah, you can order everything from Amazon.”

    Leave it to Yelena to one-up her.

    “It would be very useful.” Yelena nods, bringing her hands together under her chin in what is faux thoughtfulness. “I have no more and now I have to wait. It is very frustrating.”

    Swallowing the lump in her throat, Kate mentally regains her footing in the conversation. “I mean, you could probably give Bezos a call,” she says easily, “let him know that counter-

    agent is where it’s at. Lotta’ money to be made. Widows to be freed. ”

It’s like she’s trying to crawl into her mind to see just what she’s thinking.

    “Says the person who broke into my apartment,” she shoots back. “Again.”

She’s lifting herself from her seat on the couch the next second and Kate feels her heart make a run from its place in her chest. “You wouldn’t even know I was here.”

Green eyes watch her, delight dancing all over her face as she chuckles to herself. “Ah, I missed this,” she says with a wistful grin. “You are very entertaining, Kate Bishop.”

And as much as she wants to trust Yelena’s playful disposition and lean into the natural chemistry that permeates their conversations, all she knows about the assassin is that her sister is Natasha Romanoff, she’s a Black Widow, and she really likes mac and cheese.

There’s that playfulness back in her voice as she says, “it is not going to be easy. Very boring. Very tedious. I will gouge my own eyes out in the process.”

    Rough fingers smooth over the skin on her left forearm as Yelena begins to lift her hand up to her. The back of her left hand is cradled gently in Yelena’s palm, her touch gentle in a way that is dichotomous to her entire being. It spreads a pleasant warmth over Kate’s skin.

    “Here.” Something is being pressed into the palm of her hand. “Christmas gift,” Yelena says, lips quirked.

    Kate’s eyebrows lift. “It’s June,” she says weakly.

    “Take it.” Yelena closes her fingers around the object with her other hand, squeezing it ever- so-slightly. She’s making her way back up the stairs toward the window where there’s a rope already grappled onto the railing of her stairs. “For my favorite Hawkeye.”

    A small arrow glints up at Kate under the warm light of her apartment. It’s an earring. Only one, without its pair. It’s small -

    one that dangles from her ear - but it makes her heart clench inexplicably in her chest.

    Kate laughs incredulously. Yelena had known about her becoming Hawkeye.

    “Yelena…”

    Said woman turns in place on the window ledge. She’s bathed in orange, brilliant light as the sun sets over the city. Her blonde hair looks like fire and eyes look like gold. “Have a good night, Kate Bishop.”

    Kate huffs. She shakes her head in disbelief as she moves to close the window, trying to hide the way that she leans out slightly to see if she can catch sight of blonde hair and a white jacket. When she doesn’t, she gently shuts the window and heads up to her bedroom.

    Talking to the enemy proves to be quite successful, Kate thinks as she holds the small arrow up in front of her face.

    Clint is not going to be happy with her discovery.

    “You’re like Hawkeye!”

    June is the month that the universe decides to look down at Kate and grant her wish: a more interesting life.

    Just three days after Yelena breaks into her apartment, Kate finds herself face-to-mask with Spiderman.

    It happens as she’s falling out of her window. He catches her before she can unceremoniously splat to the ground in front of the pizza place downstairs, but it still makes her bristle, ego bruised to high hell.

In her head she's already saying ‘goodbye’ to the world, praying that Clint takes care of Lucky and that Grills will delete her browser history like he promised he would. Because for once, she’s not the one that is freaking out. For once, she’s able to see just how amusing it is to watch the same panic she feels internally on a day to day basis be displayed clearly, almost like it’s an act.

    Sure am, Spiderman. Don’t mind me, just reconsidering everything. Nothing wrong with that.

    stakeout “One of these days I will let go of my arrow.”

    “Which appears will be short-lived.” The assassin flicks the tab of the can with her thumb. A satisfying hiss of the can opening has Kate licking her lips, anticipating the flavor.

    Her words are shoved right back in her face.

    Yelena glances at her briefly over the edge of the can, checking her reaction with a teasing lift of an eyebrow. Kate can see the mischief dancing around in her eyes, far too familiar with it now, but she’s still equally surprised and abhorred by the crime playing out in front of her. The betrayal is insurmountable - she doesn’t even care that her mouth drops open in shock as Yelena begins to drink out of the can. It only hangs open further as she keeps drinking even when Kate stretches her hand out in request to get her precious drink back.

    Making strangled sounds in the back of her throat, Yelena pulls her now glistening lips away from the can and twists her face into a disgusted expression. “Horrible. Horrible .” Despite the hard downward pull of her mouth, Yelena goes back for another gulp.

    “Oh, okay, yeah you can drink that,” Kate glowers. She can feel the venomous irritation in her chest goading her to slap the can out of Yelena’s hands. “Not like I was looking forward to it or anything.”

    Finally done, Yelena pulls back and lets out a small gasp as if catching her breath. “Sorry,” she says breathlessly.

    “Give it back.” Kate snatches the can out of the woman’s loose grip.

    A loud, exaggerated cry of annoyance escapes her when she feels that the can is almost, if not entirely, empty.

    Why, gods, why?

    Anger sweeps through her. She snaps her head to the woman responsible for her misfortune, but all she gets is a sheepish shrug of the shoulders and an apologetic grin that says ‘ sorry, I’m not really sorry .’

    “I was thirsty,” Yelena says innocently, though there’s a devilish twist to the corners of her lips.

    “Uh-huh…” Kate huffs to herself, swallowing her anger and replacing it with disappointment and mourning. She looks pitifully at the insides of the can.

    Briefly, the thought of putting her hands around Yelena’s neck to suffocate her flits through her head, but she stops her imagination from getting too far ahead of itself when she reminds it of all of her previous fights with the Black Widow.

    Yeah, we’re not getting thrown off the side of a building again, she thinks as she shakes the can and hears how little is left.

    She wants to cry.

    Sighing to herself, she indulges her watering mouth and finishes off whatever is left. She’s not going to let it go to waste, even if Yelena has just ruined the best part of a stakeout.

    She makes sure to make a show of getting the last drops out just to spite Yelena, tilting her head back as far as possible while hanging the can in the air as she lets the droplets of energy drink pour on her tongue and down her throat. A trickle spills over the edge of her mouth, but she’s too determined to get the remnants out to care. Once she’s sure that all of it is gone, she crushes the can in her hand and tucks it into the back of Yelena’s seat where there’s trash accumulated.

    Reminder to Future Kate to throw all that out.

    She can feel Yelena’s eyes on her. Sharp, green eyes had traced the outline of her throat when she tilted her head back, snapping back up to follow the trickle that spilt over her skin and down the side of her neck. Gaze flitting down to her uncovered arms as they flex when she crushes the can.

    “Does this mean I don't owe you that drink anymore?” Kate grins at the assassin, her gaze flicking over to her.

    For a second, she thinks that she’s finally caught Yelena off-guard because the woman doesn’t react immediately. Her eyes - usually lit up a striking green full of intent and mischief - are almost black in the darkness of the car. Empty of their usual playfulness. They reveal an unarmed thoughtfulness that has Kate’s skin crawling and heat pooling at the pit of her stomach.

    Kate swallows the lump in her throat, pressing her thighs together when those thoughtful eyes follow the movement.

    The heavy atmosphere is gone in the next instance as the Black Widow seems to slip back into her persona, smiling in amusement. “No.” Yelena turns away from her, settling comfortably in the passenger seat. Swinging her feet up onto the dashboard, she drags a knife out of its sheath and places its sharp, pointed tip on the pad of her forefinger. “You still owe me,” she rasps, accent curling around the words.

    Kate wets her lips. She lets out an airy laugh and says, “Damn it.”

    Meanwhile, she tries not to think about how her body is currently on fire and instead dives into her phone to distract herself, combing through her notifications.

    She taps on the first one and sees that Grills has sent a picture of Lucky smiling at the camera with his tongue out, a party hat perched atop his head, to the LARP+Hawkeyes group chat.

    Attached to the picture is the message: lord lucky sends his regards to all.

    [02:23] Hawkeye 2.0: all hail lord lucky

    She saves the picture to her camera roll before swapping apps to Amazon. She spends the next five minutes looking for the latest D&D edition before putting it in her basket and buying it without a second thought. It’s become a habit to present Grills with gifts every time that he dogsits for her, both to say ‘thank you’ and to bribe him into doing it more often.

    Kate swallows the surprise when she sees a message from a random number. It’s a picture of the leader of the gang she and Spiderman had confronted a week ago being handcuffed by the police in front of the police department. Along with the picture is a gif of an animated dog dancing and celebrating.

    Without a second’s hesitation, she adds the number to her contacts, naming it ‘Spidey’ with a spider emoji next to it.

    [02:27] Kate: let’s celebrate with pizza!!

    Lila’s message is just a reply to the conversation that they were having earlier that night about her English Lit. homework, but she types a response anyway, indulging the younger girl’s thoughts on the poem she had to study. Just as she hits send, a message from Spidey comes through.

    [02:29] Spidey: I’ll swing by tmrrw?

    [02:29] Kate: bring some of that meatball pizza? plz

    [02:29] Kate: I’ll get soda

    [02:30] Spidey: y r u awake?

    [02:30] Kate: stakeout

    [02:30] Spidey: o:

    [02:30] Kate: if i don’t show up tmrw tell clint it was my assassin frnd

    Kate warily glances to the quiet blonde beside her, suddenly unnerved by the silence. She hasn’t known Yelena long aside from the events at Christmas last year and the recent surprise visits, but the assassin has never been this quiet for this long. It spooks her to her core, seeing the solemn but serene expression on the woman’s face.

    The vibration of her phone in her hands brings her focus back to her conversation with Spiderman.

    [02:31] Spidey: r u gonna get killed?!?!!

    [02:31] Spidey: DO YOU NEED HELP????

    [02:32] Spidey: WHAT R U DOING T A FACTORY?1

    Kate huffs out a quiet laugh, surprised that Spiderman bothered to trace her location but not surprised that he had managed to find it easily.

    [02:33] Kate: its cool

    [02:33] Kate: we vibing

    [02:33] Kate: but she stole my drink :(((

    [02:33] Spidey: 0:

    [02:33] Spidey: RUDE

    [02:34] Kate: ikk :((

    When she doesn’t get a response back, she assumes that the boy has fallen asleep and pockets her phone again.

    Surprisingly, the silence in the car sits there comfortably on her chest. A weighted blanket that steadies her heartbeat and allows her to feel the effects of the sleepless night begin to wind around her mind. Without the Red Bull to provide her with the energy that she had known she needed to push her through, Kate’s body melts into the driver’s seat as she rests her cheek onto the hand holding onto the arrow. Her thumb rubs at the tip soothingly.

    Yelena continues to play with the knife in her hands, flipping it over several times, throwing it up a little only to catch it again in a practised motion, and then sheathing it. Only to pull it back out again and repeat the motions.

    Listening to the assassin play with her favored weapon of choice has Kate fighting to keep her eyes open. The satisfying sounds of the blade making ‘whooshing’ sounds as Yelena flips it through the air, and the small ‘clacks’ as she sheaths and unsheaths it are beginning to lull her to sleep, the dangerous lullaby almost enough to have her close her eyes entirely.

    If I had my Red Bull this wouldn’t be happening, Kate thinks bitterly as she glares at the assassin from the corner of her eye.

    Said woman catches her gaze at that moment.

    Fuck-

    Kate looks away quickly, closing her eyes momentarily to rid her mind of Yelena with her knife between her teeth, pink tongue pressing dangerously close to the sharp edge as she fixes her hair. It’s more difficult than it should be. The image of muscles curling at her arms as fingers tightened the black band around golden hair, abs flexing as her back curves in an effort to reach behind her head - it’s all pressed to the back of her eyelids.

    The heat at the pit of her stomach is back, this time with a painful throb between her legs.

    What was that?!

    Kate grits her teeth at her inner monologue. It was nothing .

    That was something!

    She’s perfectly fine. Perfectly straight. Women can be objectively attractive.

    Yelena is objectively attractive.

    Kate brings the tip of her arrow back to her forehead again and dulls out the thoughts with obnoxious lyrics of the song she had been listening to on the way over to the stakeout.

    Of course, it’s at that moment that something begins to happen.

    Kate straightens in her seat, the shot of adrenaline her body provides waking her up immediately. Beside her, Yelena has soundlessly sheaths the knife again and swings her feet off of the dashboard so noiselessly that Kate almost thinks that she’s lost her hearing.

    Still, as Kate’s hand reaches for her compound bow, she hisses, “Be quiet” to the very still assassin beside her.

    Yelena’s head snaps toward her and Kate can see her eyes widen with incredulity. “I am sitting very quietly,” she hisses back, accent thick with annoyance.

    “ Shh .” Kate mindlessly swings her hand at where she thinks Yelena’s mouth is in a half-

    assed effort to silence her. Her blind hand manages to catch her face in a swiping motion, dragging down in a way that has the assassin growling curses in Russian as she swats the offending hand away.

    A victorious smile plays on Kate’s lips.

    Karma.

    Fortunately, Yelena swallows her pride and Kate takes it as a victory.

    They sit still in the car, apprehension tightening the air between them as they observe the movement from the factory.

    A familiar figure emerges from behind a stack of pallets. Kate’s eyes snap toward it, hand tightening around her bow as she follows her target’s hasty walk. He carries a backpack in his hand that looks full and heavy if the slight slump of his shoulders is anything to go by. It has Kate frowning in confusion.

    This is supposed to be another adultery case…

    Her questions are answered as another figure emerges from the door of the factory with a smaller bag in his hand. His stature is tall and bulky, easily surpassing 6’ and looking like he could bench Kate’s body weight without breaking a sweat. If she encountered him on one of her cases, she would turn on her heel and run in the opposite direction. The only thing that gives her hope of winning a fight with the guy if things do get ugly is the condition that he is in. Despite his shape, Kate notes that he’s walking with a hunch and a limp as if he’s been injured. It makes his stature smaller.

    This is definitely not another adultery case.

    Unless the husband is gay and into very specific fetishes involving abandoned factories and beaten up men.

    They watch as the two meet, exchanging few words as they swap bags.

    “Oh shit,” Kate breathes as she realizes what her target is at an abandoned factory for. “So he's not a cheating dirtbag, he's just a sleaze in some shady business.” She passes the arrow into the hand holding her still folded bow. Yelena won’t be happy if she tries opening the bow in the car and manages to catch her in the face with it as it extends. “At least Mrs Young is gonna be happy to hear her husband isn't banging his assistant…”

    “Ah, what are the chances,” Yelena says. Kate doesn’t move her eyes from her target, but she can hear the grin in Yelena’s voice, amusement thick as her accent seeps through into her words. “It looks like our cases got mixed up.”

    Kate’s eyes widen, looking between the tall, unfamiliar man that looks like he’s had the life beaten out of him and Yelena, who is sitting back in her passenger seat with an unreadable grin on her face. “Is that your guy?” She gestures to the bulky man.

    “Yup.” Yelena’s hand wraps around the gun at her thigh, eyes still fixated on her target with a look that has Kate thanking the universe that the Black Widow hasn’t decided to assassinate her. “Made me chase him around the city. He thinks he lost me,” she says, pulling out the gun and making sure that the safety is off. “Idiot.”

    “What’s he doing here?” Kate asks, hoping to gather some intel on her own target.

    “Making a deal.” Swiftly, Yelena pushes the car door open and begins stepping out. “Excuse me, Kate Bishop, I need to go and finish up business.”

    “Wait-”

    Yelena ducks back into the opening of the door, the same way she had when she had greeted her earlier, this time without the grin. “I'll see you around, little hawk.”

    “Wait, Yelena who's the guy?” Kate calls as Yelena slams the car door shut. “What's he doing meeting my client's husband?”

    The sound has alerted the two men that they have company, Mr Young sharply turning to look in their direction with fear in his eyes.

    Me too, buddy, Kate grimaces, me too.

    Yelena ignores him, chasing after her own target with steadfast determination as he bolts back into the factory. Kate sees, rather than hears, the bullets firing in his direction. They visibly lodge into the metal door, but she thinks she sees the man’s shadow tumbling to the floor just as the door swings shut behind him.

    A chill runs through Kate’s body. Steely realization comes crashing over her. All she can do is watch as Yelena tears the door open and positions herself to shoot him again.

    The door closes before Kate can see anything else.

    The abrupt movement has her jolting back to her senses, remembering why she’s here in the first place. When her eyes dart to where Mr Young had been standing, all she finds is a backpack.

    “Well shit .” Kate shoves out of her car. Despite the summer heat clinging to the air, her skin raises with goosebumps. She collects the forgotten backpack, figuring that whatever is inside will tell her enough of what Mr Young was doing here meeting with one of Yelena’s targets. Enough to satisfy her client and get her paid.

    Still, curiosity courses through her. Kate’s eyes stray to the metal door that had concealed the Black Widow and her target. The bullets lodged into it. Pictures the same bullets lodged into the back of the tall man.

    As hard as she tries, she can’t shake the sick feeling in her stomach.

    “Thanks for scaring off my guy…” Kate huffs, swinging the bag over her shoulder before heading back to her car.

    twenty-seven Chapter Notes See the end of the chapter for notes The drive to the farm is everything that Kate needs.

    It’s straight out of her childhood daydreams and she spends the few moments of silence convincing herself that it’s real. That she’s really sitting in Hawkeye’s passenger seat, her beautiful dog in the back, windows rolled down and her music blasting through the crappy speakers like they’re in a coming of age movie.

    Clint picks her up outside of her apartment early in the morning. So early that Kate hasn’t even managed to close her eyes long enough to sleep in her excitement by the time her phone blares the familiar Christmas jingle that reminds her of her mentor. She can’t bring herself to care about her messed-up sleep schedule when she’s rushing around her apartment to make sure Clint isn’t waiting too long for her. She thinks that she looks a little crazy. It’s like there’s a fire with the frantic way she’s collecting her bags and ensuring that the leash is properly latched onto Lucky’s collar - because the last time she had impulsively decided to take him on a walk at four in the morning he had escaped her grasp and disappeared into the city.

    Evidently exhausted, Clint doesn’t try to fight her when she takes control of the USB cable and plugs in her phone.

    “It’s too early for music,” he grumbles as he pulls out onto the road.

    “It’s never too early for music.” Kate grins wildly, and she can see the irritation in Clint’s tense shoulders. It’s harmless, though, because when a soft melody begins to crackle through the speakers and fill the silence of the car, his frown lines smooth a little.

    At seven, they stop for breakfast at a stop station. They fuel up the car, and then their stomachs with endless cups of coffee and a large stack of pancakes that leaves Lucky with leftovers to devour.

    Traumatized from her last encounter with Yelena, Kate makes sure to stop by the vending machine and buys four cans of Red Bull. Clint tells her it’s excessive and that if she drinks that much caffeine she’s going to have a heart attack.

    “I’m not driving you to the hospital when that happens,” he says.

    Kate fixes him with a haunted look, to which he rolls his eyes and lets her be.

    Images of Yelena’s figure standing over that man’s body have been plaguing her subconsciousness. The darkness of her bedroom ceiling does nothing to prevent the onslaught of stop frames of the man falling to the floor. That brief silhouette of Yelena's sturdy frame looming over her target. If anything, the darkness encourages it. It’s half of the reason why Kate can’t bring herself to sleep when the hours of the night slip past midnight. The other half is the pressing feeling of being chased - the constant adrenaline that courses through her body when she looks at the notifications on her phone and sees the incessant emails from her mother’s legal team.

    With the sweet taste of Red Bull on her tongue, the Black Widow begins to crawl into her mind and with her, she brings a commotion of emotions that Kate doesn’t know what to do with.

    So she reaches for the volume of the speakers and turns it all the way up.

    The bruises under her eyes must give her away because Clint doesn’t turn the sound back down like he usually does. He only reaches for his hearing aid and Kate assumes that he does something with it so that the music isn’t so loud.

    Kate grins at him, and he grins back.

    Her carefully curated playlist begins to transition into songs that Kate knows Clint loves. She’s proud of herself for timing the songs perfectly with their caffeine break. She’s bursting with energy and she can tell that Clint is actually starting to enjoy himself now that he doesn’t look like he’s clinging onto the steering wheel as he tries to stay awake.

    There’s a lot of Metallica and Guns N’ Roses , and Kate doesn’t know the lyrics very well but she knows the melodies because her dad used to blast their songs in the car when they drove to their summer house when she was a kid. So she sings along to the words that she does know, and she hums the melody to the parts that she doesn’t. The encouraging glances that she throws at Clint don’t go amiss, but the man pretends to not see.

    Until the familiar low synth begins to build in the speakers.

    Then Clint’s face splits into a wide grin.

    When the deep bass begins its riff, Kate feels a surge of energy fly through her body, and she straightens herself in her seat so she can angle herself at her mentor. She grins at Clint, feeling his excitement as his fingers tap the steering wheel like it's his drum set.

    Clint touches his hearing aid at his ear and Kate’s smile only widens.

    “ Tommy used to work on the docks ,” Clint all but sings, in a surprisingly decent voice but the expression on his face is dorky and Kate knows that he’s not taking himself seriously. “ Union’s been on strike, he’s down on his luck, it’s tough… ” He dramatically flings his head around to look at her, pretending to flick hair out of his eyes and trying to hide his grin as he sings, “ so tough~ .”

    Kate laughs. She laughs from her stomach and she has to put her hand on the dashboard to stop herself from doubling over because she really wants to watch the performance that Hawkeye is putting on for her.

    She can’t help but join in when it gets to the pre-chorus, because she knows the words by heart. Clint’s eyes are shining as they sing together, and Kate doesn’t think she has ever seen him look so carefree and happy. It makes laughter bubble up in her stomach and pushes through in the form of giggles as they both headbang in time to the drums as the song crashes into the chorus.

    “Woah! We’re halfway there!”

    Almost effortlessly, Kate hits the higher “woah-oh, livin’ on a prayer” that follows after and shakes her head violently as Clint sings the next line for her.

    They’re both giddy and bending over with hysterics. Kate’s eyes are watering from the raucous laughter and from the strain of her throat as their singing devolves into simply screaming the words as loud as they can. Lucky is barking in the back seat like he's trying to join in too, and it only makes them laugh harder. There’s dark hair everywhere as she shakes her head to the music. The wind rushes in through the windows and Kate tucks whatever she can behind her ears in a poor attempt to tame it, but she can’t find it in herself to care enough. The high of the music and the carefree atmosphere is too much, and Clint has to swerve them back into their lane during the guitar solo when the car strays to the side.

    Clint pointedly glances at her just as the drums and bass build to the last chorus.

    And Kate hits every note even as the key changes.

    Clint is there every step of the way, doing his best to hit the higher register but his voice cracks, and Kate’s inner child sobs when he doesn’t look embarrassed in the slightest and keeps singing with his eyes almost shut in an effort to keep the laughter at bay.

    It’s amazing and it’s beautiful and it’s everything that Kate could have ever wanted from life.

    The happy tear rolling down her cheek feels bittersweet, and Kate wipes it with a raspy chuckle and a hand on her stomach because her abs are hurting. The song changes into another classic 80’s rock song that Clint quickly adjusts to. They continue their private concert like this is something that they do all the time. Something that they’ll probably do all the time from this day forward.

    It’s only later, after they’ve both sung themselves to the point where their voices are hoarse and crackly, and the playlist transitions into Kate’s more modern picks, that Kate really revels in the achingly warm feeling in her chest.

    The burning bloom threatens to burst from her ribcage. Because everything about this reminds her of her dad. The way he would sing along to the words, and scruff her hair when she would join in. He would melt his voice with hers, harmonizing effortlessly, and Kate would look up at him in amazement.

    Guilt clutches her heart.

    She isn’t trying to replace her dad with Clint, but it still makes her feel a little sick that she recreated a fond memory she keeps close to her heart with someone else. But it also makes her feel like she’s being embraced by her dad’s strong arms again, and the singing and the music and the wind feel so familiar that she lets herself feel safe again.

    Eventually, the sugar crash forces her to pass out in the passenger seat for the duration of the drive.

    Nate wakes her up by jumping into her lap, despite Laura’s voice in the background warning him to “be careful honey, Kate needs the sleep- Nathaniel!”

    Jolting at the weight in her lap and the warm breath on her face as Nate giggles mischievously at his mother’s annoyance, Kate doesn’t hesitate to sweep the little boy up in her arms. She hugs him to her chest tightly. He groans and complains, and tries to bend away from the onslaught of kisses that she peppers across the crown of his head, but his hands are clutching onto the flannel on her shoulders, so Kate rubs her cheek on his face in that way she knows makes him cackle his head off.

    When she feels that Nate actually wants to be let go, Kate releases the small child and lets him slide off of her lap. She takes a moment as she gathers herself, wiping a hand across her drowsy face. After several seconds of emptily staring at the specks of dust in the windshield, she grabs her backpack and exits the car.

    Kate’s inner fangirl has to sit on that and mull over it for the rest of her stay at the Barton’s.

    Because there have been jokes made over the years about the Avengers being like a band.

    Particularly after one of Tony Stark’s interviews when he had made the sarcastic comment. Definitely not as cool as Ronin’s suit and sword. But Kate knows that this guitar is more priceless than any suit or sword.

    Which Kate appreciates, because Clint isn’t big on showing affection.

    Her heart is full and her body feels warm. For a few days, Kate forgets why she couldn’t sleep in the first place.

    Before she knows it, she’s back in New York.

    Her mother’s legal team catches up with her, and she’s back to wearing fancy suits and dresses as she meets with them over lunch.

    One of the up-sides to being back in the city is the big party that the LARPers throw for the Fourth of July. Jack is there, having charmed and befriended all of the members of the group. He’s all flair and swords as he makes a show of entertaining the children of some of the LARPers, which has everyone laughing and cheering at his theatrics.

    He also prepares the most extravagant hotdog for Kate, toppings falling and tipping over the sides when she tries to eat it, but she admits that it’s the most delicious one she’s ever had and he cheers loudly.

    Spiderman says that he’s glad to have her back in the city.

    He shows it by frequently swinging up to her window with pizzas, bagels, and meatball subs that Lucky adores. Kate is grateful for the boy’s presence because she’s not sure that she would be able to remember the day of the week if not for his ranting about the new Star Wars spin-off comics coming out on a Saturday. He has a countdown on his phone that he shows her every time they see each other.

    The weeks fly by and Kate’s PI business is overwhelmed with cases.

    Summer break brings engagements and relationships and adultery.

    Business is booming.

    So it makes sense that Kate is distracted when she next sees Yelena.

    “Who was that?”

    Her breath brushes hotly against Kate’s ear and the blonde almost gets a fist to the eye as Kate instinctively punches at the source of the voice.

    Fortunately for both of them, Yelena has the reflexes of a cat. She weaves and catches her wrist before it can make contact with that pretty face of hers, which sports an unimpressed eyebrow raise.

    “Yelena!” Kate’s eyes are wide, both with surprise and exasperation at the woman’s habit of sneaking up on her. “Jesus!”

    “Yelena Belova, actually,” said woman rasps, voice gravelly and light in that way that Kate hasn’t heard in weeks. The gleeful curve of her lips and arrogant lift of her eyebrow has Kate realizing that she missed the assassin. Which is a whole can of worms that she doesn’t think she has the mind power to open right now.

    “You scared the crap out of me,” Kate hisses, lightly shoving Yelena’s shoulder. The blonde lets her. “Stop sneaking up on me.”

    “You need to work on your senses,” Yelena scolds back, a disapproving frown on her face.

    She begins to walk in the direction that Kate happens to be walking in. The surprise at that doesn’t last long. The voice in Kate’s head reminds her, Yelena knows a lot about you. She probably knows how to get back to your apartment better than you do.

    “I didn't expect you.” Shrugging the duffel bag containing her compound bow and her quiver full of arrows, Kate follows her. She falls into stride with the blonde like they've done this a million times before.

    She supposes that this is what they do now: Yelena shows up and they engage in friendly dialogue, before the assassin randomly decides that she's had enough and jumps off a building.

    Kate swings around a businesswoman who looks like she will push anybody in her way in front of an oncoming car. “I haven't seen you in like, a month.”

    “Twenty-seven days.” Yelena dances elegantly around all of the busy and bustling people of New York. It’s effortless like she’s a native New Yorker, used to the speed and the crowds. It almost distracts Kate enough to not notice how the woman has kept count of how many days it’s been since they last saw each other. But she notices.

    It makes her smirk a little because Yelena missed her too .

    Yelena waves a hand in the air like she’s swatting away her words. “And it does not matter. Anybody can sneak up on you and you would be dead.” Her hand, covered in a beautiful, black henna design and an impossible amount of rings on her fingers, curls around the inside of Kate’s elbow as they’re pushed together by the crowd. “It’s too easy.”

    “I’m gonna make you wear a bell from now on,” Kate says before she can catch herself.

    “Oh?” Yelena spares her a glance. It has Kate’s heart skipping a beat, already anticipating what’s coming. “ Kinky .” Her voice, although filled with amusement, is low and provocative in the way Yelena uses to get a reaction out of her. “I did not know you were into that, Kate Bishop.”

    Rolling her eyes good-naturedly, Kate bumps their shoulders. “You’re insufferable.”

    “Mm,” Yelena purrs and Kate has to stop from tensing up under the blonde woman’s hand.

    “You like it.”

    The skin where Yelena’s fingers rest tickles. The cool press of multiple rings against her warm skin makes goosebumps rise. “Shut up,” she mumbles, making a show of focusing on the crowd around them even though she knows how to dodge without even looking.

    Yelena laughs that delighted laugh she does when Kate looks like she’s seconds away from kicking her out of annoyance. It has a small smile of amusement pulling at Kate’s lips despite her reluctance.

    When they finally turn a corner and slip away from the hoards of people on the main street, Yelena releases her arm. She shoves her hand into the pocket of her thin oversized cardigan that looks long enough to be a cloak instead. Kate doesn’t think about how the flesh on the inside of her elbow suddenly feels too cold. Nor does she think about how good Yelena looks with the thin, floral patterned fabric falling off her bare shoulders to rest around her biceps.

    Crap…

    In the weeks that have passed since their last interaction, Kate has pushed thoughts of Yelena to the back of her mind.

    She rationalizes the rogue inner monologue as best as she can whenever it tries to resurface images of Yelena being ridiculously attractive. That small skip her heart does whenever the blonde initiates contact between them? Yeah, that’s just Kate being touch-starved. The moments of breathlessness when she says something in a particularly suggestive tone? Oh, it caught Kate off-guard - she just wasn’t expecting it. Her physical reactions to Yelena’s general existence?

    She forces her eyes away from the taunting expanse of smooth golden skin.

    Brings herself to stop thinking about the texture of the small scars resting there.

    Well…?

    Kate has a girl crush.

    She thinks that maybe the universe, finally done with watching Kate slowly bring her guard down and lean into the freedom and blissful ignorance, has decided to throw her back into the deep end.

    “So who was that?” Yelena says, voice light and teasing.

    She’s way too pretty.

    Her green eyes glint playfully under the summer sun. “A boyfriend?”

    Of course out of everything, Yelena had caught the way Eli had hugged her 'goodbye.' Kate makes a show of huffing in aggravation but smiles nonetheless.

    “No, just a guy I met the other day while I was working on a case,” she says, waving her hand dismissively. “He helped me out.”

    The ‘guy’ in question had introduced himself as Eli.

    Eli had been surprisingly calm about her almost shooting an arrow at his shoulder, barely missing by an inch when Kate had pulled her bow to the side last minute. She had apologized profusely, dumbly explaining that she was on Hawkeye business before she could stop herself. Fortunately, he barely blinked at her use of the Avenger’s name. That was the first sign that he wasn’t a normal civilian.

    The second was his show of super-soldier strength as he tore apart the metal doors blocking their path into the building where Kate’s client’s harasser had hidden in.

    “Some idiot lost their bank details.” She omits Eli’s superpower strength to Yelena. “Who the fuck prints out their bank details and gives it to the first person they see?”

    “He is attracted to you.” Yelena’s smile is small, but she looks at Kate with a kind of softness that the latter is surprised to find on the assassin’s face. It’s startlingly close to affection - like she’s amused at her antics.

    “What?” Kate feels a surge of disgust push into her mouth. She scrunches her face. “God I hope not, the guy is 60 years old-”

    Yelena softly smacks the back of her head. “Not your client, stupid,” she has a glower on her face that’s too similar to the one she had given her in the elevator when Kate had accidentally revealed her new suit. The affection has gone from her face but not her voice. “The tall man.” She vaguely gestures behind them with a jest of the chin. “The one who just walked away.”

    Kate glares at the blonde, rubbing at her head. Then she processes what she’s trying to say and her mouth is hanging open in surprise. “Oh.” She can feel the realization melting on her face. “Ohh…”

    Yelena’s eyes widen, giving her a sharp nod as if saying ‘do you get it now?’

    And Kate does.

    Except, “I doubt it.”

    Yelena gives her a look that says, ‘you’re an idiot.’

    Kate sighs and shrugs her shoulders. “I mean, if he is, I don't mind.” She grins knowingly at the assassin. “He's definitely easy on the eyes.”

    Humming, Yelena narrows her eyes. Like she’s trying to read her.

    Then, her hand is reaching out to her arm again and is pulling her against the side of her body. Heart jumping to her mouth, Kate yelps as she stumbles into Yelena. She barely dodges the shirtless guy skateboarding on the pavement like a douchebag, but she’s too busy trying not to breathe in the scent of vanilla and orchids and gunpowder, to care about the audacity. The sweet, earthy, and sharp tones blend in strange conjunction that shouldn’t work, but-

    “Is that your type?” The blonde says.

    Owlishly, Kate blinks down at the woman. “What?” She huffs before she’s being tugged back into a quick walk. She clears her throat. “Smart, funny people?”

    Yelena regards her for a second, an unreadable filter over her expression. “Tall. Dark and handsome. Male. Looks like he's hiding things,” she lists quickly. Her voice lilts playfully as a smile spreads across her face. “Male.”

    Kate ignores the emphasis on the last word (because Jesus I can’t think about this right now ) and frowns pensively. “He's not hiding things…” She thinks back to his casual grin when she had stared at him wide-eyed and slack-jawed after he tore the doors apart. Her eyes narrow dubiously. “I hope.”

    “I did not like his vibe,” Yelena says wisely.

    Surprised - and considerably entertained at the use of slang - Kate grins warmly at Yelena. “Are you a master of vibes all of a sudden, or?”

    “Yes.” The Black Widow turns to look at her, expression abruptly blank. “We were trained to pick up on small tells, to understand everything about our target so we know exactly what they are thinking.” A chill slithers down Kate’s spine. “I know that you are about to cheat your diet and eat pizza.”

    Mouth hanging open, Kate searches for an appropriate comeback.

    When she finds none, Yelena chuckles jovially and bumps their shoulders together. Though their shoulders don’t really align because of their height difference. Kate is able to familiarize her inner monologue with the feeling of Yelena’s skin as her bare shoulder comes into contact with her bicep.

    Her inner monologue screams.

    She’s not too sure whether she’s happy that she decided to cuff the sleeves of her shirt because of the heat, or if she’s condemning Past Kate for her mistake.

    Just a girl crush. Girl crush. Girl crush.

    “You did not have to come at me like that,” Kate says, voice strangled. Clearing her throat in an attempt to regain her composure, she glances at Yelena in what she hopes is completely casual. “Speaking of which, are you sticking around long enough to share that pizza with me or are you here for business again?”

    “I’m on business.” Yelena eyes her curiously from her periphery. “But I will stick around long enough to make sure you don't cheat your diet,” she quips with a grin, green eyes soft and bright. “You can't die before I get to kill you.”

    Kate doesn’t know what to do with that. Is she threatening me? Or is that affection?

    The assassin doesn’t let her reach an answer because they’re standing in front of Kate’s apartment and when did we get here?

    They stand together in silence. Kate reaches the conclusion that interacting with Yelena fries her brain because she doesn’t really have a clear line of thought at any point in the conversation. The Black Widow just talks at her and spins her around in circles and leaves her so disoriented that she doesn’t even realize the familiar streets where she lives.

    Yelena rolls her eyes when Kate doesn’t do anything but stare at the front door in confusion.

    One second Kate is trying to figure out when they crossed all of those roads, and the next she’s being grabbed by the hip and being pulled closer to Yelena.

    There are searching hands shoving into the front pockets of her ripped mom jeans as Yelena crowds into her personal space. Their fronts press together. Blonde hair brushes Kate’s cheek and the skin of her chest where her shirt hangs low. Yelena’s cardigan is silk, and it tickles at her arms. There’s vanilla and orchids and gunpowder and Kate stops breathing. The only sound she makes is a squeak when fingers press firmly at her upper thighs - too closely to the sudden throbbing between her legs begging to be quelled.

    A triumphant shout startles Kate out of her panic.

    Yelena finds the keys tucked deep into her left pocket and a packet of bubblegum-mint chewing gum in her right pocket. She brings Kate’s keys to eye level and mutters something in Russian.

    Kate tries convincing herself that she’s doing a good job at hiding the blush begging to spread across her cheeks. The knowing squint of green eyes lets her know she isn’t as successful as she thinks. In fact, the slow curl of pink lips into a smirk tells Kate that Yelena is very aware of the effect that she has on her.

    Tilting her head in amusement, Yelena steps away from Kate and towards the front door.

    “Come, I will cook something good.”

    Girl crush. Just a girl crush.

    Kate doesn’t recover until they’re halfway up the stairs. After chanting her three-step rationalization process in her head enough times that the words ‘girl crush’ no longer seem like they’re words anymore. She pushes everything beneath all of the other thoughts bustling around in her head and finds that it’s effective, because her head is loud. Especially when she realizes what Yelena is planning.

    “I would be more mad that you literally just took away my pizza night, but I don't think I've eaten something that's been home-cooked in months.”

    It’s an exaggeration. Everything Kate ate at the Barton’s was home-cooked, but that was two weeks ago. All she’s been eating since then is fast food.

    Yelena, ever the health expert, makes a sound of dissatisfaction in the back of her throat. “So unhealthy,” she draws. The woman briefly glances over her shoulder as she reaches the top of the stairs. “Seriously, Kate, diabetes is not fun.”

    Kate rolls her eyes. “I don't think I have ever heard anybody say that diabetes is fun, Yelena,” she snarks back, a little peeved by the blonde’s teasing. “I am very aware it's not something I should strive towards.”

    “You are running right at it with your eyes closed.” Yelena dangles the keys inches from her face when they reach the door.

    “Which is why I'm on a diet.” Kate snatches the keys and gently nudges Yelena out of the way. “ God , my efforts aren't even being noticed.” All of her other keys and keychains jangle and clink together as she unlocks the door. She has to lean back a little to put pressure on the handle because otherwise it doesn’t open and her keys jam. “I'm heartbroken," she grunts at the effort.

    Her keys don’t jam this time, and Kate is grateful because how embarrassing would that be?

    Kate can already hear the pitter-patter of Lucky’s feet as he anticipates their entrance. As soon as the door is shoved open, Yelena brushes past her and strolls in like she owns the apartment. In doing so, she steals all of Lucky’s affection right from under Kate’s nose, hands combing into his fur and even dropping a kiss to the top of his head. She’s speaking to him in Russian and Lucky barks back like he understands.

    Maybe he can. Maybe Kate should follow in Lucky’s footsteps and learn Russian too.

    Kate tries not to be too offended by her dog’s betrayal. She knows that he’s putting on that cute act in hopes that Yelena will have something to feed him. The assassin doesn’t give in to his act as Kate would, but Lucky persists and follows her closely.

    “I make you food,” Yelena calls out, already in the kitchen by the time Kate is closing the door. She seems to be more focused on feeding Kate rather than Lucky. “But only because I'm hungry.”

    A smile forms on Kate’s lips and she shakes her head to herself. The burst of affection flutters her heart in her chest.

    “I thought it was so I didn't cheat on my diet.”

    There’s a sharp ‘clang’ as Yelena pulls a pot out of one of her cupboards. “Sure, that too,” she shouts, already at home in her kitchen.

    Kate basks in the sounds of her kitchen actually being used for something other than a makeshift arrow building station.

    With the little ingredients that Kate has on hand, Yelena manages to whisk up two bowls full of really good ramen.

    Kate sits on the kitchen counter - much to the blonde's chagrin - and watches the assassin dance around her kitchen like she’s been cooking in it for years. Once Yelena gives up in trying to scold Kate out of the kitchen, she begins to dial in her focus on cooking. It’s borderline mechanical, if not for the slight bounce in her step at the faint music playing through Kate’s phone speaker.

    Bon Jovi fills the small pauses of their conversation. Songs that Clint introduced her to have made their way onto her playlist.

    Yelena has never heard most of their songs. She smiles approvingly, though, and Kate grins in relief.

    Yelena is more familiar with her kitchen than Kate is. It should concern Kate that Yelena finds the soy sauce like it’s nothing when Kate has been looking for it for the past three months. But it doesn’t. Instead, it makes her posture finally relax as she and Yelena argue over nothing in particular. Like they’re friends and it’s Yelena’s turn to cook.

    “Are you gonna teach me that move?” Kate asks once their bowls are sat empty in front of them.

    “Which one?” Yelena is lounging in the chair she sat in the first time they saw each other in June. Kate thinks that the assassin has officially claimed it as hers. Not that she's in a rush to claim it back.

    “The one that did that.” The archer points to the two gashes marring the wall behind her.

    Yelena’s sharp gaze snaps to the imperfection, though Kate sees the moment her eyes soften. Soften the same way Kate’s do when she finds herself staring at the gashes. “Ah yes, that one.” Obnoxious as ever, Yelena smacks her hand down on the table to push herself up from her chair. It's dramatic and it has theatrics and it's very Yelena. “It is very simple,” she says, nodding at Kate to get her to stand with her.

    Three knives are procured from somewhere in Yelena’s loose cardigan. They’re sleek, sharp and blood red. Almost like they’re coated in blood.

    Images of Yelena standing over that man burst in Kate’s head.

    A shiver shakes her body.

    Yelena silently glides across the room as she brushes her hair out of her face with the edge of one of the knives, sliding into stance robotically. She holds two knives in her right hand, and one in her left. “I will demonstrate, then you copy.”

    Kate nods obediently, eyes wide and alert.

    Clint’s lessons play through in her head as she watches the assassin with keen attention.

    Yelena exhales steadily.

    With a sharp flick of her right wrist, one of the knives in her right-hand jumps in the air above her head. It spins in a controlled, red blur, glinting in the warm yellow sunlight streaming through the windows. The last time Yelena showed her this move, she had only used two knives. This is a new addition.

    As the knife reaches its zenith, the Widow swings her arms in a familiar motion. It’s the move that Kate’s more familiar with. The other two knives in either hand fly across the room. The difference in the move shows itself when her right arm follows through the upward swinging motion. Yelena brings her right hand up in time to catch the third knife she had thrown above her head with dextrous fingers.

    The first two lodge into the calendar Kate has hanging beside her industrial shelves, making a loud ‘thunk’ that has Kate flinching. As soon as they hit their mark, Yelena sends the third knife now in her hand flying after them with a downward swing of the arm.

    It lands right between the first two knives.

    “Holy shit.”

    All of the moisture in Kate’s mouth has dried out. Her heart is beating so fast in her chest that Kate can feel her shirt shaking from the tremors.

    Releasing a controlled breath, Yelena shakes her shoulders and cricks her neck. Her silk cardigan slips from her shoulders at the motion, sliding down to rest around her elbow. The floral pattern on the delicate fabric and beautiful henna on Yelena’s hand hilariously juxtaposes the danger in her posture.

    Kate’s not sure if she finds it funny, though.

    Once again, she is reminded just how out-matched she was all of those times she and Yelena had fought last year.

    I could die at literally any second.

    The blonde turns to face her, an easy curl of her lips as she smiles. “Now you try,” she says like it's the easiest thing in the world. Pulling out three more blades from her cardigan, Yelena walks back toward her with her hand outstretched. Kate inhales sharply through her nose, hand stuttering in the air as she debates on taking them. Once she does, Yelena nods. “Get in your stance.”

    Kate tries to replicate the assassin's stance. “Like this?” She asks, looking over her shoulder at the blonde for approval.

    She wears an amused smile that quickly shifts into a stern frown once she realizes that she has to show Kate how to do it. “No.” Yelena shifts closer and puts her hands on her hips. It begins to dawn on Kate that Yelena is a very tactile person. She's liberal with her touches and very casual about the gestures. With a simple tug of her hips, she fixes Kate’s position, and she doesn't act like it means anything more than her teaching her how to get into stance.

    Meanwhile, Kate exhales shakily and tries to forget the burning sensation. “Like this.”

    “Do I just…” Uncomfortable with the placement of her fingers around the hilts of the knives, Kate fumbles with them in an attempt to familiarize herself with their weight. “I just throw the first two at the same time?”

    “Yes, but first you throw this one in this hand up,” Yelena says, touching the back of Kate’s right hand where she’s holding the knives. Their eyes meet, and Yelena settles her with a deadpan expression. “Make sure it is not too low or you will drop it and stab your foot.”

    Kate nervously glances at the sharp blade in her hand. “And then I just... grab it?”

    “And throw it.” Yelena steps back, an expectant lift of the eyebrows encouraging Kate. “Go.”

    And Kate almost does. She almost releases one of the blades in her right hand, breath caught in her chest. But a voice in the back of her head that sounds suspiciously like Clint stops her and then she’s turning to Yelena and saying, “Wait-”

    Yelena groans, throwing her hands up in the air in annoyance.

    “What if I cut my fingers?”

    “Your loss.”

    “What?” Kate’s brow pulls together. “There isn't an easier way to do this?”

    Quietly regarding Kate, Yelena folds her arms across her chest. Her green eyes are narrowed slightly, but the sunlight bounces off of them and lights them up in a beautiful inferno that steals the breath right from Kate’s lips. Then she’s rolling them like she’s having a private conversation in her head that Kate doesn’t hear.

    “You do not have to throw it in the air,” she says with a plain tone. “But it is less cool.”

    Yelena’s eyes snap towards her fingers.

    Kate thinks that if she squints, she can spy a faint dust of pink on her cheeks.

    “You are less cool,” the assassin quips, averting her gaze. She unfolds her arms as she extends a hand in Kate’s direction, curling her fingers in a ‘give me’ motion.

    “I think I can live with that.” Kate places one of the blades in Yelena’s palm, ignoring the slight skip her heart makes at the contact her fingers make with the soft skin there. She shakes it off by hopping up and down like she’s about to sprint headfirst into the wall.

    It’s her first time throwing two knives at the same time, but she figures it can’t be too different from throwing one.

    Just copy what Yelena did…

    Turns out, it’s not nearly as easy as the Black Widow makes it look. Kate releases the knife in her left hand too late and sends it flying upward in a dangerous arc that has the blade clattering to the floor next to the couch. The one in her right hand is released too early, and her fingers slip around the hilt.

    Which means it almost swipes Yelena in the face.

    Yelena screams in surprise, throwing herself out of the knife’s trajectory. It’s short, but it’s loud and shockingly girlish and Kate’s eyes are widening before she can help it. The blonde is shouting words in Russian, her tongue curling around the language harshly and Kate is glad that she can’t understand her because she’s sure she’s reciting all of the curses that she knows, and is aiming them at her with a livid scrunch of the face.

    Kate laughs. She can’t help it - she never expected Yelena to ever scream like that.

    “Kate Bishop!” The assassin’s face is most definitely pink now, and Kate can’t stop smiling even as she stifles her laughter. “You almost cut my eyes out!”

    “I'm sorry but I have literally never done this move before!”

    Scowling so hard that lines form around her bared teeth, Yelena punches her shoulder. Kate chuckles, rubbing at the ache in her arm that she knows will be a bruise tomorrow. Yelena swiftly collects the knife that almost blinded her from the floor. “My poor baby…”

    “Are you okay?” Kate asks, humor seeping into her voice.

    “I am fine,” Yelena spits, marching over to the other knife that’s being sniffed at by a curious Lucky. She gently bats his snout away, scolding him in Russian. “You are never touching my children ever again.”

    Kate grins, eyebrows raising in amusement. “Your children?”

    “Yes, these are my babies.” Yelena is frowning, collecting all of her ‘children’ and slipping them back into their place, stealthily concealed by the floral cardigan around her frame. “A lot of memories.”

    “By memories, do you mean people you have killed with them?”

    Yelena pauses.

    Her back is turned to Kate, but her posture is evidently rigid. Her left hand is stuck midair where it’s holding her cardigan open for her right hand to slip into her side, completely still like she’s frozen in place. Kate wonders when it had become so easy to read the Black Widow’s body language. Wonders how she herself is able to eat and joke around with somebody who is capable of taking somebody’s life without blinking. Without stopping for a second to consider if the person at the other end of her blood-red blades has a family.

    “They were bad people,” Yelena says. Her voice is devoid of emotion.

    The images that have been haunting Kate’s dreams for the past twenty-seven days play in her mind again, like Yelena has pressed the ‘play’ button on the footage. Kate swallows. “I guess you are still an assassin…”

    “You have not killed?” Yelena turns. Her guard is back up, and Kate can no longer read her. She doesn’t know what the cloudy look in her darkened eyes means.

    Kate thinks back to the car chases at Christmas. Thinks back to all of the arrows she carefully aimed at knees and shoulders - away from any major arteries and organs. The all-consuming guilt gnaws at her consciousness at night when she can’t sleep and makes her wonder if any of her adversaries have fallen at her hands. Kate remembers all of the nights she stayed up combing through CCTV footage of her fights. Trying to see if they had gotten back up again or if they had been dragged to the nearest hospital by an ally.

    So far, she has yet to find footage of a cold, dead body.

    “No. I try to avoid it.”

    The confidence in her voice when she says it must unnerve Yelena, because the blonde has a strange look in her eyes as she stares. It makes Kate feel like she shouldn’t trust her own judgement.

    “Ah,” is all the woman says.

    “Yeah.”

    A heavy silence falls over them. The type that makes Kate skittish and has her shifting her weight from one foot to the other.

    Yelena sighs. Kate feels the fatigue in the single breath, and even though her entire being is terrified of the Black Widow standing in her apartment with enough knives to kill a dozen people, her heart aches for her.

    “This was nice.” The woman begins to move toward the window. “I enjoyed girls’ night,” she says with a knowing smile that doesn’t reach her eyes.

    Uncertainty courses through Kate’s body as she watches Yelena go through the practised choreography of latching her grapple to the floor. The bitterness in her mouth is unpleasant. Probably because she’s holding back the words that would beg Yelena to stay for the rest of the night so she could show her what an actual ‘girls’ night’ looks like. To forget that Kate even brought up her profession in the first place like it’s something that scares her. That disgusts her.

    “I still don't think this counts as girls night…”

    Instead, she makes a cheap joke.

    It makes Yelena smile a little, and the warm summer sunlight that bathes her in golds and greens is entrancing. Kate thinks that she’ll keep the image stored in her head for when she can’t sleep.

    Lucky whimpers quietly at the bottom of the stairs, looking up at Yelena with his best ‘puppy-dog’ eyes.

    “Goodbye moya lyubov ,” the woman whispers as she cups the golden retriever’s face. She presses a firm kiss between his eyes, and Lucky returns the gesture by licking her chin. “I will miss you."

    Kate is still confused by the strange dichotomy of this Yelena - the one with floral-patterned silk slipping off her shoulders and beautiful henna on her delicate hands - and the Yelena that smells like leather and gunpowder and blood.

    Still, she’s completely genuine in the double meaning of her words as she says, “he’ll miss you too.” Kate meets those sharp green eyes and smiles, soft and real. “Don’t be a stranger.”

    “I am not a stranger, Kate Bishop,” Yelena says, voice light and playful like always. “We are acquaintances.”

    Kate is grinning. “It’s a saying.”

    Yelena grins back, just as wide. “It is a confusing saying.”

    “Yeah?” Kate tilts her head as she walks to where Lucky is staring up at the blonde with glistening eyes. “What's the rating?”

    “One out of five,” Yelena says, hoisting herself up onto the edge.

    “You gonna leave a review?”

    The Black Widow spins in place. Kate doesn’t flinch when their eyes meet, even when her heart picks up its pace in her chest. She’s unabashed in her staring as a warm evening breeze billows Yelena’s silk cardigan like a cloak and curls around her loose hair. Sunlight paints her in whites and golds and greens and black and Kate already knows that this will be what she sees when she stares at her bedroom ceiling in the hours past midnight.

    Yelena gives her one last shrug, full of mischief and sass that Kate is becoming dangerously fond of. “Does it need one?”

    Dramatic as ever, the blonde tumbles backwards from her window.

    Kate huffs, smiling to herself.

    “Have a good night Yelena.”

    Chapter End Notes Kate doesn't know why Yelena has henna on her hand, but I like to think that some of the Widows sat a bunch of the other Widows down to draw designs on their hands as a way to share and reconnect with the cultures they were taken from as children.
