    miami - one Chapter Notes See the end of the chapter for notes There is a routine in place on the rare occasion that Kate receives a case that takes her out of the city.

    She’ll give Clint a call to let him know that if she doesn’t make it to their weekly calls that she’s not dead or kidnapped or in trouble (hopefully). Then she’ll drop by Grill’s apartment, get roped into a small LARPing session that involves drinks and swords and a broken vase.

    He’ll agree to look after Lucky for however long she’ll be gone for in exchange for a reward, which she’ll give without a second thought, and then leave again to go to do as much research as possible for the case.

    So far, the farthest a case has taken her has been Washington D.C, which is still closer than Clint’s farm. So when she reads that her client wants her to track the location of stolen private data to later retrieve it, and sees that the GPS tracker takes her down the East Coast to Miami, she feels the ground beneath her feet giving way.

    The large, red, blinking circle stalls at the suburbs of the city, winking at her as if to lure her there.

    Well, fuck me.

    Kate’s client says that he will take care of travel costs so long as Kate retrieves the data. He gives her a three-week deadline.

    John Earl, her client and a shareholder of many major techs and innovation businesses, seeks her out on a recommendation from one of his friends.

    It’s a funny story, really: Kate had retrieved his friend’s sex tape and stopped it from being uploaded to the web by hacking and deleting the footage from the internet. Something that took her four days of staring at her computer screen, tears streaming out of her straining eyes from having to generate line after line of code to stop the leak. It had been one of the more boring cases that Kate honestly forgot about. Though apparently the client hadn’t and out of gratitude he must have thought it apt to direct one powerful John Earl to her services.

    Mr Earl offers her a large reward for the case that has her eyes falling out of her skull.

    Who the hell offers $1,500 for a piece of stolen data?

    She calls Clint immediately but is left hanging, so she forgoes waiting for her partner’s advice and jumps at the opportunity. Not only for the money but the prestige that this will bring to her business.

    John Earl is a name that has been thrown around in passing throughout her years working at Bishop Security, but she never cared enough to listen. She has a feeling, however, that if her mother knows Mr Earl then a background check is the most reasonable thing that she can do before she fully dives into the case. It has her stomach twisting and turning as she sweeps his social media accounts, the businesses he is a shareholder of, who his friends are, his family - even goes as far as checking his latest bank transactions. When she comes up blank - just knowledge that Mr Earl is keen on horseracing, has a daughter named Petunia, and once took a picture with Beyonce - she decides to throw herself into her work.

    All Mr Earl lets her know about the data she will be retrieving is that it’s very important information for one of the businesses to which he is a large shareholder and that the people that took it will most likely be very reluctant to have it taken out of their hands.

    Kate tries to press for more, but when Earl pulls out NDA papers with his signature scribbled all over them, she decides to press her lips together and agree to retrieve his precious data.

    He’s grateful for it. Shows her by paying for her very expensive dinner at the five-star restaurant they’re dining at and is a gentleman about her getting home safe. Even insists she accepts the drive from his driver. She denies politely and he nods without pushing further.

    Kate makes sure to use the subway to get home, looking over her shoulder constantly and ensuring that nobody is following her because his insistence on calling her ‘Miss Bishop’ had thrown her off the entire night and there is something in her gut telling her not to trust the man.

    Nonetheless, she takes the case.

    When Clint finds out about it, he is surprisingly concerned about it.

    He contacts old friends and people that owe him favors from the past, trying to dig up more information on John Earl and on what the private data that Kate has been tasked to retrieve could be. The only thing that comes back that is remotely useful is that Mr Earl - like any powerful businessman and investor - has enemies. Those enemies will be protecting the data while they attempt to decrypt it and attempting to get it back will be difficult.

    It’s why her deadline is three weeks. Whatever security that data has protecting it must have been carefully curated, as if they had anticipated that it would be taken from them.

    (Kate will have to analyze it and see how Bishop Security can copy the code to improve its security defences.)

    Clint, of course, is reluctant on sending Kate to Miami on her own. He offers to go with her, just in case things get messy, but Kate refuses. Tells him that this is her chance to prove whether she is cut out for the ‘hero business’ that Clint is so reluctant about letting her get involved in.

    Instead of storming her apartment, Clint sends her the flight details of a plane ticket from New York City to San Francisco.

    Which is how Kate ends up inside a building that looks like it has seen better days.

    “Hello?” Her voice echoes in the large, empty space.

    It has the vibes of a warehouse, except there are no shelving units or equipment. Harsh, bright sunlight cascades through the skylights, which are a mixture of dirty glass and opaque plastic covers to prevent the rain from falling through. The air is musty and thick from the heat, the lack of air flow almost suffocating. There are dust particles dancing in the light as Kate carefully steps further into the building, and it almost looks aesthetic.

    Oh sure, now is the perfect time to be thinking about the aesthetics of a creepy abandoned building. Kate scoffs at herself under her breath, rolling her shoulders to ease some of the tension. Good going Hawkeye.

    “Hi?” She feels her voice waver, hand gripping her retractable sword. “Uh... it’s Kate? Kate Bishop.”

    Swirling around to check her surroundings, Kate feels pressure begin to form in her gut. The skin at her forearms prickles. Her grip on the handle of the sword tightens, hidden by the long sleeve of her flannel that reaches her fingertips as to hide her advantage of having the weapon.

    “Hawkeye sent me here? I'm looking for…” She looks at her hand where she had scribbled the name Clint had said to her on the phone. His voice had been crackly because of the shitty connection, but she didn’t want to take him away from the water fight he and the kids were having so she just lied and said she had heard him. I am a goddamn idiot, because the ink has been blurred by the sweat of her nervousness and the heat of the hot summer day. “Crap-”

    Squinting, she tries to make out the best of the horrible scrawl she had written too quickly to be legible. “Scatt… Pong?”

    “Scott Lang?”

    Kate feels her muscles jump beneath her skin. “Shit-” Her thumb flicks over the switch of the retractable sword and she spins around to hold it in front of her body protectively.

    Instead of being greeted by a weapon in her face and a fight that she is not prepared for, she comes face to face with a girl who looks like she’s trying her best to look intimidating. Dirty blonde hair is pulled out of her face in a tight ponytail, which lets the attention be drawn to the way her eyebrows furrow over her blue eyes. Her arms are crossed over her chest as she stands tall in place. She looks like she’s sizing the intruder up, eyes darting to the sword steadily held across Kate’s body.

    If Kate weren’t Kate Bishop, then perhaps her glare would be intimidating.

    Unfortunately for this girl, she is Kate Bishop. And Kate Bishop has had to handle the domineering gaze of a very deadly ( and attractive ) Black Widow more than once. Kate’s brain starts to pair and contrast the differences in their glares: the way this girl’s eyes look softer and lack the sheen of iciness that Yelena’s green eyes possess; her cheeks are a little less angular and more round, unlike Yelena’s sharp jawline and defined features; the slight uncertainty in the way that she holds herself, nothing alike the confident stance that Yelena uses.

    This girl is a softie compared to Yelena.

    To Kate’s subconscious the only rational response, it seems, is to lower the sword and amp up her charm. “Uh, hi.” Kate smiles brightly, still keeping a tight grip on the sword because Clint’s voice is in the back of her head telling her to always expect the unexpected.

    The girl raises an eyebrow. “You good?”

    Kate keeps smiling, but her muscles twitch under the strain. “Very.”

    “You’re lucky my dad didn't hear you butcher his name,” the girl says, amusement seeping into her voice as she plays with the edges of her green flannel. Hey, we’re matching! Kate shushes her inner monologue, even though she can’t help but be mildly mirthful at the similarities in their outfits. The girl’s lips twist into a careful but teasing smile. “He would have cried.”

    “The guy I’m looking for…” Kate blinks as her brain catches up to the conversation. “It’s your dad?”

    Tilting her head to the side, the girl does another once-over of Kate’s appearance - taking in the long sword in her right hand slightly hidden by the oversized purple flannel, the thigh sheath wrapped around the outside of her black jeans, and the metal-tipped boots at her feet. She meets her eyes again, this time with a little more confidence as she stretches a hand out for Kate to take.

    “Cassie.” The girl smiles amicably. “ Lang .”

    Kate’s mouth drops into an ‘ah’ before she’s reaching out to clasp Cassie’s hand. “I’m Kate.” She quickly scrambles to add, “Bishop.”

    “You said,” Cassie chuckles, turning on her heel and beginning to walk away.

    Taking this as an invitation to follow, Kate quickly puts the sword away into its sheath at her thigh and follows after the girl. Kate uncomfortably tugs at the collar of her thin flannel, pulling it away from her sweaty neck as the heat wraps around them the further they go into the large greenhouse. She sees that Cassie is leading her to a corridor that had been tucked away from the summer heat falling through the skylight.

    “Uh, where are we going?” Kate asks, glancing around her to check for exits and entrances.

    “To the lab,” she says like Kate knows what she’s talking about. Cassie walks with purpose, a slight spring in her step that has Kate relaxing a little. It reminds her of Spiderman, the slightly excited bounce in every other step. “You said Hawkeye sent you?”

    “Yeah, I’m here to collect some parts for my trick arrows.”

    Cassie glances over her shoulder, steps stuttering a little as she does so. “ Your trick arrows?”

    “Well,” Kate grimaces, waving a hand in the air as she searches for an explanation. “ Our trick arrows, but it's not like he has time to use them in his retirement home.”

    Eyebrows furrowing, Cassie leads them down some metal stairs, her own boots clomping hard in a lack of effort to muffle her steps. “Okay, first,” she glances at Kate again, expression twisting in a way that is both wonderfully amused and confused. It has a delighted grin forming on Kate’s face. “Hawkeye is at a retirement home?”

    Kate shrugs noncommittally. “Meh.”

    Close enough to a retirement home.

    “Second,” Cassie pauses halfway down the stairs, hand on the railing as she turns to look back up at Kate. Her expression is swirling with emotions that Kate doesn’t have the expertise to read, though if she had to land on one she would confidently call it amazement. “Do you... are you...”

    “The next Hawkeye?” Kate puffs her chest out before she can stop herself, a proud grin on her face. “You bet your ass I am.”

    The wide-eyed reaction that she elicits from Cassie has Kate revelling in satisfaction. This is the reaction that feeds at her ego, and Kate can’t help but take it all in.

    Not at all like the way Yelena had reacted when Kate had brought it up.

    Kate is still bristling a little at the lack of impact that it had had on the assassin. The lack of surprise or excitement, only a casual mention, like it had been something that she had foreseen.

    Well, Yelena does seem like the type of person to be a hundred steps before anybody else.

    She supposes that she can’t complain too much about Yelena’s reaction, though. The woman had gone out of her way to stop by her apartment and, in her own roundabout assassin way, had given her her congratulations. Kate feels her heartbeat pick up in her chest at the memory. The little arrow earring has been stored safely at her bedside table, along with other objects that hold sentimental value.

    “For real?” Cassie grins, mouth hanging open in shock.

    “Hawkeye approved and everything.” Kate continues to follow after the younger girl, enjoying the way the cool air of the underground level wraps around her hot skin. Although she is slightly concerned about this random girl leading her to her certain doom, she can’t help but feel comfortable in her presence. “It’s why I'm here. Clint said that I needed to start getting used to collecting parts for trick arrows.”

    Kate makes a sound of realization, which has Cassie turning around at the bottom of the stairs with a hand on a door handle. Pulling out the small rectangular box from the back pocket of her jeans, Kate extends it to the younger girl. “He also said to give this to your dad.”

    Carefully, Cassie takes the wrapped box from Kate’s hand and shakes it slightly. “What is it?”

    “I don't know.”

    They stand there for a couple of moments. They both intently stare at the box in Cassie’s hand as if waiting for it to do something. When it doesn’t lift in the palm of her hand and begin to dance, they lift their gazes.

    Their eyes meet, and Kate can’t help but laugh at the mirth and confusion in the girl’s eyes. Cassie joins her, reaching up with her hand as if to cover her smile. “Hearing what my dad has said about Hawkeye, I'm hoping it's a prank.”

    Kate’s eyebrows raise. “I didn't know the two of them were close?”

    A small, fond grin spreads across Cassie’s lips as she shakes her head in a way that tells Kate she’s used to her father’s antics. “Dad likes to think they are.”

    “Well, it does take Clint a while to warm up to someone,” Kate says, matching Cassie’s grin in her own fond smile. Her heart clenches at the thought of her partner and his over-

    protectiveness of her first mission so far from home. “He shows his love in his own way, I guess.”

    Cassie must see something in her expression because suddenly she’s saying, “are you related to him?”

    Kate’s eyes widen. “ Me? ” For some reason, her heart begins to skip and run in her chest. There’s a tight twisting in the back of her head that has been showing up ever since Nate’s birthday. “Nah, I just.” She blows out a long breath, puffing her cheeks and looking up at the ceiling. “Accidentally stole a suit from a black market auction, got involved with the Tracksuit Mafia - great guys, by the way - and destroyed New York's most loved Christmas tree-”

    “That was you? ”

    Laughing nervously, Kate waves a hand in the air. “What? No, of course not...”

    “And that somehow…” Cassie frowns pensively, pushing the door open for Kate to walk through. “Made you Hawkeye's apprentice?”

    Taking a moment to admire her surroundings, Kate feels her jaw slacken.

    There are machines scattered around the large, high-tech room, which is well lit by white and blue lights. Black metal scaffolding holds up what is the basement of the building. There is a large, glass pane dividing what looks like an office slash conference room that looks down at what is most likely the lab that Cassie was talking about. Stray parts of machines are lying around on steel tables, waiting to be picked up and put to use. The room reeks of intelligence and innovation and it’s straight out of a sci-fi movie.

    It’s a stark contrast to the old, deindustrialised, abandoned look upstairs, and it has Kate trembling like a fucking chihuahua.

    “Yeah, more or less,” Kate breathes in answer to the previous question, amazed by the lab in front of her.

    This is a superhero lair! What the hell!

    “Well, I'm still trying to convince my dad to let me follow after him.”

    That catches Kate’s attention. She turns to look at Cassie, who has her shoulders hunched together and her chin tucked into her chest, and she raises her eyebrows a little at the turmoil on her face. It reminds her a little of Lila. The thought has her face softening as she approaches where the younger girl is leaning against one of the tables. “Your dad... he's Ant- Man?”

    “Mm,” Cassie hums, a small smile of pride on her face. “The one and only.”

    “So can you do what he does?”

    Cassie doesn’t reply straight away, her fingers pulling and tugging at the bottom of her flannel. “In a way.”

    Kate comes to stand beside the girl, leaning against the table. “That's really really cool,” she says reassuringly. A genuinely warm smile pulls at the corners of her mouth as she nudges their shoulders a little. “You know, I remember seeing Ant-Man for the first time when I was a kid.”

    The younger girl looks at her from the corner of her eye, though there’s curiosity there.

    “Really?”

    “Yeah, when the Avengers broke up,” Kate says, remembering those days she used to sit in her room obsessively pouring over the Internet for clips and footage of what happened at that airport. Remembers her mother realizing for the first time that Kate could navigate Bishop Security’s software and use it to hack CCTV from other companies. She grounded me so bad…

    She pulls herself back into the moment and laughs to herself. “I saw the news and remember being mind blown by a giant man fighting Iron Man. My little, tiny self was amazed.”

    A nostalgic grin lights up Cassie’s face, blue eyes glinting in a way that has Kate feeling protective over the girl.

    “How old were you then?”

    “Uh…” Cassie tilts her head back to stare at the ceiling. “I think I was eight?”

    “Eight?”

    “No wait,” Cassie says, a hand at her temple as she thinks. “Nine.”

    Kate blinks at the girl who is much younger than she anticipated. Not that Kate had expected anything. The shock of having her suspicions confirmed, however, makes her take a break and think over it.

    “What?” The younger girl asks defensively.

    A teasing grin greets her as Kate scrunches her nose. “You’re making me feel old.”

    Cassie rolls her eyes, crossing her arms over her chest and raising an unimpressed eyebrow. “You can't be that much older than me.”

    “I'm twenty-three,” Kate says, and it still feels weird to say because she never really got used to being in her twenties.

    Especially not after everybody came back from the Blip. It had taken a while to get used to the fact that some of the childhood friends she had grown up with were still in high school while she had already graduated college. Sometimes, those five years where everybody was gone didn’t feel real. Like it was all a dream.

    “Yeah, you're only like... five years older than me.”

    “You’re eighteen?” Kate glances at the blonde, a gentle smile on her face.

    “Turned just a week ago,” Cassie says, grinning proudly.

    “You’re a baby,” Kate coos, exaggerating her voice as she reaches out to ruffle the top of her head the same way that Clint does to her.

    Cassie’s entire body freezes up and her demeanour changes immediately. It has Kate freezing in place as she watches carefully, hand lifting off of the top of her head in precaution. The girl’s voice is quiet as she says, “please don't.”

    Slowly, Kate drops the hand and rests it on Cassie’s shoulder as gently as she can. The girl has her eyes closed, breathing in and out with a determined focus. “You okay?”

    Exhaling a large, shaky breath, Cassie’s eyes reopen and she looks at her apologetically.

    “Yeah uh... sorry,” she grimaces, “It’s just-”

    “When she gets angry she gets big.”

    A new voice interrupts them. Kate twists her body towards it, her gaze gravitating towards the confident woman striding through a door at the other side of the room. She dons a black blazer over a skin-tight tank top, and heels that click when she walks across the room toward where they’re sitting. Her hair is pulled back from her face in a similar way that Cassie’s is, though her features are sharper. More recognaziable too…

    Kate’s eyes are widening in recognition before she can stop herself, having seen the woman on TV only a handful of times but remembering her because she was there. When the Avengers fought against Thanos.

    Beside her, Cassie tenses up again. “Hope-”

    “Your father is gonna be a little late,” Hope says to Cassie, her eyes, however, focusing on the screen of her watch at her wrist.

    Cassie scoffs under her breath, though she says affectionately, “of course he is.”

    Managing to cough herself out of her star-struck amazement, Kate pushes off of the edge of the table and stretches a hand out for the woman to shake the same way that Cassie had earlier. “Hi, I'm Kate Bishop.”

    The woman takes her hand gracefully, though she gives a firm handshake that has Kate’s eyebrows raising to her hairline. “Hi, Hope van Dyne - or Pym, whichever you choose,”

    Hope says, rolling her eyes at herself. “It's nice to meet you, Kate.” She takes her hand back as she strides closer to Cassie. She places the same hand on the girl’s shoulder and squeezes it affectionately. “You're the Hawkeye Clint told us about?”

    “Wh- Pym- uh…”

    Pym as in the trick arrow that makes things bigger and smaller?

    “Yeah.” Kate gulps, hands pulling at her flannel as she tries to fix her appearance. “Yeah, I'm Clint's Hawkeye.”

    Clint’s Hawkeye- what the hell? I am my own Hawkeye, not Clint’s-

    “You’re here for your trick arrows?” Hope says, pulling out a sleek black briefcase from underneath the table where Kate and Cassie had been leaning. Her movements are practised and fluid and it unnerves Kate in a way that is too familiar for her own good.

    “Yeah, uh-” Kate reaches up to her hair where it’s tied up in a ponytail, pointing at nothing in particular. “Clint said I'd be getting parts?”

    Hope glances at her through her lashes, a delicate smile on her face. “I went a little beyond what Clint asked and assembled the arrows for you,” she says. There are two swift clacks as Hope flicks the latches on the briefcase. The lid is pulled open to reveal eight beautiful arrows. “I hope you don't mind?”

    Amazement colors Kate’s expression as she practically floats to where the trick arrows are presented carefully in the briefcase. The tips are coned like the trick arrow that Clint hasn’t let her use yet, always telling her that they are too dangerous. Embedded into them, in glowing blue light, are the letters ‘P.Y.M.’ The metal of the shaft is a gorgeous deep black, slightly darker than the arrows that Kate uses but close enough in hue that she doesn’t think it will be a problem with the branding. Not with the streaks of purple wrapping around the shaft and the fletching shining the same hue of purple.

    Guess this means I graduated from Clint’s class, Kate grins.

    “Oh, yeah no,” Kate breathes, brushing her fingertips across the shaft of one of the arrows. It’s sleek and smooth under her skin. A little more slippery than her usual arrows, but she thinks that she can get used to handling them with practice. “They’re perfect,” she says anyway. “Thank you so much.”

    “It’s not a problem,” Hope says, an easy smile on her face as she watches her admire her work.

    “So what do you do?” Kate’s eyes meet Cassie’s in surprise at the abrupt question. The blue eyes are both curious and fiery with passion.

    “Cassie.” Hope’s voice holds warning.

    “What?” Cassie says, shrugging nonchalantly even though her shoulders are pulled tight with tension. Kate sees the glare that the younger girl sends Hope and all of a sudden feels like she has just walked face-first into family drama. “I'm just asking her a question.”

    Awkwardly clearing her throat, Kate reaches out to Hope as if to hold her back. “It's okay, I don't mind.”

    The woman huffs, exasperation evident in her eyes as she matches Cassie’s tense posture.

    “That's not-”

    “So?” The younger girl prompts quickly, cutting off the woman.

    Kate’s eyes dart between the two. She hesitates as she opens her mouth to speak. The last thing that she wants is to create an argument between Hope and Cassie, whose dynamic is a little too similar to the one she and her mother had.

    Bitterness washes over her mouth as Kate observes the tense, disapproving expression on Hope’s face that barely hides the care behind a concerned frown.

    “I'm a private investigator,” Kate says, looking away from Hope and to Cassie. “I get a lot of adultery cases, a lot of looking into family members and in-laws.” She says it like it’s boring so that maybe Hope will stop glaring holes into the side of her head. But when she sees Cassie’s eyes narrow - sees how they dart to the trick arrows in the briefcase and then back at her like she’s silently saying ‘you’re not telling me something’ - Kate sighs. “But once in a while, I'll get a case that's closer to hero work, which is why I need trick arrows.”

    There’s a satisfied glint in the younger girl’s eyes, mixed with something that Kate thinks is admiration.

    “That's really cool,” Cassie huffs, gaze imploring as she stares at Kate like she put the stars in the sky. Kate feels herself preen. “I wish I could do that.”

    “You could,” Kate says, pointedly looking at her new admirer with an encouraging grin. “You shrink and grow on command, right?”

    “Ye-”

    “Not on command.” Hope’s voice cuts through their conversation, immediately pulling the young girl out of her stupor like she’s dumped a bucket of ice-cold water over her head.

    “Cassie, you know what your dad thinks about this.”

    Cassie frowns so deep that lines form between her eyebrows. “He's my dad, of course, he's not gonna want his daughter running around fighting bad guys.”

    Kate’s heart does a little flip in her chest. She understands what it’s like to dream of becoming a hero and understands what it’s like to have a mother figure pushing that dream down. The situation is a little too similar to her own, Kate thinks, because it makes her chest ache in a way that is painful and it reminds her of the anxiety that keeps crawling into her head whenever she lets thoughts of her mother linger too long.

    “Kate, do you wanna stay for dinner?” Hope says, a finality in her tone that is aimed at Cassie despite her question being for Kate. “The flight must have been really long.”

    “Oh,” Kate says, blinking at the abrupt change in conversation. Her stomach tightens as it grumbles quietly. Kate tries to hide the reddening of her ears behind loose strands of hair and smiles politely. “I would love that.”

    “Maybe you can talk Cassie out of putting her life in danger.”

    With that last comment and a pointed look at the younger girl, Hope begins to stride out of the room with the same amount of confidence she had entered with.

    “Go to hell,” Cassie growls quietly. Her glare is aimed at the doorway Hope has just disappeared through, and Kate can’t help but feel herself soften with sympathy for her new friend.

    “Are you okay?”

    Cassie’s head snaps toward her, anger boiling visibly inside of her as her fists clench on the surface of the table. Her breathing is still steady as she actively tries to calm herself down. “If you try to talk me out of it I'm gonna call you a hypocrite,” she says, voice burning with frustration.

    Kate sighs, laughing slightly but she knows it doesn’t reach her eyes. “I wasn't gonna try, don't worry.”

    The younger girl huffs. Her shoulders slump, the pent up frustration draining out of her body as she closes her eyes and steadies her breathing. “Thanks,” she says, her voice shaking slightly.

    “It's no problem.” Kate closes the briefcase and tries to smile reassuringly at Cassie. She teasingly bumps her shoulder with her fist and raises a challenging eyebrow. “Now, would it be too much to ask for a demonstration of your super awesome powers or do I have to awkwardly stand around the kitchen while you and Hope duke it out?”

    Cassie lets out a startled laugh, her eyes shining brightly with amusement. “Only if you show me your Hawkeye skills.”

    Ant-Man - or Scott, as he insisted to be called - is more than willing to rant about his strange friendship with Clint.

    He discloses their experience of being trapped in the Raft with the rest of the Avengers and spending hours talking to Clint about anything and everything just to pass the time. He says that even though he didn’t want to admit it, Clint definitely liked him the best out of all the Avengers at the Raft.

    (Kate doesn’t have the heart to tell him that Wanda Maximoff has a guitar at Clint’s farm where Clint’s children speak of her in a very loving manner.)

    He talks about how they were the only two to be put under house arrest so that they could be with their families and how they had formed a shaky bond over that. Mentions how in that time where they had been trying to get everybody back from the Blip, Scott had offered to work on a trick arrow for Clint if he ever needed it in the future. Because even though Clint had been trying to retire for years, there was always that slight chance that he would be pulled back into battle.

    Kate grins guiltily when she tells him about Christmas.

    How she had been the one to pull him back into battle.

    Scott widens his eyes when he hears about everything. Nods in recognition along with Hope when Kate tells them about Bishop Security and her mother.

    It’s difficult not to be charmed by the man’s amazement of the Avengers. Especially not when Kate is a fangirl herself.

    They spend all of the dinner ranting about Captain America and Iron Man, much to Hope’s and Cassie’s dismay, though conversation quickly moves on to how Scott’s technology works. Well, not so much Scott’s as it is Hope’s.

    It’s nice hearing about Clint from somebody else - someone who is as amazed by Hawkeye as she is.

    Cassie is lovely. She and Kate fall into conversation about being heroes, which devolves into something far deeper and more intimate than what Kate has allowed herself to experience in months. Her eyes water when she tells Cassie about her mother, and Cassie is calm and understanding about it all. The younger girl is almost empathetic to a fault, because all of a sudden there are tears streaming down her face and Kate has to comfort her over her own pains and struggles. It’s too funny not to laugh over it, even when the younger girl is pushing her shoulder and telling her to stop.

    She and Cassie exchange numbers, promising to keep in touch.

    When she gets on the plane to Miami, Kate can’t help but feel optimistic about her case.

    Especially with the new trick arrows hidden in her suitcase.

    Her confidence doesn’t last long.

    For a second - for a split second - a thought passes through her mind.

    Maybe I’m not cut out for this kinda thing.

    And whoa.

    Whoa .

    It surprises her because that’s a lil’ out of character, inner-monologue, when did you become such a bummer?

    We’re so cut out for this. We’re doing just fine .

    She ducks behind the nearest column when she sees a glint of a barrel aimed right at her face. Guns. Great. At least her reflexes are getting sharper by the day. Proof that everything is perfect. This is just like her usual ‘stop the thief from getting away’ missions, except now she’s the thief and people are trying to stop her from getting away.

    I’m giving Robin Hood a run for his money.

    A bullet tears through the stone column inches from her eyes.

    “Shit! Fudge!”

    Dust gets in her eyes, which is admittedly not great but she can totally manage. Not like she needs her eyes for anything.

    The mission is going really well. Really.

    With a perfect cover, she managed to sneak into the venue by befriending some very drunk girls outside. They so happened to be best friends with the bride-to-be of the engagement party, and if it hadn’t been for the mission, she would have stuck around to do some shots with Jenifer, Francesca and Gayle. No hesitation. They were sweethearts who were a very fun time and very easy to lose when she pointed out that the drinks at the bar were free.

    (They weren’t.)

    Then, with some pretty amazing espionage work of bumping into the best man and accidentally spilling his wine all over him, Kate discovered that the target she was looking for was a family friend of the groom-to-be’s father. Which is how she managed to get the most sought after USB stick on the East Coast by kicking a 70-year-old man in the face.

    Albeit, a really mean 70-year-old man who totally deserved it after making an inappropriate comment about Kate’s very awesome dress.

    And who was also apparently a snitch.

    Hence the multiple men who now happen to be chasing after her with guns.

    Nothing new, Kate thinks, we’ve been here before, just this time they’re a lot less open to discussing their relationship woes and they’re in suits and ties - hey, maybe New York has the Tracksuit Mafia and Miami has the Suit and Tie Mafia-

    She flinches further away from the gunfire when a bullet skims her upper arm.

    “Fudge nuggets.”

    Unfortunately, she’s still blearily blinking away the dust and tears.

    She supposes it’s karma for kicking an old guy in the face.

    Regardless of whether it’s karma or if it’s just Kate’s luck finally running out, it doesn’t change the fact that she’s practically blind. She can’t analyze her threats with her eyes closed, as much as she jokingly insisted to Clint when he lectured her for her underdeveloped surveying skills.

    Obviously , there have been some hitches in her original plan. Some bumps on the road… but hey, nothing’s perfect. And it’s not like she has any major injuries - nothing that can’t heal with a handy 7/11 slushie and her sturdy first aid kit.

    “Just some-” Kate sniffles as tears roll down her face. “Some good ol’ stone in my eye. No biggie, we got this.”

    The thought of not being cut out for this line of work is already gone, but it’s too late. Her usually stellar confidence has been chipped away by the brief moment of insecurity.

    Though admittedly her confidence hasn’t been the same since receiving a Kingpin sized wake-up call eight months ago, no matter how much she tries convincing herself (and Clint) otherwise.

    A whole tirade of insecure thoughts makes it very hard to confidently judge whether jumping from this balcony and into a very rich Floridian’s swimming pool is a good idea or not.

    She briefly remembers the swooping feeling of falling from urban precipices.

    Yeah, I’d rather not.

    “Okay baby, time to test you out,” she says to her retractable sword strapped to her thigh. The entire night her fingers had been itching to grab the weapon, having yet to use her sword in action despite carrying it everywhere. The feeling of the familiar weight on her right leg has gifted her confidence.

    Dresses are great when she wants to blend in with the rest of the beautiful women at these sorts of events - not particularly practical when fleeing away from the scene. Which is why it has to go. She grabs the gorgeous black fabric, wincing a little at what she’s about to do.

    “You were a beautiful dress,” she says pitifully.

    The sound of fabric ripping tears through the silence as the guards reload their weapons.

    She can’t help the theatrics of pressing her hand up to her mouth in a mournful cry. “Rest in peace,” she breathes. She flinches when the onslaught of bullets resumes. “How rude.”

    In what she hopes is a fluid movement, Kate unsheaths the blade from its place.

    A small smile blooms on her face at the rush of adrenaline that bursts through her. God, I probably look so cool right now, she preens as familiarises herself with the weight of her sword.

    Just as she’s admiring the beautiful birthday present Clint had given her, she hears heavy footsteps rushing towards her.

    It’s one guy. She can take him.

    She smacks the bottom of the hilt into what she was hoping was the man’s face, though she must have miscalculated his height because she gets him in the throat instead.

    “My bad!” She instinctively calls out as the man stumbles backwards, hands at his throat.

    Kate is very aware that if she had hit any harder, the man would be suffocated by his closed airway.

    Not something I want on my conscience.

    Though she supposes, unfortunately, the guy is so sturdy because even with his mouth gasping for air, he still aims his pistol in her face.

    “Not the face!”

    Instinctually, she dodges away from the man’s gunpoint. She does not look like she’s convulsing as she does so.

    Nope. No way.

    With a panicked grunt, Kate swerves the gun away from her body. The gunshot that goes off right beside her head as she and the guy grapple for the weapon in his hand is disorienting. She’s hit with the realization, as she usually is in missions that devolve into fights, that the future of her hearing is looking dreadfully similar to that of her partner’s.

    As she’s manoeuvring the gun out of his hand, she sees movement in her peripheries.

    Another guard appears down the side of the balcony.

    “Sorry dude-” She grimaces, both from strain and sympathy as she throws the man between her and the newcomer. She uses him as a shield from the burst of gunfire. Fortunately for them both, Kate can feel the bulletproof vest beneath the man’s shirt, so she knows that he’s not going to die. However, it doesn’t stop him from feeling the pain. Her eyes widen as the bulky man lets out a scream that’s loud enough to disturb the earth-shattering EDM from the party. “Shhh… shhh.. . ”

    More footsteps ambush her from behind.

    These guys are either not expertly trained or are going easy on her because they’re horrible at sneaking up on her. They could have blown her brains out without her even knowing if they were just slightly quieter.

    Kate pushes the man in her hands toward the other guard, hoping for a distraction.

    She spins. Her blade makes contact with the rifle in the new guard’s arms, parrying it away as it lets loose a burst of bullets. Kate tries to kick him away, but he’s heavier than she anticipated so he barely moves an inch. It gives him the chance to smack her on the side of the head with his rifle.

    She falters to the side with a groan and a tasteful “shit” on her lips.

    Before he can completely aim at her again, Kate kicks his knee reflexively. His weight gives out under him, but his finger pulls at the trigger anyway. She parries the rifle away with her sword, though this time with so much force that it slaps out of the guy’s hands with what she thinks is a crunch.

    “Stop! I will shoot!”

    Kate’s head whips in the direction of the voice. The guard who had shot up his friend has managed to regain composure and is apparently on her ass again.

    A bullet lands right beside her, inches away from her leg.

    Her eyes widen at the burst of rubble beside her. “You shot anyway!” She complains, pushing herself up.

    I should probably get away before things get out of control.

    …

    Things are already out of control, she thinks in what is probably Clint’s voice.

    She has incapacitated the guy with the rifle, his leg twisted at an awkward angle and rifle scattering through the gap between the railing and the tiled balcony.

    That has to hurt-

    A singular gunshot rings out. She feels the bullet skim the side of her head.

    Dizziness washes over her. A curtain of red falls over her, the familiar swell of anger. It’s not a foreign sensation. When in pain, Kate often feels a fog of fury settle over her mind. One that reminds her of the many times her coaches had warned her from getting carried away by her emotions. Getting points deducted for misconduct was the last thing they wanted in competitions, and despite Kate’s impressive skill, it was one of her faults.

    No one is counting points here.

    Kate swings her blade at her last assailant, backing him up against the railing with her repetitive attacks. She can see the annoyance growing on his red face.

    When the man punches her in the eye, she’s not that surprised.

    “Ow, that hurt-”

    She is surprised when she hears a gunshot and feels a numbing pain on her side.

    Kate has already driven her blade into the guy’s thigh because the last thing she wants to do is be responsible for is the murder of someone’s family member. Besides, Clint had already told her that if you get the legs, then they can’t run after you when you evacuate. So she gets him where she knows he’s not going to die.

    But it seems that he didn’t get the memo, because she sees her vision brighten white momentarily. Pain briefly washes over her, though it’s quickly overridden by a cold burst of adrenaline.

    Yeah, she’s a little mad.

    She headbutts the guy on the nose, hard enough to make her forehead bleed.

    He slaps his hand in her face, pushing her away like a small child. She swats it away with the side of her wrist.

    An idea pushes its way into her head. It’s a dirty trick that she remembers her friends doing to each other at galas when she was younger, but she’s currently fighting for her life so she doesn’t care whether it’s dirty or not. She grips the man’s tie in her left hand and lets herself drop to her knees. The tie tightens around his neck and a choking sound erupts from his mouth.

    Kate sighs in relief as the guy crumbles, hands on his throat.

    I’ve overstayed my welcome.

    She knows that these three men aren’t the only ones who were tasked to overlook the safety of this USB. She either takes this window of opportunity or she stays and fights. Considering the wave of dizziness that hits her as the adrenaline wanes a little, she already knows what her choice is.

    It’s then when she’s reminded of the prior lapse in confidence. That maybe she isn’t cut out for Avenger-sized fights if she just barely managed to take down three amateur guards.

    Normally, Kate would be terrified to leap off a building without her grapple arrow. The new insecurity makes it ten times worse.

    After the events at the Rockefeller Centre, Clint had ranted to her about not jumping out of windows twenty floors up without being attached to anything. Kate has come to realize how wrong that fall could have gone for her. She’d felt the after-effects of her fall in her hips and knees for days after, leaving her incapable of engaging in Clint’s hand-to-hand combat training, much to her dismay.

    So she’s not very comfortable around heights without her favorite trick arrow.

    And last she checked her bow and arrows are still stashed safely in her hotel room.

    She hears the shouts approaching her location above the EDM.

    “Come on Bishop,” Kate hisses to herself. She grunts at the sharp pain that shoots up her body from her waist as she walks backwards in preparation for a run-up. “ Pull yourself together .”

    Now, Clint may call her impulsive, rash, reckless…

    Kate likes to think of it as daring, brave. Heroic , if you will.

    It makes things like launching yourself off the third floor of a very tall villa despite all rationale a lot easier to do. Which is… fine, considering that she has been cornered embarrassingly quickly and had to choose between fighting it out with armed guards or jumping off a building without her trick arrows.

    Jumping off a building trumps potential death. For sure.

    Kate kicks off her heels and grabs them in her hand, grimacing when pain shoots up her side at the strain. Without another second thought, because she knows that she’ll back out if she lets herself reconsider her options, she charges at the balcony and vaults herself over the edge.

    Except her namesake isn’t literal, so she doesn’t possess wings.

    The swooping sensation in her stomach makes her heart stop.

    How does Yelena do this all the time? Does she practice this kind of thing or is she just naturally cool?

    If Clint asks her later, she doesn’t scream. No way. She’s cool. In fact, she looks pretty fricken awesome as bullets fly through her hair and past her body to slap into the water. Like in the movies, with an explosion bursting out behind her.

    Kate barely has time to hold her breath. Water surges up her nose. She feels a sharp pain in her legs as she hits the mosaic at the bottom of the pool.

    The cold momentarily numbs her brain and she forgets what she’s doing. Until she feels a sharp prick at her leg, which has her opening her eyes in the water and remembering just what she’s doing.

    She bursts into a panicked swim.

    Gasping for air, she heaves herself out of the water. “Never going anywhere without my bow and arrows ever again.” She curses, narrowly dodging the bullets that follow after her.

    Kate disappears into the foliage and trees and doesn’t stop running.

    The thick, humid Miami heat clings to her - fills her lungs with hot air that has her head spinning. Twigs and branches slap her in the face in spite of her efforts of swatting the offending obstacles out of her way. She’s sure that if she weren’t running this quickly, then she would have been devoured by mosquitos. Her skin perspires in the heat, wet hair clinging to her shoulders and her exposed back.

    She doesn’t stop running until she reaches her destination.

    Her auto-pilot mode turns off, and Kate finally begins to feel the wobble in her legs as the adrenaline seeps out of her.

    Still, with stone-cold determination, she struts forward. She must look like a swamp monster. It’s Florida, people aren’t gonna care. Even so, she finds herself grabbing a beige bucket hat from a stand outside a souvenir shop just to comfort herself and whoever will have the misfortune of attending to her tonight.

    The fatigue only just manages to settle into her body, but she powers through it because that’s what an Avenger would do.

    Kate places the hat on her wet head as she storms into the 7/11.

    She is getting herself that damn slushie.

    Chapter End Notes apologies for the lack of yelena in this chapter, she will be showing up in part two miami - two Hallucinations are a side effect of a lack of blood flow in the brain.

    It’s what she says to herself as she sips on her slushie and blearily squints her eyes at what she’s convinced is a hallucination of Yelena striding out of the foliage and trees. Like she’s an angel descending from the heavens.

    Kate watches as the angel comes closer, drinking her slushie and ignoring the thrumming pain crawling under her skin.

    Yelena’s blonde hair is tied up in a beautiful bun at the back of her head, lifted to reveal the length of her tantalizing neck. She takes a firm step and the right side of her fringe falls into her face. Kate’s fingers tighten around her slushie when she thinks about pushing the hair back into place. How soft the texture of her hair would feel beneath her fingers. How warm the skin at her cheeks would be under her fingertips.

    If she tries to focus, Kate thinks that she can see dramatic gold make-up accentuating the green in her eyes. An emerald dress only deepens the evergreen hues and with a plunging neckline, the outfit has Kate’s eyes lingering on the taunting expanse of bare skin. It’s difficult to tear her eyes away from the woman’s cleavage when there is a swooping necklace resting right between her breasts, glinting under the orange lamppost light. A manicured hand grips the excess fabric of the green dress, lifting to prevent it from catching around her feet. The motion reveals the toned muscles at her calves and thighs jumping with every step.

    Yelena looks gorgeous. Humidity has formed a glisten on the round of her shoulders and thighs and cheekbones. Green is perfect on her tan skin and bright gold hair.

    If she weren’t an assassin, she would do a perfect job of being a model.

    Especially since she’s walking like she’s on a catwalk.

    Kate is glad for her raspberry-cherry-blueberry-lemonade concoction because her tongue would have been sandpaper otherwise.

    When she feels Yelena’s eyes meet hers across the small parking lot behind the 7/11, Kate begins to realize that this isn’t a hallucination. That Yelena, the Black Widow assassin that has been appearing at the most random moments in her life, is actually in Miami at the same shitty 7/11 that she is at.

    And she looks very, very unimpressed.

    “If it isn't my favorite assassin,” Kate says around her straw.

    Legs sprawled out as decently as her injuries will allow, Kate musters up a grin for the woman strutting toward her. Her head feels heavy, so she lets it roll back against the concrete wall she’s propped up against. The bucket hat on her head dips down further on her forehead, coming to rest on her eyebrows.

    Yelena is scowling possibly harder than she’s ever seen scowl.

    It’s kind of attractive.

    Her voice is low and gravelly as she approaches Kate, her heels clacking threateningly against the tarmac. “You have been shot.”

    God, what a woman.

    Wincing at the reminder of her injuries, Kate swallows the soothing sweetness in her mouth. “Great observation,” she wheezes, squeezing her eyes shut at both the brain freeze and the numbing pain at her side and leg.

    Kate feels the pressure on her stomach before she registers that Yelena has finally reached her.

    “You’re bleeding,” Yelena grumbles, crouching in front of her.

    Her fringe falls over her forehead, brushing over her long eyelashes which are painted gold like her eyeshadow. Kate puffs out a breath of cool air, trying to blow away the offending strands of hair out of the blonde’s way.

    One of Yelena’s hands pulls away from Kate’s body to assess the damage. Her hand, nails painted both green and gold, pulls back with dark scarlet. It’s a shame that the three rings wrapped around her fingers - a thick, detailed one at her thumb, a thin golden strip around her forefinger, and a phalanx ring at her middle finger - are stained with Kate’s blood. They would have completed Yelena’s look.

    Yelena frowns, muttering under her breath.

    Kate lets out a loud cry at the dizzying pain that pierces her side when two hands put pressure on the poorly bandaged wound. The hand that had been limp at her side shoots up to grab Yelena’s shoulder as if to stop her from applying pressure but thinking better of it.

    “Another good point,” Kate breathes when she gets used to the pain, gritting her teeth as Yelena grabs her shoulder and pulls her upper body towards her. “Well done.”

    The blonde leans into her, the fabric of her dress stooping in a way that has Kate’s eyes following the movement.

    Boobs.

    Dizzy from the pain (and maybe arousal), Kate lets herself slump against the smaller woman’s body. She smells really good. Her chin rests on the rayon of her green dress while Yelena reaches a hand around to her back, probably to check for an exit wound for the bullet.

    Is that cherry?

    A sound of frustration from the blonde cuts through Kate’s internal tirade. With the shoulder Kate is resting her chin on, Yelena pushes her back into the wall. “Why didn’t you bandage yourself before?” She asks with a growl in the back of her throat. Her hands are back to pressing into her stomach and her face is scrunching into an irate frown that has Kate cowering into the wall.

    “Didn't really have the time to think about it, y’know.” Kate raises the slushie to her lips and takes a little sip to soothe her nerves. Yelena’s glare hardens at the slurping sound. “Too busy running away from the Suit and Tie Mafia.”

    “You're an idiot,” Yelena hisses, her accent curling around the words.

    “Gee, thanks.”

    “You left a trail of blood.” The woman grabs Kate’s limp hand that had been resting on her shoulder. “Easily traceable.” Blood taints the back of Kate’s hand as Yelena places it on her wound. “You would be dead.”

    “It sure is a good thing you found me first then, no?” She tries to grin up at Yelena, though she finds that the rim of the bucket hat that sits too low on her head impedes her sight.

    But Yelena must have seen her attempt, because the pressure at her hand increases as the woman presses her hand into hers, most likely scolding her for her sarcasm. “ Suka .”

    Kate flinches and groans, instinctively moving the hand holding the slushie onto Yelena’s shoulder to gather as much reassurance as possible. Taking the hint, Kate applies pressure to her wound. She gathers the reassurance from the way that Yelena is mumbling in Russian under her breath, wet fingers tangling in Kate’s as they both try to stop the bleeding at her side.

    “Bitch to you too, damn,” she grits out, clenching her eyes shut and bringing up the slushie to her lips for another comforting sip. “What are you doing here anyway?”

    Yelena glances up from where she’s checking Kate’s leg. “Stop.”

    “Stop what?”

    The cup is slapped out of Kate’s hand. “That.”

    “Hey!” Her cry is loud and slightly jarring in the awkwardly silent parking lot. Kate stares at the spilt cup of purple deliciousness with wide, mournful eyes. She snaps her head back to Yelena, putting as much hatred as she can into glaring at her. “I was drinking that.”

    “Idiot. Fucking idiot .” Grumbling under her breath, Yelena moves her hands from Kate’s side to her own. She unbuckles the belt around her waist with a soft snap. “Kate Bishop you are the biggest idiot.”

    “Aw, thank you for considering me in the running for ‘Biggest Idiot.’” She pretends to sniff and wipe a tear away from her eyes.

    However, the same hand she uses to be annoying flies to Yelena’s shoulder again when the latter frantically wraps the belt around her leg several times and tightens it. Searing white pain shoots through her body preventing any niceties. The skin underneath the palm of Kate’s hand is soft and smooth and warm, but despite the bolt of electricity that flies through her arm at the contact, Kate doesn’t bother with her usual shyness. Her nails dig into the skin there and Yelena doesn’t flinch.

    Once Yelena deems the belt tight enough, she puts her hands on Kate’s shoulders and uses her as support to push herself up with a strained groan. “Come on,” she says, stretching out an arm for Kate to grab. “Get up.”

    Kate feels a bead of sweat rolling down the side of her face as she attempts to reach for Yelena’s arm. Her fingers latch onto the Widow Bite’s cuffed around her wrists, but Kate is smart enough to know not to pull and instead fixes her grip around Yelena’s hand. She puts all of her weight onto her good leg - her right leg - and tries to stand up. Her leg gives out under her and she falls back down on her ass.

    Annoyance pricks at Kate. “Kinda hard right now, sorry,” she snaps, a scowl curling at her lips.

    It’s not like Yelena has done anything wrong, it’s just that… it’s frustrating. And mildly terrifying. There are very few times where Kate lacks proper coordination. She prides herself in being perfectly in-tune with her body at all times after years of honing her archery and martial arts skills. The times where she isn’t coordinated, she is in the safety of her own home tripping over her own two feet as she tries to dance in her organized mess, or absolutely wasted in the company of people that she trusts. The loss of control that comes from being injured as badly as she is - about relying on somebody else for something mission-related - unearths something ugly inside of Kate.

    You’re being a little bitch right now.

    Clenching her eyes shut, Kate blocks out her inner monologue, which is currently swinging hard between ‘Yelena hot’ and ‘I’m a loser' .

    “I think the adrenaline is gone,” she says, softer in an unspoken form of apology. Yelena’s eyebrows relax a little, recognizing the change in tone for what it is. “Now… now it just hurts. Like a bitch.”

    Yelena has a strange look on her face. If Kate had more energy in her, she would make a teasing remark about how she looks constipated, but Kate has been shot twice so Yelena is lucky. If Kate didn’t know any better, she would think that the assassin looks conflicted. As if she doesn’t know what to do.

    “I should leave you here.” Yelena is frowning. Hard. There’s a deep crease between her eyebrows. “Leave you to die.”

    Kate huffs out a laugh. “You wouldn't, you like me too much.”

    Quirking an eyebrow, Yelena retracts her hand. “What makes you so sure?”

    She wouldn’t, Kate chuckles, trying to find the mirth that usually gleans in those green eyes.

    She can’t find it.

    Uncertainty begins to build as the blonde turns on her heel and begins to walk away from her. “Come on, you wouldn't.” Kate’s hand subconsciously loosens the pressure at her side and she feels the blood pour out of the wound and over the sides of her fingers. Panic bursts into her mind, a cold dread washing over her as she watches Yelena’s back walk further and further away. “You wouldn't .”

    She’s leaving you.

    The assassin keeps walking, hands wiping at the insides of her rayon dress to rid herself of Kate’s blood. Rid herself of any reminder that she and Kate met.

    Kate can do nothing but stare.

    You’re useless.

    When Yelena disappears around the corner of the building, Kate holds her breath. Maybe waiting for the woman to jump back around, a bright grin on her face and shaking jazz hands at her to say that she’s joking. But that’s not something Yelena would do. (Or at least, she doesn’t think Yelena would do that, because she doesn’t know much about the blonde and that’s a whole other terrifying thought process she doesn’t want to divulge right now.)

    She left you because you’re useless. You can’t do anything. You can’t do anything right.

    The tips of Kate’s fingertips are buzzing like the bubbles in a soda can and it sets her breathing off-kilter.

    You’re alone. You push everyone away. Just like Mom.

    Kate presses a hand to her eyes. She presses hard enough to cause a burst of light to flash at her eyelids where her fingers are. All she needs to do is regain her senses, and then she’ll be able to pull herself together and get some medical help. She just needs to regain the confidence to push herself up from her spot and go after the blonde.

    “Yelena?”

    Using the resistance from the concrete wall behind her and her good leg to push herself up from the tarmac, Kate manages to lift herself from the ground. The rough texture of the wall has the skin at the palm of her hands stinging as she scrambles to keep herself lifted. Sweat builds at her forehead where the bucket hat suddenly feels too tight. Her wet hair clings to the back of her neck and her exposed shoulders and it feels like something is holding onto her. Looming over her back. It worsens the moisture accumulating in her heaving chest.

    Once she’s on her feet Kate takes a moment to breathe. She tries to recall the exercises the therapist from her childhood had taught her. The ones that she had to do to keep herself from having a panic attack whenever somebody would mention her father or her father’s death.

    Damn, it’s been a while.

    Exhaling shakily, she clenches her fists at her sides and musters up the energy. Resting a fist against the wall for support, Kate begins to hobble towards the direction Yelena has disappeared in.

    “Yelena,” she calls, voice wobbling.

    You’re not cut out for this. You can’t even look after your wounds.

    Fuck you inner monologue, you’re being an ass.

    Why didn’t you just go back to the motel? You have your med kit there.

    …

    You’re useless.

    Kate’s breathing falters and she stumbles. “Yelena?”

    Her vision begins to dim around the edges, but she can faintly feel her feet dragging her forward to the corner of the building.

    “Ye-” She gasps when her chest begins to ache. Like her lungs have collapsed. “-lena!”

    I’m gonna die. I’m gonna die by myself. I’m gonna die by myself in Florida. Of all places.

    “Fuck.” She wheezes, groaning a little when the pain at the side of her head begins to throb. Tears prick at her eyes, but she refuses to let them fall with stubborn determination. “Why why why…”

    Why Florida?

    Why now?

    Why?

    “What are you doing?”

    Yelena, now donning her own black bucket hat with ‘I love Miami’ written across it in white bold letters, is standing only feet away from her. It casts a shadow over her face, the rim almost shielding her green eyes away from Kate’s startled gaze, but she looks inexplicably good in it. There’s an exasperated expression pulling her mouth down in displeasure but Kate is too wrapped up in the burst of emotions that lift the pressure at her chest to care.

    She almost sobs in relief.

    “I thought you left,” Kate breathes.

    At the sound of her voice cracking, Yelena’s features melt. Audibly inhaling, Yelena’s shoulders tighten. Her lips press into a thin line, the hard lines around her mouth disappearing. The exasperation transforms into something unreadable. Kate wants to think that from the way her eyebrows furrow and the glare in her eyes soften, Yelena looks concerned. (Kate would be concerned, too. She probably looks like she’s one breath away from having a full-blown panic attack.)

    The blonde grasps at Kate’s elbows with the tips of her fingers to stop her from collapsing against her. Plastic crinkles against Kate’s forearms where Yelena’s hands are.

    Kate stumbles forward and Yelena lets her step into her space. Allows Kate to bunch her blood-stained hand in the silky fabric of her emerald dress.

    They stand close enough that when Yelena exhales through her mouth - too soft to be a sigh but too loud to be anything but a ‘what am I going to do with you’ - Kate can feel it on her lips. Despite the humid heat permeating the night, Kate’s body trembles. Yelena’s hands, which are still at her elbows, are calm and stable. It feels like an anchor that she can cling onto while she steadies her breathing.

    Yelena is silent as she does so. Her eyes are unwavering, though they are deeply guarded and Kate wants to know why. She wants to know why the assassin looks like she wants to combust.

    She doesn’t ask.

    When Kate’s breathing is steadier, Yelena glances around them in a sweep of their surroundings.

    “Come on.” She gently nudges Kate. She wraps an arm around her waist to encourage the taller girl to swing her arm around her shoulders.

    Slowly to not aggravate Kate’s injuries further, they make their way over to a picnic table on a patch of grass beside the 7/11. Kate struggles not to inhale the scent of cherry, chlorine and blood in the space between them. The wrist hanging at Yelena’s shoulder is being clutched by the blonde’s hand, whose fingers struggle to grab both Kate and the clean bandages and bottle of water. At Kate’s hip, deft fingers grip the skin there both in reassurance and in support.

    “Sit down.”
