    Kate hums, frowning down at her laptop screen. “This doesn’t look like anything that would be useful to a business.” She grimaces as she tries to salvage the stitches at her side, willing her hand not to shake as she holds the needle to her skin. “ Ouch - why would John Earl want this?”

    “My contact is still trying to find out more about this guy.”

    “Did they find anything?” Kate huffs when she tugs the thread at her skin. A YouTube tutorial is the best she can do in terms of guidance here.

    “Everything is quiet.” Clint grumbles, voice distant as he walks away from his phone. (It’s a habit that Kate is trying to get him to break out of because she has to remind him time and time again that she can’t hear him if he’s at the other side of the room.) “No news or headlines on him or his family since last year.”

    “I couldn't find anything either,” she says, dropping the needle at the surface of the table where she has her laptop decked out.

    “I have a bad feeling about him.”

    “You and me both.”

    Kate drops into the chair, taking a hearty gulp of water from the large bottle she had gotten from the crappy vending machine outside. Her face is still aching from the blows that Yelena landed on her, now and then pulsing to remind her of her encounter with the blonde.

    “What does Yelena want with this?” She wonders out loud, remembering the cuts and bruises the assassin had acquired. Kate had barely managed to properly land any hits, and even then the only blood that had been drawn had been from the injury that had already been there on her forehead. If the Black Widow was that injured, then that means she wants that USB just as much as she does.

    Clint grunts. “Could be a case.”

    Humming, Kate takes another swig at the water.

    There’s silence on the other end of the line, but Kate can feel the anticipation through the phone. She knows well enough by now that whenever Clint goes deadly quiet - his movements still and his habit of shifting and shuffling coming to rest - it means that he is mulling something over. That he wants to say something. “How are you handling that?” He asks finally.

    “I'm okay.” Kate sighs. She thinks back to the feeling of Yelena successfully grappling her, warm thighs squeezing the life out of her and lips pressed to the shell of her ear. Head hanging back, Kate presses a hand to her eyes and holds back a frustrated groan. “Still pretty pissed.”

    Clint makes a sound of understanding. He shuffles around and there is a faint sound of ‘clacking’ and ‘ thumping’ through the speakers. “Can't trust an international spy too easily.”

    Kate feels her ego cry out inside of her. “I know that.”

    “It's not your fault, kid.”

    “Hm.”

    “Hey, listen.” His voice comes closer and whatever it is that he’s doing on the other end of the line stops. “I can drive up and be in New York in six days to help out with Earl.”

    “I don't need help, Clint.”

    “I know you don't,” he says, his smile showing through the cracks of his voice. “I just miss you.”

    The smile that spreads across Kate’s face is inevitable, as is the warm feeling in her chest. “Don't use your rare moments of softness on me,” she laughs, though it’s strained as she tries to stop the swell of emotions as she remembers what it’s like to be at the Barton farm. The constant company is available to her whenever she pleases, whether it’s Lucky pressing against her side or Nate deciding that his favorite seat in the house is Kate’s lap. The scent of dry grass of the countryside and the fresh sunlight streaming through the windows. Voices shouting over each other from somewhere in the house as Lila and Cooper argue about the smallest of things.

    “The kids miss you too, you know?”

    Kate’s smile is bittersweet. She pulls a new shirt over her head, trying to put a layer between her heart and the rest of the world. “Yeah, about that,” she says. “Nate has been spamming me with the most random gifs.”

    “Oh I know,” Clint laughs. Something clatters outside of her motel room. “He's been going crazy. Laura had to-”

    Kate stops listening, standing from her chair and reaching for the sword holstered to her thigh. “Clint, wait.” Quietly, she grabs her laptop and slips it into her duffel bag, backing away from the window in front of the desk slowly as she closes the bag.

    It’s deadly silent. Her gut is tugging in her stomach.

    We need to run. Don’t be dumb like those horror movie protagonists and listen to your instincts. There’s someone outside, go.

    Hoisting the bag over her shoulder, Kate reaches for her phone on the table and takes it off the speaker. “I’m gonna have to go,” she breathes.

    “Everything okay?” Clint’s voice is rough with concern.

    Kate glances around the room, making sure that she isn’t leaving anything important behind. “Yeah, just-”

    The glass of the window shatters as an eruption of gunfire tears through.

    “Shit.”

    “Kate-”

    “Gotta go.”

    Ducking, Kate makes sure that the spray of bullets is random and that whoever is outside doesn’t know where she is. Holes pierce themselves in the wall over the bed. While the sound is loud enough to disguise her movement, she crawls her way over to the bathroom. There’s a window big enough for her to crawl through which she had the foresight to prepare in case she needed to make a dramatic exit out of, a box of cleaning products that had been left under the bathroom counter already propped up against the toilet lid for her to hop onto.

    She hoists the duffel bag on the edge of the window, reluctant to throw it outside in case somebody is waiting for her to do just that. With desperate determination, she pulls herself into the gap.

    She can hear the bedroom door being kicked open. Screams from other residents at the motel resonate through the walls.

    Kate’s heart pounds in her chest as she drops out onto the other side of the window, tugging the duffle bag’s straps over her shoulders like it’s a backpack as she grips her sword tightly. She makes a run for it across the parking lot, hiding behind the parked cars and making sure that her movements are never straight bee-lines. Worst way to end up getting shot - she hates it when movie characters do that.

    Just when she thinks that she’s lost her assailant, the glass of the window of the car she is hiding behind bursts above her head. Shards scatter into her hair.

    “You’re gonna have to pay for that!” She calls out to her enemy, shaking off the glass.

    No point in pretending they don’t know where I am.

    More bullets spray the car, lodging into the one parked right beside it for good measure.

    Kate curses. She can hear the faint scuffing of boots to her right. So she goes left instead, crawling on all fours to stay hidden behind the body of the car.

    “Thank god Clint isn’t here to see this,” she grumbles.

    When there’s another round of gunfire, she makes a run for a large pickup truck just a parking space away. From the corner of her eye, she catches sight of her assailant. A dark- skinned woman, dressed in all black despite the humid, August heat, with a rifle larger than her bow and ammunition swinging at her chest in holsters. Her black hair is pulled back into braids not too dissimilar to the ones Yelena likes to incorporate into her own hairstyles.

    Their eyes meet, and the assault rifle is immediately trained on her.

    Kate throws herself behind the body of the truck, clenching her eyes shut as she feels the vehicle swaying from the force of the bullets.

    “Wow, you really want me dead,” she says more to herself than to her enemy. She swings her duffel bag off her shoulders, pretending like her hands aren’t shaking as she tears it open and struggles to pull her bow out. The corners catch on the lips of the bag. “Shit shit shit… now is not the time.”

    The footsteps of her assailant are getting closer. Kate thinks that maybe she’s getting a kick out of terrorizing her with the incessant gunfire.

    You are not gonna die here, Bishop.

    Giving up on her bow, Kate spins in place and extends her sword.

    As the woman appears at the end of the pickup truck, aiming the rifle at Kate with the finger ready to pull the trigger, Kate sends a quick prayer to Thor and hopes that the blade of her sword is big enough to deflect bullets.

    She doesn’t have to worry about that though, because there’s a flurry of black and blonde and then the rifle is being kicked from the woman’s hands.

    Yelena is here, dressed in black pants that look really comfortable and Kate will have to ask her where she got them because that kick was pretty high and it looked effortless in the material. Her blonde hair is tied up now into two separate ponytails, small braids tangled in them as they swing around to follow her momentum. Momentarily, Kate worries for the assassin, because the sweltering heat of the sun can’t be doing anything good to the tight black shirt clinging to her body. Not that the shirt is doing anything good to Kate’s mental state either, what with the blonde’s muscles visibly flexing beneath them as she goes hand-to- hand with the woman.

    Kate snaps herself out of her stupor, shaking her head and goes back to extracting her bow from the duffel bag now that her assailant is distracted.

    She swaps it out for her sword, deciding that if there’s going to be assault rifles in this fight then she should at least get to use her speciality. Muscle memory pulls her quiver over her head, and as soon as the familiar weight is strapped on her back, her hand is rifling through the fletchings for an electro-arrow.

    Yelena is wrestling a knife from the woman’s hand, barely dodging a slash to her stomach.

    We don’t have the time for trick arrows!

    Gritting her teeth, Kate yanks on one of her normal arrows and is shooting it through the air before she can even take a breath.

    It manages to catch the woman in the shoulder, right over Yelena’s head where the Black Widow had been attempting to wrestle her away from Kate. Feeling the sudden jolt in the woman above her, Yelena releases her and kicks her in the chest.

    Oof, yeah she’s definitely gonna be feeling that for days, Kate laments, grimacing in sympathy.

    Yelena spins around to look at her, green eyes alight with exasperation and concern. Kate thinks that the assassin isn’t even trying to hide anything from her anymore.

    That being said, she remembers the last time she thought Yelena hadn’t been trying to hide anything from her and how that had ended. Kate’s lips pull into a scowl as Yelena paces toward her, her hand snatching her duffel bag closer where her laptop and the USB are.

    There’s an overwhelming surge of fury rising inside of her. “What the fuck are you doing here,” she growls, pulling herself up from the concrete with help of the pickup truck.

    “Saving your ass.” Yelena presses a gloved hand to Kate’s uninjured side as she reaches her.

    “I don’t need you to-”

    “ Move .”

    Despite her reluctance, Kate obliges wordlessly. She follows Yelena as the blonde heads back toward the motel, glaring hard at the space between her shoulder blades. She’s wearing her scabbard again, knives poised and ready for her disposal at her shoulder.

    She’s so fast, Kate thinks, her eyes widening as the distance between her and the assassin begins to grow.

    In all fairness, I was shot in the leg.

    Oh sure, if that’s the excuse you want to use.

    It’s a valid excuse.

    Kate’s eyes stray. There’s a glint in the reflection of one of the windows of the motel room. The hairs at the back of her neck raise, her gut tugging wildly at her stomach. With a shot of adrenaline burning through her muscles, she sprints forward and catches Yelena around the waist. She manages to tackle her to the floor just as bullets spray the space where they had just been seconds before. Instinctively, Kate wraps herself around the smaller woman, arms surrounding her body protectively. Clenching her eyes shut, Kate silently prays that the bullets won’t hit Yelena. Though it seems that the assassin has other plans because she pulls at the collar of her shirt and drags her away from where they’re sprawled. Bullets chase their moving bodies. They scatter across the concrete, shuffling so that they can hide at the front of a car.

    “ Kate ,” Yelena snarls. The assassin crowds into Kate’s space. Her eyes are burning with a fire that Kate has never seen before. “What was that?” Kate feels the leather of her gloves scraping at her chest as Yelena grabs the collar of her shirt. “Were you planning to take those bullets? You are not bulletproof .”

    Absent-mindedly, she tries to push away Yelena’s hand. “You’re welcome, geez.” She’s panting hard, trying to catch her breath.

    Not satisfied with her answer, Yelena presses her forehead against hers. It stings at the bruise that had formed there when she smacked the blonde in the face. “Don’t.” Her breath brushes against Kate’s parted lips. “Don’t be an idiot.”

    Kate glares right back at her. “I’m not. I’m being rational.” She stubbornly pushes back against Yelena’s forehead and their noses brush. Her heart thunders in her chest. “Which one of us will be more useful when the other is injured? Which one of us has more experience?”

    For a second it almost looks like Yelena wants to fight back. She doesn’t. Instead, she exits Kate’s space with a curse in a foreign language.

    Yelena unholsters a gun from her hip and focuses on aiming over the hood of the car they’re hidden behind, tugging down on the trigger to dissuade their assailant from approaching.

    “Who is that?” Kate asks.

    “Someone who really wants us dead.”

    Kate pulls an explosive arrow from her quiver. “Really?” She drawls. She nocks the arrow and waits for Yelena to drop back down for cover. “I would have never guessed.”

    “Dip shit.” The assassin drops down.

    Just as quick, Kate stands without giving the assailant a chance to think that she can shoot back. She releases the arrow in the direction Yelena had been shooting.

    As soon as the bowstring is released, Yelena is there tugging at her arm. Neither of them wait to watch the arrow land. Kate chases at Yelena’s heels, flinching when she hears and feels the explosion go off behind them. She hopes that it will give them enough time.

    Relief floods her when they just manage to make it around the corner before an onslaught of bullets catch the wood of the buildings.

    “That bike there,” Yelena says, slowing down from a sprint into a jog. There’s a beautiful black motorbike parked at the front of the motel on the grass and Kate has to stop herself from commenting on how gorgeous the vehicle is because damn . “Get on it and turn on the engine.” She throws a set of keys at Kate as she spins around, pulling out a gun to check that they’re not being followed.

    It’s hers?!

    That’s hot.

    Shut the hell up oh my god-

    Kate fumbles with the keys as she runs toward the bike. She tries not to think about Yelena sitting on the bike, her thighs wrapped around the body and her biceps flexing as she revs the engine. But the images are already flooding her mind and it’s making it very difficult to figure out how to turn on the engine.

    See what you did? We can barely function now!

    Gunshots go off just as the engine roars to life, and Kate allows herself a small victory cry. The leather seat is warm beneath her and the body of the bike shakes impatiently.

    It makes her feel like a badass.

    “Get on!” She calls to Yelena, who is slowly walking backwards toward her with her gun still trained at the side of the building.

    “I'm driving.”

    “Yelena-”

    “Kate.” Yelena reaches her, back pressed to her knee. “Move.”

    Clicking her tongue, Kate reluctantly slides backwards. “Fine.” She waits until Yelena finally looks away from the building and swings her leg over the bike. Swiftly, Kate snatches Yelena’s gun from her hand. “Give me that.”

    Yelena’s head twists over her shoulder, the offended expression on her face hilariously comical. “ Suka .”

    The grin that Kate gives in response must irritate the blonde enough for her to abruptly rev the engine and send the back wheel spinning against the grass. It immediately wipes the smile off of the archer’s face. “Bitch,” Kate shouts over the sound of the bike tearing off and into the road, her arms scrambling to wrap around Yelena’s waist so that she isn’t thrown off from the inertia.

    She doesn’t miss the little laugh that Yelena does, but she decides that it’s better to pretend like she doesn’t hear it.

    -

    “I'm gonna need you to start explaining.”

    Yelena shuts the door behind them and locks it. “There's nothing to say.”

    Bristling, Kate turns to face the woman and places the duffel bag onto the coffee table.

    “Yelena, I swear to God if you don't tell me what the hell is going on I'm going to knock you out again.”

    At that, Yelena’s expression brightens in disbelief. Her eyebrows rise to her hairline and a gorgeous smile blooms on her lips. “You? Knock me out?” She laughs that annoying laugh that makes Kate feel like she’s an idiot, and the archer flexes her hands and grits her teeth. “I would like to see you try.”

    “You want me to remind you that I knocked you out in this room?” Pointedly, Kate looks toward the sleeping-gas arrow neatly placed on the coffee table beside the putty arrow.

    Yelena shrugs. “Beginner’s luck.”

    “Don't make me do it again.”

    Nonchalantly, Yelena fixes the stray strands of hair out of her face as she walks over to where Kate is standing. “Your interrogation techniques require work,” she says, a mischievous glint in her eye.

    It pokes at the barely contained rage inside of her, which is only getting worse the longer Yelena pretends like nothing is wrong and they can continue playing this game where they pretend to be anything more than acquaintances. When Yelena reaches for her duffel bag, Kate can’t stop the rage from bursting through the seams. The palm of her hand makes contact with Yelena’s cheek, not too dissimilar to how it had done in that elevator at the Rockefeller Centre. The resounding slap rings out in the warm heat of the apartment, and Kate immediately registers her lapse in judgement when the assassin snatches her wrist out of the air and twists it.

    “Kate Bishop.” Yelena’s fingers tighten into the tendons of her forearm. Her eyes are dark, the light shining behind her casting a gloom on her expression that reflects the scorching wrath that Kate feels inside her. It makes her blood run cold as Yelena hisses, “if you do that again, I will cut off your hand.”

    Stubbornly, Kate narrows her eyes. She hides the fear that races through her body behind her grudge and merely swallows the lump that forms in her throat.

    The lack of regret in her silence only incites even more anger on Yelena’s end. The blonde steps into her space (Kate’s breath does not catch) so that if she wanted to, she could lean in and easily press her lips against the archer’s. But she doesn’t. Instead, she yanks at Kate’s wrist. Kate stumbles forward into the little space that is left between them and feels their foreheads press together like they had in the parking lot.

    Kiss her.

    No, punch her.

    Kate holds her breath, willing herself not to breathe in the familiar scent of leather and vanilla and orchids. It brings out a false sense of security and a yearning to forgive the woman. But she reminds herself of the pain she has been feeling for the past six days and steels her resolve.

    “Your life is in danger because of this information,” she says. Her voice is low, devoid of emotion but Kate can see the way the brown flecks kindling in the dark emerald threaten to slit her throat then and there. “There are a lot of people after this who would kill anybody to get it.”

    “Including you,” Kate breathes.

    “I would not kill you.” Yelena’s glare softens and her immobilizing grip at her wrist loosens. Kate is positive that she will have a bruise there tomorrow. “We can work together.”

    Laughing incredulously, Kate pulls back and out of Yelena’s dizzying presence. “After what you did?” Her chest aches painfully at the reminder. “You think that I can trust you after that?”

    Yelena frowns and rolls her eyes in annoyance. “I did what I had to do.”

    “You’re insufferable,” Kate growls, walking away from Yelena entirely because her heart is beating too quick and her mind is tearing itself apart as she briefly considers the woman’s proposition.

    “I could have let you die.”

    “Why didn't you?” Kate spins back around. Her glare is accusatory.

    Surprisingly, Yelena does not have an answer for her. She merely presses her lips together and looks away from Kate, her hands playing with the Widow Bites at her wrists.

    It only makes her angrier, because why won’t she talk about it? Why won’t she talk about how she went out of her way to patch her up when she could have just left her to die like she said? It’s frustrating because it makes the optimistic part of her deliriously hopeful that Yelena cares.

    Huffing, Kate stretches to pick up the duffel bag from the table and turns to sit down in the armchair. There’s adrenaline coursing through her body but her body is aching from head to toe, and she needs to sit down.

    Yelena sighs audibly. “I am not the only Black Widow eager to get her hands on the USB.”

    What?

    Kate’s eyebrows raise but she quickly furrows them to hide her reaction.

    Get your shit together-

    “Don't act like you're not surprised, I told you it doesn't look cool.”

    Kate shakes her head, the sides of her mouth pulling down. “I’m not surprised.” She blatantly ignores the scoff and glare of disbelief that Yelena gives her and clears her throat. “Explain,” she says, gesturing with her hand to tell her to ‘go on.’

    “The woman that we just fought with is a Black Widow,” Yelena says, moving past where Kate is sitting in her armchair and towards another room. “And she will not stop until she gets her hands on the USB and your laptop.”

    Black Widows. There are Black Widows after the information on the USB. Black Widows.

    What do we know about Black Widows? Think Kate. You have a big brain. The biggest of brains. You literally have a college degree, use it.

    Tilting her head back, she glares at the ceiling and tries remembering everything that Clint and Yelena have said about Black Widows. Clint hasn’t disclosed much information, which is very unhelpful and very frustrating considering he was best friends with one for years and should really impart his wisdom on his mentee. Yelena hasn’t been any better. She’s secretive and talks a lot about nothing, and Kate realizes that all of their conversations have been carefully orchestrated by the blonde so that she doesn’t have to say anything of importance.

    But sometimes , Kate thinks, she will drop bombs in conversations to throw me off-balance.

    Chemical subjugation.

    “She's being mind-controlled.”

    Yelena stops where she’s hauling a bag into the room. Her back is towards Kate, but the archer can see the tension in her shoulders. It makes her nervous because there are not many times where Yelena shows any type of reaction towards Kate’s comments. And even though she is currently very, very mad at Yelena right now, the last thing she wants to do is do anything to make her uncomfortable.

    Still, she tentatively continues and says, “you want to save her.”

    The blonde startles into motion and Kate jumps in her seat, scared for her life. Yelena puts Kate’s trick arrows into her backpack. There’s a harsh frown on her face.

    Kate’s coping mechanisms kick in. “I thought acting like you're not surprised wasn't cool?” She jokes, smiling hesitantly.

    Yelena’s eyes snap to her. “This is serious.”

    “I'm being very serious,” Kate shoots back immediately, her smile falling off when she sees it’s not welcome.

    “John Earl is not a good man.”

    “He seems pretty harmless to me.”

    Yelena deadpans her. “This is why I didn't want you to get involved.”

    “Good thing neither of us cares about what the other wants,” Kate says as she sinks further into the armchair. All she wants is to stick an icepack to her face where Yelena had punched her earlier on in the day and sleep until the headache pounding in her head is gone.

    There’s a pause in their conversation as the blonde quietly mulls over her words. It’s awkward and tense and Kate wants to die. Yelena zips up her bag and makes a quiet noise.

    “Kate…”

    “So what?” Kate interrupts her because she can hear how charged Yelena’s voice is. “What do you wanna do now?”

    Their eyes meet. Yelena plays with her Widow Bites again, her face still stoic and unreadable. “We need to get to a safe place,” she says finally, her voice regaining its clinically. “The Widow is not the only one who will come.”

    Groaning as she pushes herself up from the armchair, Kate grabs her duffel bag and slips the small straps back over her shoulders. Amusement colors Yelena’s expression as her viridescent eyes dance up and down Kate’s appearance, stalling slightly longer at the way she has equipped her bag. “There are three files in there.” Kate gestures vaguely to her bag and begins to walk over to the front door.

    “You hacked it?”

    “Of course,” Kate says, rolling her eyes.

    Yelena makes a face. The corners of her mouth twist downward and her eyebrows raise.

    She’s impressed.

    Kate tries very hard not to preen at that information, though she can’t help the surge of adrenaline the confidence boost gives her. She folds her arms over her chest and tries frowning instead, going for casual as she asks, “so why is the information so important?”

    “One of the files in there is a passcode to dangerous software,” Yelena says. She pulls her own bag over her shoulders and walks towards Kate with that confidence that makes Kate’s heart skip a little.

    “How dangerous?”

    Scrunching her nose, Yelena tilts her head from side to side. “Let’s just say that we don’t want anybody to get their hands on the programming to a Black Widow’s mind control.” As she comes to stand in front of Kate, she runs her eyes up and down her body, leaving a trail of blazing fire everywhere they touch. Her hands reach out to Kate’s high waist pants and quickly tug at the belt there to retrieve the gun that Kate had stolen from Yelena earlier.

    “Is that what this is? Why would Earl want that?” Kate immediately catches the ‘really bitch?’ look Yelena gives her and rolls her eyes. “Okay, that was a stupid question…”

    Yelena considers her for a second. There’s a slow smile forming on her lips that Kate finds herself watching raptly, though she’s pulled out of her stupor when they begin to move. “We need to go.” The blonde sidesteps her and begins to make a move for the door. “We can’t stay in the same place too long.”

    As her hand stretches out for the handle, a loud ‘crack’ ruptures the silence and the door flies open.

    Impulsively, Kate grabs the back of Yelena’s scabbard and pulls her back from the door. They both see a large, bulky shadow in the doorway and what looks like two large rifles in either hand before they’re disappearing behind the wall that separates the kitchen from the front door. The deafening stampede of bullets stops the question that Kate wants to ask Yelena.

    Two perfectly straight lines of holes decimate the perfectly wooden floorboards, and Kate winces because she had to admit that the apartment Yelena has been squatting in had really good decor.

    Yelena slides out from where Kate has pinned her against the wall and puts herself between her and the living room. “What did I say…” she grumbles.

    Kate glances at the smaller woman standing protectively in front of her. “Congrats on calling it sunshine, do you want a sticker?” She jokes as she unhooks her bow from over her head, intending to use it as a bat.

    Out of the kindness of her heart, Yelena shoots out two warning shots. It sends their intruder reeling back around the corner for cover.

    “You could have knocked,” Kate calls out to them. Yelena glares at her over her shoulder, the annoyance clear in the way her eyebrows raise in a ‘really? You’re doing this now?’ way.

    Kate ignores her, because she’s being a party-pooper, and walks toward the doorway of the kitchen. “It's rude to just barge in.”

    At the sound of her voice getting closer, the figure rounds the corner and points the rifles at her. “Give me the laptop, little girl,” he snarls beneath his mask.

    “I am a grown woman.”

    Kate swings the bow at his head as hard as she can, successfully landing the hit across his cheek. She drops to her knees, gritting her teeth at the pain that shoots up her left leg on impact. The gunshots miss her by mere inches, her strands of hair at her ponytail pulling tightly as bullets swing by. She swings the bow at his knees this time, grimacing when she hears a disturbing crunch. A shout of pain lets her know that the intruder’s knee has broken.

    He collapses to the floor but tries to aim at her again. Kate hooks her bow around the rifle and pulls, effectively tearing the weapon from his hand. It scatters across the kitchen tiles.

    A gunshot rings out as he shoots at her. It misses, instead piercing the ceiling and sending plaster snowing down on her. She kicks him in the face, praying that he passes out from it. He doesn’t, but his arm slumps to the floor along with his rifle and he mumbles something about how he’s going to destroy her when he gets the chance.

    “ Kate ,” Yelena calls from where she has managed to swing a makeshift rope outside of the kitchen window.

    “Coming!” Kate looks back down at the guy and kicks the rifle still in his hand away from him. “For your sake, I wouldn't tell your buddies you got taken down by a little girl.”

    -

    They leave Miami on Yelena’s motorbike and drive for three hours. The roar of the engine quiets as Yelena stops them in the back of the parking lot of a warehouse beside the wire fence, the chorus of cicadas ringing out to replace the mechanical grumble. Purple taints the sky, the sun having set only minutes before as Yelena drove them farther up the Floridian coast, and it casts a cool hue on the blonde’s demeanor.

    Kate allows the tension to leave her shoulders temporarily, leaning into the false sense of security for a second.

    From the moment that Kate woke up from the dreamless sleep that still unsettles her to her very core, she has been on autopilot. Her mind has been focused on getting back the USB, trying to crack the mystery, and getting away from people chasing after the USB. Even when she was talking to Clint on the phone, she hadn’t felt like she could let up. She hasn’t had a moment to just… breathe.

    It’s been a long day.

    Judging by the heavy silence between them and Yelena’s lack of movement, it seems like she feels the same way.

    Her arms are still wrapped around Yelena’s waist, but it feels like if she moves now then everything is going to crash down back on her shoulders and she won’t be able to breathe again. Besides, Yelena isn’t moving either.

    There’s a Black Widow after this and Yelena is trying to save her. Maybe…

    Kate’s chest aches and she pushes that line of thought away.

    She focuses on the rise and fall of Yelena’s chest. How her ribs expand against the skin of her arms, and how if she blocks out the ringing of the cicadas then she can hear the small exhales. Warmth radiates from Yelena’s back despite the cooling temperatures of the night, softening into Kate’s front in a way that both burns and comforts at the same time. Vanilla and orchids and clean shampoo overwhelm her senses, the prior scent of gasoline and wet summer heat melting into the background. The palms of her hands press against Yelena’s stomach, the flesh there soft as the blonde allows the tension to leave her body.

    It doesn’t feel like a hug, but in the back of Kate’s head, she is hyper-aware that the prolonged contact isn’t normal. It can’t be pushed under the rug as anything else.

    Sighing, Kate forces her arms away from Yelena’s waist.

    At her movement, Yelena huffs out a breath and dismounts the bike. “I need more of the counter-agent,” Yelena says, voice hoarse.

    “Did Bezos manage to get production started?” Kate says, sliding off the bike. The call back to their first conversation back in June makes her head spin a little. Kate feels the ache tighten her muscles in her body as she moves for the first time in three hours, today’s exercise managing to catch up to her.

    Yelena rolls her eyes as she tugs at the wire fence beside them. “Ha, ha.”

    Once Kate is off the bike, Yelena begins to walk away from the parking lot and towards a car rental place beside the warehouse. Kate takes the hint and follows her, fixing the straps of the duffel bag digging into her shoulders. “Where are you gonna get more?”

    “Ohio.”

    Briefly checking that Yelena is being serious, Kate laughs. “And how are we getting to Ohio?”

    Amused, Yelena smirks a little and shrugs. “We have a car.”

    “We do?”

    The blonde approaches one of the many cars parked in the rental place - a modest, dark red Toyota Prius that looks a little grimy and covered in dirt - with a piece of wire from the fence. Kate anticipates what Yelena is going to do and keeps an eye on the main building. The lights are turned off. It doesn’t look like there’s anyone around, but Kate still ensures that nobody is going to jump out at them. There’s a soft ‘clacking’ sound and the archer glances back at Yelena, who grins up at her cockily.

    “Now we do,” she says, the corners of her lips pulling up into a confident smirk that has heat crawling on Kate’s cheeks.

    “You have a bad habit of stealing cars.” Kate rolls her eyes and tries to pretend like she isn’t blushing.

    Yelena tugs the door open and slides into the car, reaching for the sun visor and catching the car keys that drop. Kate raises her eyebrows. The assassin winks. “Get in,” she says, voice light and joking and it makes Kate want to joke back.

    But she doesn’t.

    “I hope you know that it’s gonna take us at least an entire day to get up to Ohio,” she says when she gets in, pushing the duffel bag between the two front seats and into the back where Yelena has put her backpack. She’s relieved to lean back against the seat, her back begging for support after the long motorbike ride. It makes the faint ache at her face entirely more noticeable.

    “That’s okay,” Yelena says, bringing the engine to life. “I heard a little bird got her licence.”

    Glaring, Kate buckles her seatbelt in and pulls back the recliner of her seat a little. Her legs are too cramped. “So first you steal from me, and now you force me to drive for hours?”

    “You want me to knock you out and leave you here?” Yelena swings her hand to Kate’s backrest and twists around to look behind them as she backs up the car to pull out of the parking spot. The green in her eyes glints as the last traces of sunlight catches in them. Her eyelashes cast blue shadows on her cheekbones, contrasting spectacularly with the honey blonde strands of hair.

    She’s gorgeous and we hate her but she’s so pretty and likeable but we can’t trust her.

    Kate moves her eyes away from the visage beside her and slumps down in her seat. A part of her wishes that she could just forgive Yelena for stealing from her because she has good reasoning, but the ache in her chest is still there and it’s still making it hard to exist.

    “You’re a bitch,” is what she says instead.

    Yelena hums as she pulls them back onto the road. “You still like me.” Her voice is soft and knowing and it endears Kate.

    “Clearly.” Even though there’s bitterness in her mouth, she can’t stop the sweet affection in her tone as she says, “otherwise, I would have punched you in the face by now.”

    “Good to know,” Yelena breathes.

    -

    “Yelena.”

    “Hm.”

    “That Range Rover.”

    Both of their eyes fly to the rearview mirror. A black Range Rover drives in the same lane as them, peeking only slightly above the roof of a silver BMW. “I see it.”

    “It’s been following us since we got on the highway,” Kate says quietly.

    They haven’t said a word since Yelena started driving two hours ago. The silence permeating the air between them is too thick to even think about starting a conversation - not that Kate is in the mood to even attempt to hash out their differences.

    “Range Rovers are bad guys’ cars, right?”

    Yelena makes a sound that’s a half-scoff, half-laugh. She glances at her from the corner of her eye, blonde hair falling in her face, her eyebrows pulled together in confusion. “I don’t think ‘bad guys’ discriminate against car brands…” Mirth drips from her voice, and even if Kate is doing her very best at keeping her eyes off Yelena, she can still hear the smile forming around her words.

    “Okay, but if I were a bad guy, I would so choose a Range Rover.” Kate twists in her seat to look at their stalkers better. “Big, hunky cars. Good for off-roading.” She squints at the blacked-out glass. “Those windows are probably bulletproof.”

    Humming, Yelena glances at the rearview mirror again and considers the car. Her gloved hands twist around the steering wheel and her lips purse.

    Kate perks up in her seat, a small smile beginning to spread on her lips. “We’re gonna do a car chase, aren’t we?”

    “Do not sound so excited,” Yelena scowls.

    Ignoring the pessimist beside her, Kate loosens her seatbelt and leans into the back. “I haven’t done one of these in a while,” she groans as her fingertips brush against the rough fabric of the straps of her duffel bag. She strains again and flinches when the pain shoots up her side. When she finally manages to pull her bag closer and unzip it, she lets out a victorious laugh. “Have to say,” she says as she leans back into her seat, this time with her bag in her lap. “There’s a strange thrill to them.”

    “What are you doing?” Yelena glances at her, exasperation in her voice.

    “You’re gonna need a distraction, right?”

    “You’re still injured.”

    Kate pulls out her bow and quiver. “Hasn’t stopped me before.”

    Yelena snaps her head to look at her, eyes wide with warning. “Keep your seatbelt on,” she warns, her gloved hand wrapping around Kate’s forearm as the archer goes to unbuckle her seatbelt.

    “I got this.” Kate unbuckles her seatbelt anyway and begins to pick out another putty arrow. She only has two left. I need to get more trick arrows. (After the success of knocking Yelena out, she thinks that the putty arrow has jumped up several spots on her trick arrow rank list.)

    “Zhizn’ ebet meya …” Rolling the window down, Kate begins to pull herself out of her seat. “Hold on.” Yelena steps on the accelerator and swerves the car into the fast lane.

    “Okay, they’re definitely following us,” Kate says, watching raptly as the Range Rover pulls out from behind the silver car it was attempting to hide behind and gives chase.

    She tightens her grip on her bow and holds onto the door of the car as Yelena begins to push the speed over the speed limits. Her heart is pounding in her chest, the adrenaline shooting sparks to her fingertips. The blonde looks determined to lose them. Kate thinks it’s so that she doesn’t get the chance to hoist herself out of the car and put herself in danger, but whoever is driving the Range Rover is dead set on chasing them. They’re dodging and weaving between cars on the highway with their stalkers close on their tail.

    “I’m gonna fire an arrow at them.”

    “And how are you gonna do that?” Yelena drawls, visibly agitated as she repeatedly glances behind them.

    Kate is already pulling herself out of the car as Yelena tears ahead. “Try not to swerve the car too hard,” she calls inside.

    Cool wind whips into her hair. Kate is fortunate that she is wearing a tight shirt; the hair from her ponytail flying into her face from behind her is already distracting enough. Her abdomen tightens as she hooks her feet to the bottom of her seat and does her best to hold on. The car is moving considerably faster than when Clint had been driving it, but the adrenaline is clouding her judgement and there’s a strange, burning necessity inside of her to prove her worth.

    With the putty arrow nocked, Kate trains it to the windshield of the Range Rover gaining speed behind them. She sees her reflection in the glass - her silhouette painted against the fluorescent white lights and the black night sky - and stalls.

    There are people in that car. Human beings.

    The windows of the Rover begin to roll down. A glinting of metal lets Kate know that the people in that car are armed and ready to take their lives if they need to.

    Kate expels all of the air in her lungs and lets the arrow fly with the last breaths of oxygen.

    Purple chemical bursts on the windshield and the Rover abruptly begins to slow down. Relief soothes her nerves. Yelena shouts something incorrigible from inside the car and Kate slaps her hand against the roof of the car as it jerks. Her heart drops out of her chest. Over the deafening beating of the wind at her ears, Kate hears the screech of brakes and the conflict of tires against concrete as the Rover begins to spin out and Yelena overtakes an unsuspecting blue car.

    However, as soon as the Rover comes to a stop in the middle of the highway, two more swerve around it and continue the chase.

    Crap - what the hell?

    Kate grits her teeth and leans down into the opening of the car. “There’s more of them,” she shouts over the wind.

    Yelena looks at her with panicked eyes. It’s then that Kate notices the pressure at her calf where the blonde has her hand tightly gripping her leg, the other doing its best to manoeuvre them around other civilians. “Get back in,” Yelena calls, tugging at the denim of her pants. “We’re going to lose them.”

    Reluctantly, Kate drops back into the passenger seat and rolls the window back up. Moving her jaw from side to side, Kate tries to pop her ears and get rid of the muffled static blocking her hearing.

    The Black Widow leans over her, expertly keeping the steering wheel still with the tips of her fingers as she tugs Kate’s seatbelt and pulls it over her. Kate takes the seatbelt from her hand, rolling her eyes at the unnecessary gesture that could have been saved if only the blonde used her words, but decides to say nothing. She buckles herself in and ignores the swooping feeling in her stomach as Yelena leans back in the driver’s seat.

    It’s the acceleration, she tells herself. Not the warm, sweet, floral scent.

    Yelena jerks the car and Kate’s heart comes to a stop. The latter’s eyes widen as the blonde charges full speed ahead. Holding her breath, Kate digs her fingernails into the palms of her hands and watches as Yelena just narrowly slips their car between a pickup truck and a large moving truck.

    They leave behind a cacophony of car horns as the Rovers struggle to dodge the two large vehicles.

    Holy shit. What the hell. Oh my god.

    Heat crashes down on her body and renders her speechless. Yelena is shouting victoriously in Russian, whipping her head back to watch their chasers struggle around the two confused vehicles swerving in their lanes. Her voice is loud in the car, complete with a joyous laugh that has Kate’s heart beating wildly. Her green eyes are bright with child-like excitement and the grin on her face is wide and toothy and her gums are on full display and Kate feels like she’s being pushed off a precipice. There’s another swooping feeling that has her breath catching in her throat and the moisture in her mouth drying up.

    It feels a lot like falling.

    Fuck. What was that?

    She can’t decide whether she’s scared for her life because of Yelena’s insane driving, or if she’s beyond turned on by the rush of adrenaline and showcase of skill.

    The blonde turns back around and presses down on the accelerator again, a passionate spark now alight in her eyes.

    They pull off of the highway as they near Jacksonville, darkness enshrouding them as the streets begin to enter the residential areas of the suburbs. Kate’s body is shaking with the confusing blend of emotions raging on inside of her, her hands trembling as she grips an arrow.

    “We should get to cover and swap cars,” Yelena says finally, her voice holding that steady confidence that makes Kate feel like everything is going to be okay.

    The archer grits her teeth and chances a glance in her direction. The rise and fall of her chest have slowed down. Her breathing has returned to a steady pace again and the ardent passion has lulled into what looks like content satisfaction. There are blonde strands of hair falling into her face and pressing to the reddish bruise on her skin. Yelena looks beaten up, with the cut on her forehead only just now beginning to scab over. Kate’s eyes follow the slope of her nose and stop at her mouth. Her lips are pink and glistening and begging to be bitten.

    I can’t trust her. I can’t trust her. I can’t trust her.

    Kate swallows the gnawing desire to press their lips together steal the oxygen from Yelena’s lungs and instead focuses on the mission.

    -

    For the past three hours, since they left Jacksonville, Kate has been pinching her thigh every minute to make sure she stays awake. The drowsiness that has been pulling her to sleep hasn’t gotten the better of her yet, but Kate’s head has been dropping every time she lets her guard down. Sleeping is the last thing that she wants to do right now.

    Infuriatingly, Yelena looks unbothered. Her eyes have been trained on the road with complete laser focus like they both haven’t been awake since at least six in the morning when Kate ambushed the apartment. Guilt stings at Kate as she takes in the angry red bruise spreading on her cheek from where Kate had headbutted her and smacked her with her bow. On her forearm is the faint reddish marks of teeth. Even with all of her injuries though, Yelena is perfectly awake and conscious and looks like she has been sleeping better than Kate has.

    By the time Kate processes that she and Yelena are teaming up, they’re too far from Miami for her to backtrack and change her mind.

    Since Kate got her hands on the USB she has been attacked four times (though Yelena didn’t physically attack her, she did emotionally and that’s somehow worse).

    She is very aware that the only reason she was able to get the USB back from the blonde is that she was going easy on her. Again. (Which is both condescending and touching and Kate is upset for wildly different reasons because of it.) Her attacks and movements had been non- lethal. There was an obvious worry for her safety when she had started bleeding from her wound, which confirmed that Yelena was doing her best to avoid her injuries. The exact moment that Yelena underestimated her, Kate took advantage and used the trick arrow.

    She doubts that the other Black Widow, who is under mind-control, will have the same mercy.

    Yelena’s experience and skill overshadow hers, and Kate doesn’t know the precise details of what a ‘counter-agent’ is but if the assassin is willing to drive them across the country then she has a feeling that it’s something that will help them defeat the Black Widow.

    So Kate stays.

    She stays and tries not to fall asleep, because if she does, then Yelena might take the USB and laptop and betray her like she did last time.

    “You should sleep.” Yelena’s voice is low, her accent thick.

    Kate tenses, blinking herself back into consciousness.

    “Your body will heal quicker if you rest,” she continues just as softly.

    “I’m fine.”

    “Kate.”

    “I’m not sleeping.” She briefly glares at the blonde, silently warning her away. She doesn’t stop the animosity from revealing itself in her tone. “If you want to sleep, pull over and I’ll drive.”

    There’s a small squeak of leather against leather as Yelena’s gloved grip tightens around the steering wheel of the Nissan. After spending all day together, it’s easier to read the other woman’s body language and notice when and how she reacts. Kate can feel the tension emanating from her body, the tightness of her jaw and the cloudy filter over her eyes suggesting that she is deep in thought.

    “You’re mad at me,” Yelena says. Her voice is quiet, and it’s said as a statement though Kate can hear the slight lilt at the end of the sentence that makes it sound like it’s a question.

    She doesn’t respond. Doesn’t think that she can or that she should, because if Yelena doesn’t know that what she did hurt her this much, then that means that whatever feelings of trust - or at least friendship - she had held towards her had been one-sided. And that’s just…

    embarrassing. And really crappy.

    Kate is still hurting, and the unpleasant ache is still there.

    So she grits her teeth and clenches her fists to keep all of the emotions compartmentalized. Bottle them up and shove them down.

    Focus on the mission, Kate.

    “You were shot.” Yelena’s voice is less unsure now. “Twice.” There’s conviction and slight anger now as her voice raises. “You were going to die.”

    Yeah… not one of my best moments. The embarrassment begins to crawl back across her body. Kate slumps into her seat and begs the universe to swallow her up and hide her from existence.

    “Why didn’t you heal yourself?” Kate can feel the glare that Yelena shoots her as the car speeds up a little. “I know you’re smarter than that.”

    “Why didn’t you let me die?” She shoots back.

    At the silence, Kate turns in her seat to look at the assassin.

    Their eyes meet and her breath catches at the storm swirling in the dark emerald boring into her. The muscle in her jaw jumps beneath her skin.

    Yelena looks away with her frown pulling down on the corners of her mouth and that cloudy filter back over her eyes. And the frustration begins to bubble up again, because why is it so hard for the assassin to answer that one question? It’s not like she’s asking her to reveal her life story, or asking her to confess her deepest and darkest secrets. If anything, this should be easy for her to answer: a simple calculated decision between helping her or letting her die.

    But no. No. Yelena refuses to answer and it makes her mad because is it because she doesn’t want to admit that she cares? Does she care? Does she not want to care? And if she doesn’t want to care what does that mean?

    Why do I even care?

    Exasperated, Kate groans and presses her fingers into her eyes.

    There’s an audible sigh that exudes frustration and the fact that they’re both so emotionally inept makes her feel a little better. “I did not mean to hurt you,” Yelena grumbles. Very reluctantly, Kate muses. But she says it nonetheless. “I had a mission to complete.”

    Kate’s chest aches.

    “I didn’t know that you had taken this case.”

    “What a strange coincidence that we took the same case,” Kate snides, “in the same location.”

    “For a moment I thought you were following me.”

    And what?

    “Is that what you think of me?” Kate can’t help the rise in her voice as she spins on Yelena. “That I would follow you all the way to Miami ?” The anger is red-hot and blinding. “Is that how pathetic you think I am?”

    There’s another frown on her face, and she looks a little like a wronged puppy. “No.” Yelena glances at her with her eyebrows furrowed. “No, that is not what I meant.”

    “The only reason that I’m going with you to Ohio is that I need that counter-agent.” Kate can’t stop the words from falling out. The spite and bitterness lace her words and she admits that she’s speaking with the intention to hurt - if Yelena even cares enough to get hurt. “After this, I don’t want to see you show up to my apartment again.” Tears prick her eyes at her own words, and her voice cracks as she says, “I’m tired of your games.”

    The silence afterwards is charged - surprisingly with more pain than Kate had bargained for.

    This feels like shit. Kate clenches her eyes shut. We are never doing this again. Nope. Nuh-uh. Note for Future Kate: don’t trust Russian assassins. Not without scoping out the field first… maybe Clint is right sometimes.

    Oh god, Clint.

    Kate pulls her phone from her duffel bag for the first time since she was ambushed at the motel and sends Clint a couple of text messages to let him know that she’s okay. She sees that both Spiderman and Nate have texted her too with a flurry of memes and gifs respectively, and it staves off the scorching emotions begging her to just open the car door and throw herself out.

    A little melodramatic but it would send the message.

    She stares at the picture of Lucky that Grills had sent her and feels a sweet smile bitterly spread on her face, tracing her thumb over his dumb, derpy face. There’s an empty feeling in her chest as she registers how long it’s been since she’s felt the warmth of her best friend curled up against her. All she wants to do is get the mission done and get back home to Lucky and her apartment.

    No more gunshot wounds.

    No more Yelena.

    “Kate…” Yelena’s voice is hoarse and gravelly, and Kate can feel her pain.

    Sighing, Kate looks at her.

    The blonde bites her lip. “Please sleep,” she says quietly.

    They don’t say anything after that. Kate thinks about it for forty-five minutes, doing her best to fight off the fatigue begging to pull her into unconsciousness. At this point, the ache in her body has melted into a nice pulse that feels both painful and strangely comforting.

    Eventually, though, her eyes water from trying to keep them open for so long. Yelena has soft music playing in the background, somehow having found a radio station that plays strictly indie songs.

    So with reluctance, Kate slips into a restless sleep.

    “Hawkeye,” Kate rasps, wiping her face from sleep.

    “I have something.”

    She perks up at that. “What is it?”

    “Earl’s daughter.” She hums. “She was murdered two months ago and the media isn’t talking.” Her breath catches. “They’re keeping it quiet so only close family relatives know.” Confusion twists inside of her. “Something about being caught in the crossfire of Earl’s shady business and her being collateral-”

    And if that doesn’t trigger some unresolved emotions.

    “-bad for their reputation, those bastards.”

    “What?” Kate says it even though she hears him perfectly fine. It’s a weak attempt to try to process the onslaught of images of blue and red lights and the blurry disappointed expression on her mother’s face. An attempt to get her bearings right. She clears her throat. “He didn’t look like a guy whose daughter just died.”

    Clint, ever so eloquent, grunts like a caveman. It does a pretty good job of soothing her. “Powerful people know how to play the part,” he says off-handedly.

Do you think Earl wants to use the Black Widow to get revenge for his daughter?”

There’s…” The faint squeak lets Kate know that the man is sitting in his leather armchair (positioned carefully to face the other couches and inconveniently away from the TV). “It’s a lot more complicated than just being careful and not trusting her.”

    “Whatever.”

In fact-” His voice picks up in volume. “If anything, I think you needed it.”
