    “Ha ha ha.” The dryness in her voice does nothing to keep the mild amusement from swirling in her chest pleasantly. “You are not that funny old man.”

    “I can put Nate on the phone and he’ll tell you otherwise.”

    Kate’s mind wanders. She thinks about how different her life is now.

    This time last year she was enjoying her summer vacation like she had nothing else to live for, which means stupid parties that ended up with her drunk off her ass and with the occasional one night stand that Greer and Franny chased out the next day. Back then she still had no idea where her life was headed. Especially when her mother was training her to take over Bishop Security and insisting that her childhood dream was completely unattainable.

    Look at that Mom, Kate thinks as she listens to the quiet sound of Clint fumbling with something on the other end of the line, I know Hawkeye’s family better than I know you, apparently.

    That thought stings particularly bad.

    There was no need. Absolutely no need.

    Clint must sense her spiral because he makes a soft noise before he speaks. It pulls her out of her reverie. “What’s up?”

    Kate tugs her lower lip between her teeth and frowns. There are too many answers to that question. Her mouth opens to say some of them, but nothing comes out. “Ugh… I just-” She frowns harder. “It’s not like you get a ‘mental health’ course when you become a hero.”

    He laughs dryly. “It kinda sucks, right?”

    “Ha…” Kate thinks back to his speech about being a ‘hero’ and its repercussions. His vehement refusal at the idea of one of his children following in his footsteps in a way too similar to Scott Lang’s protectiveness over Cassie. Bitterness stains her tongue. “‘Kinda’? My mom is in jail and I have people trying to kill me left and right.” A flash of gold and emerald has her saying, “Also, having a Black Widow play with your head isn’t the best feeling in the world.”

    “Welcome to the life of a Hawkeye.”

    Yelena is possibly the most confusing human being that Kate has ever encountered.

    Kate’s intuition is usually immaculate. She’s good at taking a look at someone, getting a general vibe of what they’re like, and then judging whether she wants to continue interacting with them or forget about their existence.

    But the blonde messes with her intuition. Kate knows that Yelena is dangerous. The assassin knows more than two hundred ways to kill a human being and she isn’t afraid to use them.

    The knowledge alone should be enough to scare her away, maybe even make her dislike Yelena. (It doesn’t. A messed up part of Kate finds it attractive.) But her incredibly attractive quick wit and her approachable, down-to-earth demeanor makes it incredibly difficult for Kate to rationalize any kind of dislike towards the blonde.

    If anything, Kate’s intuition tells her that she and Yelena are infuriatingly compatible. On a level that she doesn’t think she has ever experienced with anyone else.

    It’s frustrating when she is aware of this - when she desperately wants to know what it would be like to explore that dynamic with Yelena - but she can still feel the gaping chasm in her chest formed by her betrayal.

    “She’s so complicated,” Kate grumbles broodingly.

    Clint, who has allowed her the space to be lost in her thoughts, hums. “It runs in the family,” he says tersely.

    “Hm?”

    “Come on,” he says, voice heavy, “you think it was easy with Nat?”

    Kate’s eyebrows raise.

    “That little nightmare double-crossed me so many times I’m surprised I ever learned to trust her.” His sharp, annoyed tone is oozing with affection that Kate has come to associate with Clint showing his love in his own way. It makes her already aching chest churn and twist even more as she finds kinship in the mourning undertones.

    “Even in the end she…”

    His voice catches.

    “She…”

    Kate’s heart breaks for him.

    “Well.”

    Clint doesn’t talk about Natasha a lot. Kate doesn’t blame him. She tries not to press for more than what he gives her. She listens to the rare moments he voices her name and the reverence in which he does so. She’s familiar with the grief that clings to names of people that passed away too soon - too abruptly - and is even more familiar with the silent mourning that comes from it.

    She wonders how Clint had gone from trying to kill Natasha to speaking of her in a way that made Kate miss a woman she had never met before.

    Kate recalls, then, that Clint has experience when it comes to Black Widows and their many, many dichotomies. The danger and death and blood that cling to them but the life and light and yearning for good that washes all of that away. She knows that Natasha Romanoff yearned for good because she had dedicated her life to the Avengers with a vehement determination that had made Kate even more dead-set in her goals to follow in her heroes'

    footsteps.

    Kate has seen the candid shots of Natasha on the battlefield. There are many moments, even in conversation, where Yelena reminds her of that.

    But Yelena is not Natasha, and Kate isn’t Clint.

    “You two were family, though.” Chewing her lip, Kate glances to the gas station where Yelena had wordlessly disappeared into.

    Clint huffs on the other end of the line. “It took us a while to get there,” he says reverently. “A lot of bumps in the road.”

    This is one of those moments where Kate has to hold herself back from asking too many prying questions. Recently, these moments bring the hope that maybe one day she and Yelena will be friends. Partners. That she will be able to look at the Black Widow and know that she will have her back, and vice versa.

    The hope endears the side of Kate that longs for human connection, but petrifies the side of Kate reminding her that Yelena can’t be trusted.

    As if hearing her thoughts, Clint says, “Look kid, I’m not saying you should… y’know, trust Yelena.” He laughs gruffly. “God no, that is the last thing you should do right now unless you wanna end up with a scar the size of your hand.”

    Eyebrows raised, Kate glances at her phone in amusement. “Clint, I’m starting to think you’re keeping details of the Black Widow section in the Hawkeye Protocol from me…”

    “Ah,” he says, mirth and nostalgia in his voice, “you have no idea, kid. Just…” He clears his throat, giving way to an awkward tone that reminds Kate of just how uncomfortable he is with showing his affection. “Don’t be too hard on her, okay?”

    Kate glances at the gas station again.

    She thinks about the harsh words she had spat at Yelena last night and guilt immediately begins to swell up inside of her. The surprisingly vulnerable expression that the woman had made had stung because in her anger Kate had hurt Yelena. (Had hurt them both.) And even though that had been her intention at the time, she regretted it the moment she calmed down. Truthfully, she hadn’t expected to be successful. Yelena always looks so nonchalant and detached, so Kate has never considered the possibility that she is prone to emotion just like she is.

    It doesn’t make her any less emotionally unavailable.

    And that’s a string of thoughts that has been passing through the back of her mind for a while now.

    Kate may have been surprised by the intense attraction that is apparently guaranteed when it comes to interacting with Yelena, though she has accepted it for what it is. However, after Yelena left her in the motel room and it had hurt that much…

    Yeah, sure Kate, great idea. Let's catch feelings for someone who will probably never reciprocate them.

    Clint proceeds to confirm just how unattainable Yelena is and Kate has to push herself to cling to his words for comfort. “Black Widows, they’re… they don’t trust easy.” Kate grunts a little to let him know that she’s listening, but also because it hurts a little. “They like to think that they’re built to operate on their own. At least at first…”

    Kate thinks about Natasha and the Avengers.

    “Something about being better as an individual than as a team- I don’t know, I always hated that speech. Whatever, what I’m trying to say is-”

    She smiles a little.

    “It’s all bullshit. Crap the Red Room taught them.”

    Ah, yes. The Red Room.

    A surge of bitterness and fury swings full force within her. It’s born from an unwelcome protectiveness when she thinks about an eleven-year-old Yelena scrambling to stitch her body back together; when she thinks about the conflict swirling behind Yelena’s eyes as she decides between doing what she thinks is right and what she has been taught is right. It shouldn’t be that hard to choose, Kate thinks. Yelena should be free from the shackles weighing down her mind.

    “And I guess that as Hawkeyes…” Clint continues. “Well, the best we can do is keep our cool.”

    “Keep our cool?”

    “Give her the space that she needs so that she comes to you when she’s ready.”

    “That sounds like psychological suicide,” she says, thinking back to the pain she felt.

    “I don’t know, Kate.” Clint’s voice is doing that thing where he speaks like he’s imparting wisdom on his student. It makes her smile at the knowing tone because that’s what she is to him. “You’re strong. Stronger than you think.”

    The words are slightly disarming, but they help Kate’s anger dissipate. She knows that Clint says them not because he’s trying to reassure her or manipulate her into feeling better about herself, but because he thinks they’re true. The thought is reassuring, especially after the horrible experience of seeing her confidence fragment right before her eyes. Relief soothes the insecurities festering inside.

    Sighing, Kate says, “so Hawkeye Protocol says I should expect to get double-crossed?”

    Clint hums. “Hawkeye Protocol says don’t be surprised if you do get double-crossed.”

    “Well,” Kate huffs, rubbing a hand over her eyes, “fudge nuggets.”

    A gravelly laugh comes through the phone, which vibrates in her hand as a result. Kate sinks into the comfort that it provides.

    Her voice is wistful and quiet as she asks, “Is it even worth it?”

    “I mean…” Clint clears his throat. “Nat- she-”

    Recognizing that Clint can’t get the words out, Kate hums. “I know.”

    He’s quiet for a few moments like he usually gets after mentioning Natasha. “I don’t know a lot about Yelena, but-” Kate smiles when she hears the inhale and pictures him make that emotionally constipated expression that amuses her every time. “If she is anything like her sister, then she has a saviour complex bigger than all of the Avengers put together. And sometimes,” he says, speaking in a tone that lets Kate know that this isn’t just about one sister, “that saviour complex is going to make her do stupid things like double-cross people she cares about just so that they’re safe.”

    She thinks about the countless people currently chasing them down for a simple USB. She thinks of the woman pointing the rifle at her face with murderous intent and Yelena flying in out of nowhere to fight her off. She thinks about Yelena’s warm hands on her body as she tended to her wounds.

    Kate may be more than willing to throw herself into the river of denial, but she isn’t dumb.

    “Fucking Black Widows,” she breathes, affection seeping into her voice as she watches Yelena exit the gas station finally. Their eyes meet as Yelena walks towards the car, and Kate feels the burst of butterflies in her stomach hit her.

    Oblivious to Kate’s inner turmoil, Clint chuckles quietly. “You’re telling me.”

    -

    “Let me help,” is the first thing Yelena says to her since last night. She says it like an order, but the expression on her face is wary. Her appearance is mellowed out by the small wisps of sunlight that peek through the leaves of the tree the car is parked under, her hair illuminated a soft gold. Kate’s heart clenches. Yelena takes her silence as refusal, and her tone mixes with concern as she says, “please.”

    Already endeared by Yelena’s gesture of getting her a Red Bull, Kate can do nothing but nod.

    (The blonde had dropped into the car with a plastic bag full of food, water, medical supplies and energy drinks, and had purposefully left the bag open so that Kate could see its contents. A Red Bull was lying on top of a Monster - which is probably Yelena’s preferred choice of energy drink.

    Kate had swooned so hard that she had to bite her lip to stop the smile from forming on her face. She looked at Yelena who was busying herself with the contents of her backpack. At that moment, Kate hadn’t been able to stop the grin on her face because Yelena looked so awkward while she was pretending that she wasn’t discreetly checking her reaction from her peripheries.)

    Yelena approaches her slowly, gesturing for her to lift her shirt up.

    The air is less humid here, and there’s a cool morning breeze that feels pleasant on her skin as she rolls her tight shirt up so that Yelena can tend to her wounds. She does her best to ignore the scrape of Yelena’s rough fingertips against her stomach, but she’s so drowsy that everything feels like it’s blurring together. Like how gentle Yelena’s hands are when she’s touching her, or the firm press of her thumb at her hip, or the cool blow on her stomach-

    Wait. What?

    Raising her eyebrows, Kate looks down at Yelena.

    Frowning, the blonde weaves a suture through the needle, unaware of Kate’s gaze currently watching her. Her hair is fixed on the back of her head in a bun, golden strands sticking out of it in a messy flurry, her fringe falling into her eyes. The bruise on her cheek is a dark purple and the cut on her forehead looks like it’s finally healing.

    Without a single thought in her mind, Kate reaches out and brushes the hair from Yelena’s forehead. Her fingers tickle on the blonde’s cheekbone as she does so.

    Yelena freezes.

    Crap.

    Green eyes meet hers and Kate feels her heart do a little dance that makes her want to combust.

    The amused grin that spreads on Yelena’s face is enough to have Kate feel like she’s sixteen with a stupid crush again. Which is incredibly infuriating because having feelings is not as easy as it used to be before Hawkeye stumbled into her life - or she had stumbled into Hawkeye’s life. She can’t just approach the person that she likes and ask them to go on a date with her. (A strategy that has yet to fail her.) Yelena isn’t a normal person - Yelena is complicated and confusing and dangerous. Dangerous not because Kate thinks that she is going to kill her (she hopes) but because she knows that there is a high chance that anything that happens between them will end up in heartbreak.

    We don’t need that. Nope. We’re barely hanging on as it is.

    Yelena laughs breathily, the sound melodious and unbearably pleasant on her ears.

    But she’s so cute-

    Nope. No. No, she’s not.

    Kate tries to steady her heart rate as Yelena gently brushes her thumb over the skin at her side.

    She’s also hot-

    Kate blushes and pointedly looks away.

    Oh my god- Okay. You’re on time out. No more talking.

    She clenches her fists as Yelena expertly tugs the clumsy stitches Kate had touched up yesterday out of her skin and begins to restitch them back together with a lot more skill than she had. Tears prick at her eyes from the pain, but she purses her lips and muffles her whimpers. Yelena is quick and efficient, and every once in a while she pauses and blows and it’s strangely comforting because her wound feels like it’s burning.

    Once Yelena is done, she reaches into the plastic bag and pulls out a clean bandage. Kate is very aware that her cheeks probably look blushed, but there’s nothing that she can do about that so she pretends like it’s not happening instead.

    “Take these,” the blonde says, handing her a strip of antibiotics.

    “Okay,” Kate exhales.

    When Yelena backs away, the archer finally feels like she can breathe again. There’s a gentle expression on her face that Kate reads as concern, and it’s frustratingly convincing. “Does it hurt?” She asks, green eyes dancing down her body.

    Shrugging, Kate rolls her shirt back down and pushes away from where she was leaning on the side of the car. “A little.” She makes a ‘what can I do’ face, eyebrows bouncing up and her mouth twisting to the side. “But ya know… we vibe.”

    Yelena’s eyes scrunch up with silent laughter, the amusement evident in the barely hidden smile tugging at her mouth. “It looks like it is healing well,” she says quietly, though there’s a melancholic tinge to her words. A reluctance to reach out and show anything more than cynicality. It reminds Kate of her harsh words from last night and it makes her cringe. “You have done a good job.”

    “Look, I don’t wanna…” The words catch in her throat. She takes a breath and reminds herself to think before she speaks, because she has a bad habit of just talking without a destination. “I just… I’m still hurt but-” Tentatively, Kate glances at Yelena and meets her eyes. “Thank you,” she says, trying to show her gratitude through a hesitant smile. “It wouldn’t be healing this good if you didn’t help me. So… thanks.” There’s an awkward pause after and she tacks on, “I guess.”

    Mirthful, Yelena’s eyebrows shoot up. “You guess?” Her voice is teasing.

    “Shut up,” she grumbles, lightly kicking at the blonde’s leg.

    Yelena dodges her kick and follows the movement of her foot to press down on her toes with the tip of her boot. The playful nature of it all makes Kate grin a little, and she glances at Yelena’s face and sees the unreadable twist of her lips.

    “Also,” she adds, “I know you think you’re super-human or whatever, but you need sleep too.”

    Rolling her eyes, Yelena backs away from her a little more and picks up the plastic bag from the dirt. “How flattering, little hawk.”

    A cool breeze brushes through the space between them, shaking up Kate’s hair around her from where it’s messily tied back in a ponytail. Similarly, the blonde strands of hair push into Yelena’s face, encouraging the woman to duck her head to keep the dust particles flying in the wind. Her nose scrunches from the discomfort and Kate has to force herself to look away because her chest convulses painfully.

    Humming quietly to distract herself, Kate desperately swims to keep herself above the wave of emotions that hits her.

    “We don’t want you passing out, do we?” She says playfully, trying to keep the overwhelming rush of affection from her voice. But Yelena must hear the soft edge to her tone and glances at her with wide, hopeful eyes. Guilt swirls in Kate’s chest when she registers the dark shadows pressing into the woman’s under eye. She looks exhausted now that the sun has peeked over the horizon.

    “No, we don’t,” Yelena says with an unreadable expression.

    “Okay.” She reaches her hand out, palm faced up, and wiggles her fingers in a ‘give me’

    motion. “I’m driving.”

    Yelena clicks her tongue and glares at her, but it lacks its usual bite. When they drop back into the car, the air between them feels clearer. Less weighty. Less painful.

    -

    The sun has set again when they arrive in Ohio, as has any traces of anger Kate has been holding onto.

    With the copious amount of time Kate has had to think as she drives them through both South and North Carolina, she has mulled over the situation long enough to have walked herself through her feelings three times. You can never be too sure, right?

    Forgiving Yelena isn’t the easiest thing in the world when in the back of her mind, there’s a warning voice telling her that Yelena is literally a professionally trained assassin that knows how to manipulate people into bending to her will. The voice sounds like Clint at first, but the more she hears it, the more it reminds her of her mother. Her mother’s calculating tone and her cunning eyes that made so many of her employees crumble at her feet.

    All in all, piecing everything altogether makes it easier to rationalize forgiving the assassin.

    She was trying to protect me, she thinks, glancing at the sleeping woman beside her. But is that because she thinks I’m incapable of taking care of myself? A frown overtakes her features.

    Her thought process continues like this for the rest of the journey.

    Music plays in the background as an attempt to keep her attention on the road and not anywhere else. Considering that she had only had two near-crashes that had barely jolted the sleeping woman in the passenger’s seat, Kate would consider the drive her most successful one yet. The added stress of driving as smoothly as possible to allow Yelena a good sleep has her eyes straining and watering by the time they swap over in Virginia, at which the blonde makes a few sarcastic quips. She staves the soreness off by sleeping a little more.

    The continuous relaying of sleeping and driving means that by the time they make it into Ohio, the only conversation they have exchanged has been about whether the group of men sitting outside the small bar they stopped at in West Virginia were looking at them because they wanted to kill them, or if they were just looking to hit on them.

    As soon as one of them approached Kate with two drinks in his hands, and a terrible opening one-liner, they concluded that it was the latter. Yelena had laughed freely as Kate gave the guy a ‘hard-pass’, sitting back to watch everything go down with curiosity and amusement clear in the glint in her eyes. Later, when they had sat back down in the car to eat their food, Kate had shoved at Yelena’s shoulder out of pure annoyance but had offered an amicable grin.

    Yelena had just shaken her head and laughed lightly in response.

    You’re a little fucked, her inner-monologue provides helpfully as her heart does multiple summersaults in her chest.

    Only forty-five minutes after pulling into Ohio, Yelena parks their car in the car park of a national park. She’s quick to gather her belongings and leave the car, gesturing for Kate to follow her. They walk through the trees in silence for half an hour until the pain in Kate’s leg begins to slow her down. Wordlessly, Yelena fixes her backpack properly onto her shoulders and stretches an arm out for her.

    Kate narrows her eyes dubiously. “I don’t need your help,” she says (lies).

    Rolling her eyes, Yelena steps towards her to grab her closest arm and swings it around her shoulders. “I don’t need you dragging behind,” she shoots back once Kate is pressed against her, her hand finding purchase on the dip of her waist above her hip. “Just stop acting like you are fine and allow me to do this.”

    A grunt is all the blonde gets in response.

    Despite the assassin’s encouragement to use her for support, Kate does her best to not put her full weight on the smaller woman every other step. Her ego is already bruised enough, and the thought of having to rely almost entirely on Yelena makes irritation bubble up inside of her. The anger that has been fueling her motivation has dissipated and she no longer feels the impulse to punch Yelena in the face, but a strange necessity to prove her worth has filled the vacuum left behind. She can’t really ‘prove her worth’ if she’s hobbling at her partner’s side.

    Despite that train of thought, Yelena’s grip on her is tight enough that when she tries to pull away, she’s brought back to press harder against her body. The snarky glances the blonde sends her are returned just as irately.

    Still, her inner-monologue doesn’t get the memo.

    Her skin is so soft. Does she use moisturizer? How often does she exfoliate? Ask about her skin-care routine-

    Kate drowns the voice out with thoughts about the mission: what they’re going to do after they get the counter-agent; where they’re going to go; if she’s still going to give the USB to Earl considering the ambiguity of his intentions; whether she and Yelena are ever going to see each other again after this is all over.

    The last thought echoes particularly loudly in her mind, even as she begins to formulate a plan for her case.

    She supposes that once the Black Widow is free from mind control, then the code that’s in the USB will be of no use. Like trying to send an email from an email account that doesn’t exist anymore. At least, that’s how she assumes this ‘counter-agent’ works. If she and Yelena free the Black Widow, discover what the coordinates are for, and help the Black Widow disappear from the system, then the data in the USB will be of no use to John Earl. Meaning that both she and Yelena can get what they want.

    Damn, my brain is just so big sometimes.

    Satisfied with the conclusion she has reached, Kate allows herself to focus on the bigger issue at hand: the large farmland situated in the middle of a forest with hardly any footpaths or roads leading to it.

    Oh my god. This is the day that Yelena finally kills me, isn’t it? She was waiting to get me alone in the middle of fucking nowhere.

    Warily glancing at the assassin beside her, Kate tries to gauge her mood.

    She looks… fine, I guess? Maybe a lil’ grumpy? Not the end of the world though, I slapped her and survived. Twice.

    Kate grimaces at the reminder.

    Wow, I really have no self-restraint, do I?

    Yelena catches her repeated side-eyes and on the fifth glance - that is not as discrete as Kate had hoped, evidently - she glowers threateningly and hisses, “what? Is there something you want to say? Do I have something on my face?”

    “No,” Kate says quickly. “No, no. Don’t mind me.”

    Although the dark has Kate squinting blearily, she can tell that this place is bigger than the Barton’s farm and definitely more ‘farm-like’, what with one of the buildings emitting a soft rumble that sounds like pigs snorting. Between that building and a decently sized country house is what looks like a small metallic farm pen that’s currently empty. The country house is more of a bungalow, though Kate can see that there’s an attic from the window above where the front door is glowing with yellow light.

    There’s someone home.

    Assuming that that’s where they’re headed, Kate begins to walk toward the country house.

    “Kate, no.” Yelena’s hand at her waist tightens, gently pulling her back from the worn footpath leading up to the front door.

    The weak yellow glow in the distance just barely touches the green in her eyes and illuminates them with a warm hazel that’s startlingly pleasant. The corners of her mouth are downturned into a frown, her eyebrows furrowed in thought.

    Quietly, Kate nods and allows Yelena to guide her away from the house. Yelena steps through knee-high grass, letting Kate be the one who walks on the path that only allows one person to pass at a time. The grass is a lot less eroded than the footpath they had been walking on previously, short blades of grass peeking through dry mud. Probably less foot traffic.

    Kate only notices another, smaller building when they pass through several more trees to the side of the country house.

    And she only sees it because of the verdant fairy lights scattered in the bushes and trees surrounding the cabin.

    Yelena’s footsteps falter as they approach.

    There’s something in the silence between them - the rustle of foliage as a cool summer breeze pushes through the fields, the faint sounds of wildlife from between the trees, the unsteady breaths that Yelena takes beside her - it feels intimate. A lump forms at Kate’s throat. The more that she looks, the more that she realizes that the fairy lights are moving. That the fairy lights aren’t fairy lights, but fireflies swarming by the dozen in the trees. They cast a green light on the cabin, and a green light on them as they continue to quietly watch the dance. Kate feels the arm around her waist tighten and registers the vulnerability radiating from the woman’s body.

    “Welcome home,” Yelena breathes, reverence in the rasp of her words.

    Kate wills everything inside of her not to look at Yelena in that moment. To have some self- control and keep her eyes away because if she looks now, she’s scared about what she’s going to feel.

    At some point or other in their silence, her breathing falls in time with Yelena’s - or maybe Yelena’s falls in time with hers, she doesn’t really know. All she knows is that it happens. There’s something about the thick air between them that makes Kate feel the calmest she’s felt in months while at the same time makes her feel like she’s suffocating. The subtle rise and fall of Yelena’s shoulders staves off the incessant feeling of being chased that has been haunting Kate.

    Yelena is warm. Her hands are warm and her skin is warm and that look in her eye is warm.

    “Come on,” she says.

    Kate feels warm. “It’s beautiful,” she whispers, reluctant to break the quiet between them.

    Yelena hums and guides her toward the cabin with familiarity in her step, and Kate puts the pieces together.

    To Yelena, this is home.

    -

    The heavy atmosphere breaks as soon as they step through the front door.

    Yelena is quick to dump her backpack onto the counter of the small open kitchen and gets to work. She bustles around the space like the house is on fire. Kate thinks that she can smell traces of burnt firewood in the air that filters through the open windows. All that Kate can do is watch as the woman disappears and reappears from different doors, awkwardly sitting on the edge of a (surprisingly comfortable) couch.

    She thinks that the modest space she’s in right now is the living room slash kitchen, with a flat-screen TV perched on the wall opposite the couch. Yelena moves into a door to the left of where Kate is sitting and doesn’t come back out until five minutes later dressed in far more casual clothing (very baggy sweatpants and a loose shirt), so she concludes that that must be the blonde’s bedroom. When she dips in and back out of a door a little further along the wall with fresh bandages and medicine, Kate assumes that that’s where the bathroom is.

    As she watches the spectacle in front of her, Kate sinks further and further into the couch.

    Her eyelids are weighing down heavily, and despite the extra three hours of sleep she managed to squeeze in between West Virginia and Ohio, she can feel the exhaustion of a lack of proper night’s sleep weighing down on her.

    Unceremoniously dumping everything that she has been collecting in her rampage into Kate’s lap, Yelena kicks the side of Kate’s boot to wake her. “Stay here,” she orders, eyebrows raised expectantly.

    “Uh… why-” Taking that as an affirmation, Yelena decidedly nods once and strides away from her. The assassin is already disappearing out of the front door by the time Kate comes to her senses. “Oh okay.” Kate sinks into the very, very comfortable couch. “Sure I’ll stay here,” she says to the wooden ceiling, her muscles practically hugging the pillows.

    Sleep catches up to her, and Kate lets it submerge her as she hugs her duffel bag close to her chest.

    -

    Kate feels the presence in the room before she’s fully awake. The weight of somebody’s gaze on the side of her face alerts her.

    Willing her heart rate to keep as steady as possible, she keeps her eyes closed and remembers everything that Clint has taught her about being snuck up on. She holds her breath as she gauges what the best plan of action is.

    “Hello, Kate Bishop.”

    Crap. Way to go, Kate.

    The familiar accent curling around her full name immediately gives the woman away.

    Sighing in relief, Kate lets the soft, soft cushions swallow up her body. “I’m assuming you know Yelena.”

    “Yes.” A woman with black hair tied back in braids steps into vision, a small wooden basket tucked into her side under her arm. Her eyes are sharp and calculating, though her demeanor is nonchalant. As if she isn’t at all surprised that Kate is here. “Yelena is with her father, Alexei,” she says in an almost knowing tone, which is curious because no, ma’am, I don’t know an Alexei . “I am assuming she is looking for me but she does not know that I am here.”

    “Her father?” Kate’s heart begins to pick up.

    Her dad is here? Oh my god, am I gonna meet him? What if he wants to kill me?

    Why would he want to kill you?

    I-

    The woman frowns slightly, tilting her head questioningly. “That is what I said, no?”

    “No, yeah, it is but-”

    “You are injured.” Her movements are strikingly similar to Yelena’s, in that she steps into Kate’s personal space without a second thought and begins to tug at the shirt that has rolled up Kate’s torso in her nap.

    Is this a Russian thing? Or just a Black Widow thing? Is this woman even a Black Widow?

    “Bullet wound?” She asks when she sees the bandages that stretch around her side. Kate tenses uncomfortably under the scrutinizing gaze. “Hm, Yelena did this?”

    “Yes ma’am.”

    She hums again, nodding quietly as if she were mentally assessing Yelena’s work. For a moment it looks like she wants to say something about it, maybe comment on what could have been done better or how Kate could have been taking care of it more efficiently. And that’s confusing in itself because Yelena is ridiculously skilled. (Kate has to bite her tongue to stop herself from trying to defend Yelena’s honor.)

    The woman must see the blatantly confused expression on Kate’s face because she thinks better of it.

    “You do not look like you have been eating well,” the woman says instead, eyes judgmentally scanning her appearance. It makes heat rush to her cheeks as she self-consciously shifts on the couch. “Nutrients are very important for people who are doing this type of work, Kate Bishop. You must look after yourself. Come, we have leftovers from yesterday. Have you ever tried stroganoff?”

    Nutrients- Food- Leftovers-

    What?

    Overwhelmed by the ambush of criticisms on her eating habits, Kate scrambles up from the couch. “Oh, uh-” The expression on the woman’s face is stoic and analytic and it throws her off-balance for a second. She blinks for a couple of seconds, trying to analyze the woman’s intentions before she rolls her shoulders back and decides to roll with the punches. “Yes, I have, actually. One of my mother’s associates was from Russia so we ate it when we had dinner with him.”

    “It is good, no?”

    She grins a little. “Delicious, ma’am.”

    “Well, you need to eat. Food is important for recovery when you are injured,” she says. Her voice is cool, but it’s juxtaposed by the motherly insistence of getting her to eat. “Now tell me, Kate Bishop, why did Yelena have you hidden away in her house?”

    ‘Hidden away?’

    “I- wh- I don’t-”

    The woman watches her mouth open and close for a second, her face devoid of any emotion.

    Abruptly, her posture straightens and her chin tilts towards the front door. “Ah, I see,” she says with a small quirk of her lips.

    Kate barely has time to register the heavy footsteps rushing closer to the cabin before the front door is swinging open and a panicked Yelena is rushing through.

    “Melina,” is all Kate catches before the blonde is launching herself into a tirade of Russian.

    The woman, whose name Kate assumes is ‘Melina’, merely rolls her eyes as Yelena approaches her and tugs at her elbow, almost as if she’s trying to pull her away from Kate. “Yelena,” she says with a scolding tone to her voice that eerily reminds Kate of her own mother. “It is rude to talk in a language that your guest does not understand-”

    And then, ironically enough, Melina proceeds to rant at Yelena in Russian.

    Under the very faint glow of the lamplight, the highs of Yelena’s cheekbones look almost blushed. There’s an exasperated expression on her face that makes Kate feel almost sorry for the blonde. She’s been on the receiving end of one of these tirades many times before, so she feels for Yelena, she really does. But after everything that has happened over the last week it’s disarming and hilariously entertaining watching the deadly assassin crumble at the hands of this woman.

    The blonde is lucky that Kate is busy driving because otherwise she would have wrapped her hands around her throat and tried to throttle her.

    This must be what Clint was talking about.

    Huffing, Kate tries to contain her frustration and breathe before she speaks. “But you're free now, Yelena,” she says, trying to show sincerity in her voice. “You get to learn how to be more than what you've known.”

    From the corner of her eye, she sees Yelena look at her. Kate glances out of pure curiosity and sees the bittersweet smile on her face, and Kate already knows what she’s going to say before she says it. “That is easier said than done,” she mutters quietly like she’s trying to soften the blow of her words.

    And it’s not like Yelena is wrong. It isn’t easy to try to break out of the world that has been moulded for you, and Kate may be speaking from a place of privilege - especially considering that the Red Room sounds like hell on earth and nothing like the comfortable life that Kate has lived - but she has to have hope. She has to have hope that there is something better out there than the hand that life deals you with. There has to be something better out there for Yelena.

    She deserves it.

    “You said it yourself,” Kate says. “It's your actions that make you who you are, not pretty words.”

    Yelena doesn’t say anything else after that. But Kate feels the weights tilt in her direction and her stomach swoops dangerously. It gives her hope that maybe after this, the blonde might consider her words.

    -

    The plan goes as well as Kate can hope it can go.

    Which, in hindsight, is not very well, considering that her expectations are low but hey, she’s trying to be optimistic about it.

    Their plan changes a little when they arrive at the facility, which they had previously thought was a mansion that belonged to some Samuel Alvarado (a man who had been easy to trace once Kate managed to get her hands on the list of people who could want John Earl’s daughter dead). The security is higher than they had anticipated, with around fifteen guards dressed and armed like they’re part of a military unit surveilling the building. Yelena lets her know that they are not affiliated with the military, but Kate can figure out as much with the way some of them clumsily handle their assault rifles. It throws a wrench in their plan because they had accounted for maybe seven - ten maximum - guards.

    “I need you to stay back,” Yelena says as she prepares her pistols almost mechanically.

    “What? No way.” Kate is already defensive enough with the inkling feeling that Yelena is purposefully trying to keep her out of harm’s way. The last thing that she wants to do is to be babied by this person who she desperately wants to be able to call a partner one day. If she got Hawkeye, an Avenger, to crumble to her persistence, then she can get her friendly-

    neighborhood Black Widow to do so too. “There are, like, thirty guys out there and one of you.”

    Said Black Widow rolls her eyes and raises her eyebrows at her, unimpressed. “I have handled more.”

    And she’s probably right.

    I don’t know. We don’t know a lot about Yelena’s past.

    In a moment of weakness, Kate’s eyes trail down Yelena’s arms. The muscles at her biceps and forearms bulge as she sheaths a ridiculous amount of blades into her scabbard. All of the moisture in Kate’s mouth disappears.

    I mean, she definitely looks like she can take them on.

    One of those arms is reaching out to her the next moment, and Kate shivers back to reality when gloved fingers snap in her face. There’s an exasperated expression on Yelena’s face, but Kate can see the glint in her eye that tells her that the blonde is holding herself back from teasing her. She wets her lips and ignores the blood that rushes to her face, focusing on Yelena’s words again.

    “I am going to draw them towards the entrance of the estate,” Yelena is saying, pointing towards the extravagant golden gate. Subtle. “When I give you the sign, you can enter through the basement window like we planned and hack the security system.”

    Hack the security system. Erase all traces of that code to control the Black Widow. Find out if there are any backups and if I can, track them down and download them.

    Sounds easy enough.

    Except there’s an uncomfortable weight in her gut that’s telling her that this entire thing is riskier than they’re acknowledging.

    “You’re not going to be fighting off all of those guys on your own,” Kate says, setting her jaw stubbornly. Yelena may be a professionally trained assassin, but she’s still human. She has a mortal body. She cuts and she bleeds and she dies like any other human being. “The odds are too crappy, at least let me cover you from up here.”

    “The odds are perfectly fine.” Yelena is entirely expressionless. There is not a trace of emotion on her face. “Focus on the plan.”

    “The plan sucks,” Kate grumbles.

    “If you try to cover me, your position will be revealed.” The assassin begins to lift from her crouch, hands curling around her pistols. “You are too injured to take on more than three people at once.”

    “But-”

    “ Kate .” Yelena’s expression shifts into a frown. Her dark eyes soften into a cool jade under the warm evening sunlight, and Kate can already feel her resolve crumbling. The use of her first name only is manipulative, and Kate has a feeling that Yelena is very aware of what she’s doing by allowing this much emotion to show through her cold-hearted assassin facade. But it’s entirely too effective because when she says, “I need you to stay back,” Kate is already huffing out frustratedly and sitting back on her heels.

    She mulls it over.

    She knows that she’s injured. That even though the wound on her leg has healed on the surface, the muscles that were impacted are still doing their best to recover and regain their strength. The hole that had torn through her side has been healing well over the past week, but the exit wound is still too sensitive for her to be straining it too much. Ideally, Kate should be resting it for another week or so before she even thinks about doing archery again.

    Fuck this. Fuck getting shot. Not being able to help is not badass. Not worth the scars at all.

    Sensing her inner conflict, Yelena shifts closer and leans down. Her gloved hands slide across the skin of Kate’s neck as they move to grip the base of her skull, effectively tilting her head upwards so that her stubborn gaze meets the debilitating green in Yelena’s eyes. Kate’s heart leaps to her throat.

    “Kate, stay .” Yelena breathes across Kate’s mouth, her forehead pressing against hers.

    “I’m not a dog,” Kate grumbles despite herself.

    The corners of Yelena’s lips quirk up. “Then you will be smarter than a dog and not follow me when I leave.”

    Kate narrows her eyes.

    Stupid fucking Black Widows. Stupid Yelena. Stupid stupid stupid.

    Just as easily as she had sensed her conflict, Yelena senses her acceptance and exits her space.

    Kate’s breathing skips as the assassin goes back to curling her hands around the pistols strapped to her hips. She shakes her head, hoping to rid herself of the pressure on her skin. “I’m pretty sure there’s going to be an emergency lockdown that kicks in ten minutes after I try to hack the system,” she says in a warning tone. “We’ll have to get out of there quickly if we want to make it out alive, so I’ll unlock the doors to the garage and we’ll take a car to get away.”

    Yelena nods. “Then we will meet at the garage before the ten minutes is up.”

    “Deal.”

    “If I don’t make it in the ten minutes, leave.”

    Kate scoffs, anger prickling at her as she looks up to glare at the blonde. “Yelena-”

    “Kate Bishop.” This time, Yelena’s glare is harsh. Threatening. “Take the information and leave.” Her accent thickens as does the growl in her voice. “I can handle myself.”

    Stupid. Stupid. Stupid.

    “I’ll tell you if I find anything strange,” Kate says instead of lying.

    Yelena looks at her for a moment, eyes scanning her features. Kate does her very best at maintaining a poker face, willing herself to think about Lucky and seeing him again and showering him with affection and attention to make up for the past two weeks.

    Convinced, the Black Widow disappears into the treeline.

    Meanwhile, Kate heads back to the car to retrieve her bow and arrows.

    -

    Kate doesn’t listen.

    Of course she doesn’t listen, who the fuck does Yelena think I am?

    The moment that Kate had found the live footage of blood-stained Yelena getting her ass handed to her by the other Black Widow, all traces of their plan had been thrown out of the window. There was an unfair advantage for the other Black Widow because she had support from two guards. Yelena had been successful in stripping them of their weapons, but they were still strong enough to land a couple of hits while the Black Widow came at her with a blade.

    Honestly, the fact that Yelena is even holding her ground is impressive in itself.

    But judging by the blood staining her face, arms and legs, Kate isn’t sure how much longer Yelena is going to last.

    She impatiently waits for the data to download into her USB arrow and makes sure to kick the guard who had decided it was a smart idea to try and sneak up on a very annoyed, very irritated, and very Avenger-in-training Kate Bishop and hold her at gunpoint. The sound of his body smacking to the floor at her elbow strike to the face had been satisfying, though not satisfying enough to quell the urge to fight.

    The very second that the monitor alerts her that the data transfer is done, Kate takes an explosive arrow from her quiver. The guard is slumped against the door, but she knows that the entire building is equipped with fire alarms and sprinklers.

    He’ll be fine, she thinks as she pulls back the bowstring and releases the arrow at the computer system.

    The ground shakes beneath her feet, but she’s too busy running up the stairs to care.

    By the time Kate gets to the office, Yelena has dropped the two guards and is now doing her best to hold off the Black Widow.

    Unsure of how much damage she is allowed to deal to the Black Widow, considering that they’re currently trying to free her from mind control, Kate hesitates. Her weight shifts from one foot to the other. Her fingers twitch at her bow, analyzing the way that the woman launches Yelena over the side of a desk like it’s the easiest thing in the world. Kate knows that it’s not the easiest thing in the world.

    Hissing an inhale through her teeth, Kate reaches for an electro-arrow.

    Just as the Black Widow is about to drive her dagger through Yelena’s throat, the trick arrow strikes her right shoulder blade. The blade scatters to the floor as the woman convulses.

    Yelena scrambles to her feet, grabbing the fallen weapon and distancing herself from the Black Widow. “I told you to leave!” She growls at Kate from across the room, running in the direction of the window wall streaming the daylight into the office.

    “Like I’d leave a partner behind,” Kate shouts back.

    The Black Widow, who hasn’t fallen to her knees despite the electricity coursing through her body, wrenches the trick arrow from her back.

    Kate clicks her tongue.

    Gonna have to up the voltage on that.

    Before her fingers can make contact with the fletching of her net arrow, her vision jostles and a sharp pain shoots through her head. Stumbling to keep herself on her feet, Kate instinctively holds her bow in front of her protectively. Even with her vision swimming, she can make out the shape of another guy dressed in an infuriatingly crisp white dress shirt. In his hand is a yellow vase that both looks and feels resilient.

    The black beard on his face somewhat matches the image of the owner of the facility. “Is this the asshole I’ve been waiting to meet?” She calls out to Yelena, though the sound of furniture breaking and small cries of pain lets her know that she’s not going to be getting an answer from her partner.

    “Who the fuck are you?” The guy growls.

    Ooh, we have a talker. My favorite.

    “I’m so sorry for my manners.” Kate grins as charmingly as she can through the pain, straightening her posture into a casual stance. “I’m Hawkeye.”

    Recognition gleams in his eyes, but his brow furrows as he looks her up and down. “The bow and arrow guy?”

    With a wink, Kate says, “The one and only,” before swinging her bow across his head.

    Payback.

    It’s her adversary’s turn to stumble into a desk, the yellow vase dropping to the floor and shattering to pieces.

    “That looked expensive,” she says, trying to stall for more time to catch her breath. “Even if you don’t like them, you shouldn’t use art pieces as weapons, Mr Alvarado. I’m sure there are potential buyers out there.”

    A guttural cry interrupts her rant as Alvarado lunges at her with a letter opener.

    Who receives letters anymore?!

    Kate tries to swing out of the way, but her wounds slow her down. The edge catches her cheek, which is better than her eye where Alvarado was aiming at, but it still procures blood on her skin. A sharp pain shoots up her side as she throws herself on a desk to avoid another anguished slash.

    Desperate, Kate swings her bow at the man again. It hits him in the chest, but his hands are quick to wrap around it and yank it out of her hands.

    Time to get creative.

    The first thing that her hands grab is the bottom of a computer monitor. Straining her stomach in this position, she lifts the monitor over her head and brings it crashing down on Alvarado’s head as he tries to lunge for her. He falters, giving her enough time to roll back off the desk and effectively put a barrier between her and her enemy. He pushes against the desk, shoving the edge against Kate’s thighs and making her lose her balance.

    As she trips back, a hand yanks her downwards.

    Leather and blood and gunpowder drown her senses.

    Several gunshots ring out. Kate registers that Yelena has pulled her behind a toppled desk and is shooting in Alvarado’s direction.

    “‘Lena,” Kate winces through the sharp pain at her side. “I think the Widow is here.”

    Yelena, with her face bathed in blood from her hairline down to her jaw and a withering glare that is sharpened by the contrast of the red and green, looks down at Kate. A shiver of danger swims down Kate’s spine and she almost cowers back at the sight. Almost lets herself think about all of the bodies that had to fall prey to the assassin’s hands for her to be painted red.

    Yelena kicks her roughly at her calf and growls, “You are so annoying.”

    “You love me,” Kate says, pointedly ignoring the annoyance that is oozing from Yelena’s demeanor.

    Glancing around the side of the table, she sees that the Black Widow is protecting Alvarado from the bullets of Yelena’s pistol by shielding his body with hers. They duck and hide behind desks and columns, but Yelena is relentless with her aim. Luck must be on the other Black Widow’s side, or Yelena is a lot more skilled than Kate previously gave her credit for because not a single bullet lands on the Black Widow’s body. There are small cries of pain and curses being screamed at them as bullets skim Alvarado’s arms or legs.

    Their targets disappear through a door into the hallway.

    Kate scrambles to her feet. “I’ll distract Alvarado,” she says, scooping up her stray bow from where it had fallen. “You get the Widow.”

    Wordlessly, Yelena reaches for more ammo in the pockets of her pants and disappears into the hallway.

    Kate is slower to follow, taking the moment of silence to calm the anxiety that rushes through her as the metallic taste of blood coats her tongue. From her peripheries she sees the bodies of the two guards slumped on the floor. Crimson pools beneath their heads. By the time she gathers her senses, she has lost sight of Yelena. However, the red footprints left behind on the marble floor leads her in her direction. She assumes that Alvarado will be attempting to escape the facility, what with most - if not all - of his guards incapacitated. But the back of her neck prickles as she jogs past an open door.

    Her instincts are right as she ducks and the air above her sends her hair swinging to the side.

    An arrow is already notched in her bowstring by the time she spins around. It’s released and she catches Alvarado in the shoulder. He screams in pain but refuses to drop the painting in his hand.

    Widening her eyes dramatically, Kate looks at the piece of art. “Woah, where did you get this?”

    “Shut up!” With a deep rumble in his chest, the man throws the painting at her with his good arm.

    Kate sidesteps the painting, ignoring the pain in her leg as it catches on her thigh. “Aw, come on,” she says through the shocks shooting up her spine. “I was gonna offer to buy it from you.”

    “I’m gonna enjoy killing you,” he hisses, blood staining his teeth as he scowls. Then he lunges. His fist aims for her face but she dodges. There’s another fist coming up to her injured side. She swivels and dodges again, this time a little more drastically in her effort to avoid the hit.

    Another fist. It catches her jaw. Another fist. It lands on her cheek.

    Disoriented, Kate swings out wildly. The uppercut sticks.

    There’s a ‘clack’ as his teeth clatter together. Alvarado stumbles back, gripping his jaw in pain. “Fucking whore,” he spits, saliva and blood flying from his lips.

    With one last strike to the head, the man crumples to the floor. “Now that’s just rude,” Kate pants, grimacing as the wound on her side strains.

    Thank god.

    As she stands over the man’s unconscious body, either making sure that he’s passed out or taking it as a moment to catch her breath, the pain in her body begins to seep into her mind. Trembling, her legs carry her down the hallway, eyes trained on the bloody footsteps. Her face is throbbing quickly in tandem with her heart rate, breaths raspy as she tries to shove oxygen into her lungs.

    A part of her tells her that she should sit down. That Yelena will be fine - she already took care of fifteen guards on her own, she doesn’t need her. She’s injured and hurt, and tired.

    We got what we came here for.

    Her thighs quake beneath the weight of her body as she stumbles down the stairs in the direction of the front door.

    Yelena will be fine.

    Her body follows the trail of blood.

    Yelena is not fine.

    Kate’s eyes land on the scene playing out in front of her.

    Yelena is not fine.

    Thoughtlessly, Kate’s muscle memory leads her through the motion of picking out an arrow, sliding it on the arrow shelf, weaving her fingers around the bowstring and pulling it back to her mouth.

    Shoot.

    “Don’t.” Yelena’s voice calls. It’s strained. There are hands wrapped around her neck. She can’t get them off.

    Shit.

    The Black Widow on top of her is weaponless, but the hands that are enveloped around Yelena’s throat look like weapons in themselves. The only thing preventing her from crushing the blonde’s windpipe is Yelena’s own hands holding back the incessant grip of her fingers, a pained grimace on her face.

    Stupid.

    Kate’s heart pounds at her chest.

    Stupid.

    “Kate.”

    Green eyes shift.

    Glinting under the last gleams of sunlight is a familiar glass vial just out of reach of Yelena’s hands. Kate’s vision tilts. Her breathing is off. Red tauntingly pulses and swirls beneath the glass surface as if calling out her name, begging to be released. The air is jagged as she breathes it in, piercing her nostrils as she trains the arrow on the vial.

    She exhales, all of the air in her body rushing out as the tension snaps. Her fingers fly off the string. The arrow whistles.

    It catches the vial perfectly, the angle erupting the glass in the direction of the Black Widows. Red bursts from its confines and immediately swathes the two women.

    Kate’s fingers twitch at her side, ready to notch another arrow.

    Ready to kill.

    But her readiness isn’t required. Visibly loosening, the hands around Yelena’s throat release their grip and the blonde audibly gasps for air, coughing and wheezing. The woman perched atop her waist leans back, previously deadly hands now delicately trembling at her sides as she comes to her senses. A haunting look falls over her features - a gut-wrenching concoction of disgust and fear and anger and anguish. The sob that tears through her body and shakes her off Yelena is terrifying.

    Kate doesn’t think she’s ever seen anything like it.

    Yelena pushes up from the floor, her breaths still audible as she tries to regain oxygen. Her eyes meet Kate’s and it pulls Kate from the spiral that she was unknowingly sliding into.

    There’s an unspoken understanding between the two of them.

    The blonde is the one to look away first, moving to console the sobbing Black Widow. Not wanting to intrude on the moment, Kate shakily takes in a breath and begins to walk away to look for a getaway car.

    -

    Kate gets the USB to Earl a day after getting back to New York. The dinner is tense. Much less pleasant now that Kate is aware that the man sitting across from her is hot on the heels of a former Black Widow, desperate to get his hands on her for vengeance.

    It’s not that Kate doesn’t sympathise with the man’s loss - she can’t even begin to imagine the pain of losing a child - but she knows the pain of losing someone that she loves. She knows that grief doesn’t have to resort to vengeance. Even if it contradicts the surface level meaning of what it is to be an ‘Avenger,’ Kate doesn’t agree with the idea of using whatever means necessary to exact revenge on those responsible. That Black Widow isn’t responsible for what Alvarado made her do.

    So in that USB is everything that she has found on Samuel Alvarado, who has gone into hiding now that he doesn’t have his Black Widow protecting him. Hopefully, that leads Earl’s chase for justice to the person truly responsible.

    When she opens the door to her apartment, leaning back on the handle like she always has to do to get it open, she’s ready to sink into the comfort of her bed and sleep for two days.

    She kicks her boots from her feet and leaves them in the hallway. Her feet scuff the cool floor as she walks to the kitchen.

    It’s her exhaustion that prevented her from noticing the presence in the room.

    As she goes to open the fridge, Kate notes the bottle of tequila sitting on the counter beside it. “What did I say about coming to my apartment, Belova?” Her hand brushes over it, contemplating the thought of indulging the urge to drink until she forgets everything about Miami, but she resists and instead pulls out the bottle of Pepsi from the fridge.

    The silence that follows is not silent enough for her to even be tricked into thinking that she’s speaking into nothingness. She knows that somewhere in her apartment, Yelena is sitting there watching her pour out a glass of soda instead of the tequila she gifted her. Kate’s favorite. She doesn’t ask how she knows.

    She knows a lot of things about me.

    “I wanted to say ‘hello’ to Lucky.” The raspy, accented voice comes from the direction of the couch. Definitely not as comfortable as Yelena’s.

    “Is that so?” Kate brings the glass up to her lips, trying to hide the small smile on her lips as she finally turns around to face the blonde.

    Yelena, beautiful as ever, is sprawled on the couch with Lucky in her lap. Tousled over her shoulders, her blonde hair falls into her face, partially hiding the bruises that have refused to go quietly beneath the make-up. The tresses glow a pale gold as the cloud-impaired-sunlight falls into the living room in the form of white light. The overcast weather makes her eyes glisten an almost blue hue, the cool green refusing to display any information. She’s wearing a thin bomber jacket, perfect for the transition of summer into autumn, but it makes Kate feel a little overdressed in her white jumpsuit.

    Following her train of thought, Yelena’s eyes dance down her appearance, leaving a trail of blazing fire in their wake.

    At the lack of scratching from Yelena’s hands, Lucky pounces from the blonde’s lap and skitters over to Kate. With a happy bark, he jumps up at her front and waits to be greeted despite his nonchalance at her return.

    What a traitor.

    Kate indulges her dog anyway because she can’t blame him for choosing to enjoy the affection from Yelena.

    “Did the meeting with John Earl go well?” Yelena asks, pushing herself up from the couch.

    “As well a meeting with a shady businessman can go…” She sighs, releasing Lucky’s face and grabbing her glass. With slow movements, trying not to strain the still-healing injury at her side, she pads over to the blonde and perches herself on the armrest of her couch. “What about you? Is the Widow okay?”

    “She’s fine. Back at the farm now.” Yelena rolls her lower lip on her teeth, looking down at Lucky as he prances back to her. Her hands shake his fur and the frown on her face relaxes. “She will be going to a safe place with the others.”

    Kate hums, watching Lucky pant happily as he proudly looks up at her. “Good.”

    “Are you?”

    Not really.

    “How are you doing?”

    Not that well.

    “I know that you do not trust me, but I was serious Kate Bishop.” Yelena steps closer. Kate can smell the familiar scent of vanilla and orchids on her, and it soothes the climbing anxiety. “I do not want to see you dead.”

    Yelena hadn’t seen the small breakdown she had in the car after Kate had walked away from the Black Widows, but her red-rimmed eyes and shaking body had given her away. At the time, Kate hadn’t been lucid enough to register what was happening, but Yelena had gripped her by the shoulders and pulled her into her arms. The console of the car had impaired the hug, but the blonde hadn’t allowed Kate to pull her head away from her shoulder until her breathing had calmed down.

    Hug her again.

    Shut the hell up.

    Kate grips the glass between her hands tighter. “I’m gonna be okay, 'Lena,” she says. Her voice seems to loosen the tension on Yelena’s face, instead replaced with what feels like affection. “I’m going to the Barton's for a while to take my mind off things - heal up since…” She gestures to her side where her side still aches sometimes. “Well, you already know.”

    A quiet laugh slips past Yelena’s lips and Kate matches it with her own.

    “And…” Kate’s voice catches in her throat a little and she coughs a little to dislodge it. “Clint is getting me some professional help, too, when I come back.”

    I really need a therapist after all of that…

    Yelena’s face does something strange - a half-frown, half-smile - but she seems to settle on a gentle, neutral expression. “Good.”

    “What about you?” Kate asks tentatively. “Where are you going?”

    “I am not sure…” The blonde trails off, looking out of the window.

    The conversation that they had in the car plays out in her mind. Kate desperately hopes that Yelena will take her advice.

    “Well… as long as you don’t plan on double-crossing me any time soon,” she says with a teasing lilt to her voice. “You’re welcome to come to say ‘hello’ to Lucky.” And just because everything finally feels settled now that Earl has his USB and Kate is free from the people chasing after her since Yelena ‘took care of it,’ she shrugs her shoulders and playfully nudges Yelena’s leg with her barefoot. “If you’re in the area or whatever.”

    The blonde allows the contact, grinning impishly as she rolls her eyes. “I cannot say that it is very high on my to-do list, little hawk.”

    “You’re still a bitch for that,” Kate says, narrowing her eyes at her. “I hope you know that.”

    Yelena laughs and reaches down to stroke behind Lucky’s ears. “I did what I had to do, I do not regret it…” Her voice trails off as it gains sentimentality, and Kate’s heart skips in her chest as their eyes meet again. “Besides, we had fun.”

    Tilting her head to the side like she’s considering it, Kate hums.

    The grin that spreads across Yelena’s face is almost breath-taking, her green eyes glinting in the cloudy light.

    “I’ll see you around, Yelena.”

    Yelena nods, moving to Kate’s window where everything is already set up for her to drop out of it dramatically. “See you, Hawkeye.”
