    For a while, everything feels alright.

    Until it doesn’t.

    It’s not like she misses Yelena. (She does.) She barely knows her. (She wants to.) And who knows when the blonde will jump back into her life again? It’s not like she cares, anyway. (She definitely does.)

    Being back in New York feels like a slap in the face.

    I’m lonely.

    Her therapist reinforces that epiphany. Her therapist isn’t wrong.

    Growing up as an only child affected her more than she likes to admit. Kate is prone to loneliness, it seems. This shouldn’t be a surprise, really, but she still has to sit in the teal armchair and let the realization sink in.

    Kate hates being alone. She makes friends everywhere she goes. She tries to establish connections and friendships so that she always has somebody to talk and joke around with and keep herself busy. Even when as a child she threw herself into extracurriculars, both because it fueled her newfound passion for ‘avenging’ and because it was an easy excuse to make connections. Sure she begged her mother to move out the very day she turned eighteen, but at the end of the day, her childhood bedroom was always there for her to return to whenever she wanted to. An offer she took surprisingly often despite her claim at independence.

    Wow. That’s not something I wanna think about too hard.

    But she can’t turn to her usual coping mechanism of pushing down and bottling up her emotions. Apparently , it’s not healthy and she should really stop doing that.

    So she tries to stop.

    And it’s infuriating. Especially when her plan to try and forget about Yelena includes compartmentalizing her emotions and shoving them deep, deep down into her subconsciousness.

    Now that her plan is skewed, forgetting about Yelena isn’t as easy as she would have hoped.

    So she tries to distract herself in between therapy sessions. She contacts Jack and convinces him to fence with her every week. She joins multiple LARPing events and admittedly has a lot of fun shooting at people without worrying that she’s going to accidentally end their lives. She and Spiderman hang out outside of fighting crime and end up trying to finish watching all eight seasons of Brooklyn Nine-Nine. Even Cassie texts her to let her know she will be visiting New York to see what the city is like for college.

    Weeks pass and Kate isn’t lonely anymore.

    But she is touch-starved.

    With the stress of her mother’s ongoing legal matters invading her life, Bishop Security still leaning on her identity to keep business going, starting a freelance private investigation business from the ground up, and generally trying to keep her mind busy so that she doesn’t fall into the darkness looming over her… her libido has been non-existent. The last time she got herself off was maybe six months ago, and even then it had been too unsatisfactory to even consider trying again.

    Then Yelena crashed through her apartment window and flipped everything upside down.

    It may sound like an exaggeration but that is exactly what it felt like.

    It’s been three weeks since she has last seen Yelena. Everything is going okay, it’s not like Kate has caved and tried to find Yelena’s phone number so that she can check up on her and see how she’s doing. She doesn’t care.

    Nope.

    No.

    Definitely not.

    Of course, nothing can ever go right.

    When she startles awake with a moan forming in her throat in the shape of Yelena’s name, a mind-numbing urge to slip her fingers into her (probably ruined) underwear and the fresh images of her dream playing in the back of her eyelids, Kate becomes aggravatingly aware of one thing.

    She’s incredibly sexually frustrated.

    Kate is very confused about what to do with this piece of information, and the vague images that her brain has formulated that flash in her mind. Her right brain tells her to do what anybody else would do: get herself off. In a surprising turn of events, her left brain agrees. The sexual frustration is distracting to the point that she misses the bullseye more than once in one of her target practices and it’s actually concerning. So the logical and hedonist sides of her brain shake hands and agree that getting it over and done with is the best decision.

    So Kate tries.

    It’s embarrassing how quickly she melts under her own touch, and how quickly she feels herself reaching her climax with the vague images of the assassin playing out in her mind. But then the guilt hits her like a freight train and the climax slips through her fingers.

    Or rather: it doesn’t.

    It feels wrong, for some reason, to masturbate to the thought of somebody who has only shown vague signs of reciprocation, and even then it’s so vague that Kate feels like she’s going crazy. Kate is usually good at sensing when somebody is attracted to her - her intuition is rarely wrong, and even when it is she can flirt her way in and out of situations. But this is Yelena. Who knows if the woman is naturally flirty or not? What if Kate has been projecting all of her lust onto their interactions? What if Yelena only sees her as a potential friend?

    The guilt is so overwhelming that she forces herself to forget everything about this and settles for the next best thing: one-night stands.

    This is a great plan. It’s not like we haven’t done anything like this before - it worked fantastically well in college. Besides, people do this all the time. Nothing wrong with that. Nothing wrong at all.

    …

    You jinxed it.

    In an embarrassing turn of events, Kate’s plan to keep her dignity completely falls apart the minute that she cums to the thought of Yelena.

    Okay, wait. I know how that sounds.

    It wasn’t intentional.

    The guy was very good-looking and built like an athlete. Kate did feel attracted to him, even beyond the looks department. They vibed well. She had fun talking to him and bouncing comments back and forth, the mental games just foreplay for the inevitable tension that formed between them. Even though she knew that if they had met under any other circumstances they would have probably made good friends, she knew that they both knew what they were there for.

    And it was… okay.

    The touch-starved side of her indulged in the human contact, and that was probably what made everything seem fun at first.

    It’s taking too long.

    Her mind wanders. Her thoughts stray and her eyes fixate on the bedroom window where there the last trace of sunlight is falling through. The light is colder now that fall is coming, with it bringing grey skies, fiery red and yellow leaves, and the season of hot beverages.

    I wonder what Yelena looks like in this lighting.

    Images of the blonde filter through her mind. The first time that she saw Yelena on that rooftop, her hair swinging into her face, cheeks flushed from exertion and the cold, the part of her lips and darkness in her eyes. Yelena tumbling out of her apartment window, once in the winter with yellow light casting a halo and twice in the summer. Yelena between her legs, her hands trailing on the skin of her thighs, the same dark eyes roving over her body. Kate’s dress tearing under the strength of her hands. Her fingers slipping between her thighs.

    And then the dream that has been intrusively interrupting every thought she has had for the past few days presses play.

    Suddenly, the pleasure that she feels is overwhelming, building and building and-

    Yelena.

    Fortunately for Kate, the moan that tumbles out of her mouth is incoherent enough that it just sounds like Kate is babbling nonsense. Un fortunately for Kate, she is very aware of the fact that that moan was for somebody other than the person in bed with her.

    It’s mortifying.

    Immediately after catching her breath, Kate scrambles out of bed and stammers out a bunch of excuses to explain her disappearance. The guy (Kate has totally forgotten his name at this point) looks a little confused, but not too affronted by the situation. It seems that he is pleased with his performance and isn’t discouraged by Kate’s eagerness to leave so quickly.

    The guilt comes back two-fold.

    Kate’s go-to solution is to go to the nearest bar and drink her problems away.

    After that, things become blurry. She bumps into a group of girls in the bathroom of a bar who immediately adopt her into their circle. Kate lets them fix her make-up, all of them taking turns pitching their ideas for her new look and squealing every time they stop to admire the progress. There are a lot of raunchy comments dressed up as compliments thrown around and Kate can’t help but double down and return the energy like this is just another Friday.

    The group of girls convince her to go to another bar that is more crowded, enticing her with the promise of free liquor. By then Kate is tipsy enough that her balance is slightly off-kilter, but the atmosphere is addictive and she can’t help but accept the drinks that come her way. She thinks that, at some point in the night, spurred by a cheesy chant and liquid courage, she ends up doing body shots with some of the girls in that group. One way or another, she ends up with several new numbers in her phone and lipstick stains on her neck.

    The contrast of this and three hours ago is disorienting, but Kate enjoys the brief contentment that has loosened all of the muscles in her body and loses herself in the social interaction.

    (Maybe her therapist was right when she said that she avoids being alone at all costs.

    Damn you, therapist. )

    Somewhere between recklessly dancing on a tabletop and downing a shot of vodka (does Yelena like vodka? Or is that just a stereotype for Russian people?), she reaches the point of drunkenness where her mind switches from asinine fun into slightly depressed introspection. Dancing and singing and laughing is no longer fun. All she wants to do is curl up in a ball and watch that environmental documentary released during the Blip that had gotten Wasted Kate through her worst college nights.

    That’s how she bumps into a girl called MJ.

    MJ is sitting outside the bar sipping on a beer when Kate meets her.

    Gracious as ever, Kate is dragging herself down the street in the direction she thinks is her apartment, having told the group of girls she was dancing with that she really needed to go walk her dog. (Nobody questions her despite it being two in the morning.) She thinks that she’s dragging herself down the street, though in reality she’s just slowly descending onto her hands and knees as the world around her spins. MJ evidently watches the whole thing from the railing she is perched on.

    “New York is weird, man,” she says when Kate decidedly slumps against the street to use her jacket as a pillow.

    And that’s how Kate meets her soon-to-be friend.

    MJ walks over to her and crouches beside her. She can’t remember who started it but they somehow end up debating the psychology of the people who continue to live in New York despite it being the epicentre of multiple world-ending events.

    Before they know it, it’s four in the morning and they’ve drank more drinks than what MJ can pay. Kate discovers that she’s a freshman in college and feels immediate sympathy for her financial situation. So she pays for both of them so that they can migrate to a random bench to continue their conversation.

    Kate is in the middle of demonstrating her parkour skills while MJ laughs her ass off at her ridiculousness when Peter shows up.

    Or not Peter.

    Except he is Peter, Kate just didn’t know it at the time.

    The new guy and MJ know each other, though not very well, MJ tells her. Kate doesn’t mind the new person, since he seems to genuinely care for both their health and safety. He’s rambling about it being dangerous to hang around at this time of night, especially when they look like they just drank an entire bar empty. (Kate glances at the number of beer bottles on the side of the bench and feels the sudden urge to use the bathroom. How can her bladder hold that much liquid?) It doesn’t take long for Kate to notice that something is going on between her two new friends. From the way that they interact, they’re like two awkward teenagers trying to flirt. It’s really cute.

    Kate watches from a safe distance and inspects the newcomer. He looks a little rough… like he just crawled out of a fight. Bruises are peeking from the collar of his shirt and a poorly concealed cut on his forehead. It has Kate’s intuition setting off.

    “You sound familiar…”

    Visibly startled, he turns to look at her. “What?” His voice is hoarse, slightly breaking at the end. “No I don't?”

    “Yeah…” Kate narrows her eyes. “Yeah, you do.”

    “Wh- I don't-”

    “I know you.” His voice is familiar. “I'm pretty sure I know you…”

    MJ perks up, suddenly curious. “You think you know him?”

    Nodding, Kate puts her hand to her mouth and tries to recall where she recognizes his voice from. “Yeah, I just... can't remember where from…”

    “Maybe he just has that face. I get the same feeling too.”

    “No-” His eyes dart between the two of them. “Look, you don't know me. We literally just met. I don't-” His hands are wildly gesturing in front of him and Kate catches the bruises painted on his knuckles. “I don't even know what your name is!”

    Kate hums.

    MJ’s head swivels between the two of them. “This is Kate,” she supplies helpfully when Kate doesn’t respond, placing her hand on Kate’s shoulder.

    Kate narrows her eyes and hums louder.

    “Kate this is Peter,” MJ continues, clumsily gesturing with her hand and accidentally smacking him in the chest. He winces. “Parker.”

    She tests how the name sounds on her lips, trying to see if it’ll trigger her memory. “Peter…”

    “Hey!” Peter slaps his hands together. The sound irritates MJ, who scrunches her nose in his general direction. “How about I go get us some water, huh? It's getting late and-”

    “Oh, we were planning on getting some food.” MJ interrupts him casually, slinging her arm around Kate’s shoulders. She has the height advantage, so Kate ends up burrowed under a whole lot of denim and curly hair. “Probably tacos.”

    She isn’t too perturbed by the movement, however. There’s just something about Peter’s whole aura that is familiar enough to have her mind running around in circles. Kate loves puzzles. She loves solving them. She loves the satisfaction and pride that comes with the success. The harder that they get, the more satisfying it is. It’s probably why - even though it’s been a month since she left - she can’t get Yelena out of her mind. Yelena is a walking, talking, breathing puzzle and Kate is hooked on the adrenaline and intrigue that comes with solving her.

    “... and I'm pretty buzzed,” MJ is saying, blatantly ignoring Peter’s worried grimace. “We should grab some shots before we get tacos.”

    “No-”

    “Whatdya think Katie-Kate?”

    Kate sees the way that Peter nervously runs a hand through his hair. His awkward shift of weight from one leg to the other. There are reruns of Brooklyn Nine-Nine playing in her head. “Coolcoolcoolcoolcoolcool.”

    Peter’s eyes gleam with recognition.

    Oblivious to the dawning realization currently crashing down on Kate, MJ removes her arm from Kate’s shoulders and looks back at Peter. “She thinks it's cool.”

    “Ohmygod-” Kate’s eyes widen. “Spidey?!”

    With reflexes that Kate now recognizes, Peter lunges forward and slaps his hand over her mouth. “Kate no-”

    “What?” MJ frowns, her eyes now being the ones to narrow in suspicion.

    “She said spider- there was a spider in your hair.”

    “What? Where?!”

    With MJ momentarily distracted, Peter loosens his very tight grip on her mouth. “Oh my god,” Kate says, though her voice is muffled by the palm of his hand. “You're-”

    “Shh- don't-” Peter hushes her, eyes wide as he dramatically scans their surroundings. Kate immediately recognizes his body language from their numerous ‘missions’, when Spiderman scouts the perimeter for possible threats and danger. “You can't tell anyone.”

    Kate decidedly takes his hand away from her mouth and straightens up. “What the hell? Why are you here?” She warily scans his features. He looks so young. But also like he’s been through some shit because there’s a familiar haunted look in his eyes that reminds Kate of the feeling that has been clinging to her all this time. Still, the suspicion isn’t all gone. “Did you plan this?”

    “No!” Peter shakes his head vehemently. “Why do you think I would- no!” He runs his teeth over his lip and anxiously glances at MJ. In a quieter voice, he says, “Look, I'll explain everything later-”

    “Explain what?” MJ rejoins the conversation now that she has ensured that there are no spiders in her hair.

    They both look at her with blank faces.

    MJ’s brow furrows in thought.

    In a drunken attempt to divert her attention, Kate throws her arms around both of their shoulders and shouts, “Tacos!”

    “Tacos!” Peter joins in, though his smile is nervous.

    “Fuck yeah!” MJ is quick to jump on the hype, her arm snaking around Kate’s shoulders as she begins to tug them down the street. “Tacos!”

    So Kate finds out Spiderman’s true identity and Peter spills everything .

    From his relationship with Tony Stark and how his death impacted his life (there are a lot of tears shed), to the controversy with Mysterio last year, to Doctor Strange and his wizardry.

    They talk for hours. Lucky provides emotional support like he always does, kissing away the tears from Peter’s face on more than one occasion, and allowing the boy to cling to him while he gushes about everything that has happened in this past year. Her dog is more than happy to finally be able to cuddle Peter now that he’s not wearing his suit, and even though Kate wants to have him on her lap because she misses his weight on her, she allows him to do his job as Bestest Boi.

    Kate powers through the nauseating hangover with the aid of water and shawarma, courtesy of Peter himself, and comes to face the flood of memories of the night before.

    Including that very mortifying one-night stand.

    God, I can’t believe I came to the thought of Yelena.

    We should forget about that.

    But my therapist-

    Fuck your therapist.

    Days melt together. Archery becomes her therapy - the ache in her muscles much more pleasant than the ache in her heart that is commonplace after a conversation with her therapist. Her practice hours increase - especially when the insecurity that had unveiled itself in Miami begins to creep up again - and the challenges that she sets herself become more and more difficult.

    Peter is a little startled when he finds the blindfold on her coffee table, but when she explains what it’s for he immediately relaxes and demands a demonstration. She hits the bullseye two times out of ten - less than she would like - but Peter’s awe and encouragements make her feel a little better.

    That being said, nothing really changes between her and Spiderman. Except for the fact that she calls him Peter (or Pete, or Petey, or P) now and their hangouts become more frequent since he doesn’t have to worry about hiding his identity from her.

That part of Kate’s brain is shut down quickly by the innovative drive because a boomerang trick arrow is such a good idea, I don’t know what Clint was thinking.

    Absolute genius, Kate thinks as she admires the prototype boomerang arrow in Peter’s hands.

They bounce ideas off each other quicker than Cassie can get a word in, and while Peter rolls with her sometimes bizarre comments, Cassie looks a little concerned for her mental health.     Kate is thriving.

    Her private investigation business is holding up; the income provides her with a sense of independence that is liberating.

    Her loneliness is cured by Peter’s visits, hang-outs with the LARPers and calls with the Barton’s.

    Therapy is…

    Fuck therapy.

    Her therapist won’t stop telling her that even though things seem like they’re okay on the surface, a lot is going on underneath that Kate isn’t addressing. Kate will make off-handed statements that her therapist will take time to pick apart - like how her refusal to talk or think about her mother’s betrayal is because she’s afraid that she won’t be able to reduce her emotions to logical steps and foresight like she usually does with everything else and-

    Kate is thriving.

The occasional intrusive thought now and then. Kate is fine with it being just that, with the intermittent thought that she would have liked to be friends with Yelena. Just friends. Because there is no time for feelings or dating, or fantasising about hot assassins, especially considering how busy her life has gotten now that she’s healed and back in business. One-night stands are fun and satisfying and less complicated.

    You jinxed it. Again.

    It’s almost midnight when Kate is walking Lucky when someone jumps out of the shadows and tries to take her life.

    She and Lucky apprehend the mystery assassin: Lucky protectively growling and barking, tugging at the person’s leg with his bared teeth, and Kate instinctively using her scarf as temporary handcuffs while she waits for the police to arrive. There is are bruises swelling on her body from where the assailant landed really good kicks and punches. A few cuts to her thick coat where a blade almost impaled her, but other than that, she is fine.

    Totally fine.

    Her hands are shaking when she gets home at seven the following morning, having spent the night at the police precinct while the police did their job. Wendy had arrived at six and helped her get out of there as quickly as possible.

Kate needed to get out of her apartment and clear her head (it was one of those restless nights that brought anxiety and archery hadn’t been able to quell the darkness) and Lucky had been whining at her when she had been putting her boots on. So she gave in to his pleas and attached the leash to his collar.

    “Mom?”

    “Kate.” The voice on the other end of the line crackles through. It takes the breath out of her lungs as if a weight has just been dropped onto her chest. “Oh, baby-”

    Shooting up from the couch, Kate double checks the number. “Mom oh my god-”

    “How are you? Are you okay? Is everything okay? Are you safe?”

    “I'm-” Kate’s brain is trying to catch up - trying to decipher whether this is another dream or if this is reality and her mom is there, on the other end of the line and talking to her. After a year of no contact. “Everything is okay. What-”

    “I'm so sorry baby.” Her mother sounds exactly the same as she did when they last spoke.

    Kate knows because there have been restless nights, where she wakes up from a dreamless sleep and craves the comfort that only her mother can bring her. There are videos online of multiple speeches. She doesn’t let herself forget the sound of her voice. “I'm sorry I haven't been taking your calls, I love you. I love you.”

    It’s difficult to breathe. “I love you too, Mom.”

    “Oh, it's such a relief to hear your voice.” The relief does show in Eleanor’s voice: the sigh that follows her words and the softening of her tone. Kate can almost picture her melting against her seat. “You don't understand how long this year has been…”

Danger shivers through her and phantom pains that her body still remembers from last year begin to tighten her muscles. “A very powerful and dangerous man. The last thing that I wanted to do was to put your life on the line.”

    “Look, the reason why I called…” Eleanor trails off, and Kate can see the way she bites her lip in hesitation. She can see the crease between her eyebrows as she frowns. “I'm going to be placed into witness protection. I have enough information on everything that has been going on in Fisk's circle for the past decade to bargain my way out of this.” Kate almost forgets how to breathe. “The trial is happening in January next year but- he isn't happy.”

    “What?”

    “I'm getting Victoria to send over someone to debrief you on what's going to happen in terms of your security-”

    “Wait- wait, Mom.” She runs a hand through her hair and begins to pace. “I don't- are you in danger?”

    The silence that follows is enough confirmation. Her heart aches in her chest. “I need you to listen to me.”

    Kate’s fingers find her bow perched on its shelf, seeking the comfort of the cool, metallic texture. “You’re in danger, aren’t you?”

    “What are you going to do?”

    What I have to do.

You are my child.” Her voice does that thing it always does when she knows that Kate is going to do the complete opposite of what she wants her to do. “Please... don't do anything stupid.” Kate wraps her bare hands over the rubber grip. “Please, I'm begging you.”

    Oof. Big hurts. Much pains.

    “I love you,” Kate says hoarsely. “But just because I understand why you did what you did…” Her fingers skim the holes on her target - the one on the bright red of the eight points ring she had managed to hit with her eyes closed. “It doesn’t make it okay.”

    “Stay safe, Mom. Use all of that effort to keep yourself safe,” Kate says. “I'll be okay, I promise.”

    “You can't promise me that, Kate.”

    A sharp ache grips at her chest.

    She knows that they’re both thinking about Kate’s father.

    “I know,” Kate says shakily, “but I'm not alone, okay? I have connections too, now.”

    The air around her freezes. She can hear the dread in her mother’s unsteady breaths before she says, “You're still…”

    “Yes.” Confidence pushes her chest forward and lifts her chin up. Kate can’t help the slight pride that she feels at being able to tell her mother that Clint has practically adopted her into his family. “I know you tried to have him killed but he is a good man.” Her heart skips a beat as she adds, “and the assassin you hired is better than that.”

    Kate ignores the prickle of annoyance at her mother’s lack of faith in her, reasoning that she’s just being a parent. “I’ll be okay, Mom, I promise .” She digs her forefinger into the bullseye where multiple arrows have formed a hole. The words feel like cotton in her mouth but she says them anyway. “I love you, okay?”

    “I love you too Kate.” Eleanor’s voice is resigned, and Kate’s breathing steadies at the reassurance that she has won this argument. “I can't wait to see you.”

    Sighing, Kate rests her forehead against the target. “I can't wait to see you too.”

    Eli Bradley shows up again, this time cockily interrupting one of her fights with someone who can give her intel on the Tracksuit Mafia.

    With the increased risk of her living alone when there are people who want her dead or captured out there, Kate has been more careful with her security. She has put her private investigation business on hold for a little while, only taking cases that she can do from home and can finish in one or two days. She and Peter have been trying to look into Fisk since the call from her mother, but even Peter is dealing with his own issues.

    “I don’t like the imbalance in this,” Kate deadpans. “It makes me feel like I’m being treated like a child and I hate it. I am not a child.”

    Yelena sits back on the arm of the couch. Her expression is unreadable but her eyes are steady as they scan over Kate’s body language, which is admittedly defensive as she crosses her arms across her chest. She honestly doesn’t know if Yelena is thinking or if she’s doing that thing that interrogators do to get more information out of people.

    Kate doesn’t deal well with silences.

    “Are we gonna be partners or are you gonna babysit me?” Kate taunts.

    Yelena’s eyebrows lift in surprise. Tilting her chin challengingly, Kate holds the blank gaze. It’s intimidating as hell and Kate wants to make a run for her bow and arrows to defend herself in case the assassin changes her mind about Kate dying any time soon, but she’s getting used to the weight of Yelena’s abrupt silences. Amusement colors Yelena’s features but she holds back a smile. “We’re partners,” she says.

    Kate blinks.

    Oh.

    “Good.”

    Well then.

    Honestly, she’s a little surprised at how quickly Yelena gave in.

    Judging by the chuckle the blonde makes, Yelena thinks her shock is the funniest thing in the world. “Satisfied?” She asks playfully with her stupidly dark red lips and her stupidly smoky eyes.

    I hate her. I hate her so much.

    Even when we win, we still lose.

    But in the end, that’s what Kate likes, right?

    If she wanted to, she could tell Yelena to leave her alone. To get the point across that she doesn’t want her help. But she doesn’t.

    The endless drive to win at their little mental games is addictive. It feeds perfectly into Kate’s competitive nature, and she has a feeling that Yelena enjoys is just as much. Yelena loves winning - she can see it in the victorious grins and smirks that spread across her lips - and she hates losing if her reaction to Eli is anything to go by. (The blonde spent their thirty-minute walk back to the apartment critiquing Eli’s fighting style, which she had apparently seen, as a way to get back at him.) Contrastingly, as much as Kate likes to win, she finds a weird pleasure in losing. In a weirdly masochistic way, she likes the frustration that builds every time she loses to Yelena. It makes her want to fight back twice as hard because she knows that the satisfaction she will feel when she does win will be worth it.

    She takes in the open, welcoming expression on Yelena’s face and softens. Huffing, Kate runs a hand through her hair to dispel the tension in her body and begins to walk in the direction of the kitchen.

    “I have mac and cheese,” she calls over her shoulder to Yelena.

    Totally didn’t buy it in case you came back or anything.

    There’s an adorable noise of excitement from behind her and the sound of Yelena’s eager footsteps. “A woman after my own heart,” she declares dramatically.

    Kate rolls her eyes but doesn’t stop the affectionate glare that forms on her face when Yelena bumps her hip to overtake her. The blonde immediately scours through her cupboards for the familiar box of mac and cheese, instructing Kate to get a pot and put it on the stove. They fall into an easy banter, arguing over nothing in particular and suppressing the smiles swimming beneath the surface.

    -

    Kate convinces Yelena to watch Marley and Me.

    It doesn’t take a lot of convincing, really. All Kate has to do was tell her that she has sweet popcorn on the ready and a fluffy blanket to keep the cold away. Yelena makes a big deal about being Russian and thus being resistant to the cold, but fifteen minutes into the movie she caves. As discreet as possible, the blonde edges closer and closer until eventually, she’s wrapped in Kate’s blanket cocoon. Then she complains about Kate’s lack of heating and admonishes her for the poor insulation of the building.

    Like it’s my fault.

    Ignoring the push and pull of their interactions as they try to familiarize themselves with the dynamic of their friendship, being with Yelena feels easy.

    The overwhelming scent of vanilla and orchids is strangely soothing, and while she isn’t touch-starved - what with the recent rendezvous she’s been having - there’s something about having the smaller woman pressed up against her side that feels like rain in the desert. An inexplicable relief that steadies her breathing. It’s not too dissimilar to how it feels when she melts into the rare silences between her and Peter. The palpable human connection satiates her loneliness.

    Isn’t it weird that you’re watching a movie and eating popcorn with someone who was trying to kill your mentor less than a year ago?

    It’s weirder that your mentor is the Hawkeye. Childhood idol. The reason why archery is such a big part of your life. You’re friends with his children and his wife calls you every week to make sure you’re eating alright. And you’re friends with Spiderman.

    Having spent half of the movie being overly aware of the literal assassin sitting by her side, and the abrupt existential crisis, she isn’t that invested when she tunes back into the story. So when Marley dies, Kate doesn’t cry like she usually does.

    Lucky shifts on the carpet where he is curled up, whimpering like he always does when they watch something where an animal dies. He bumps his head against Yelena’s legs, nipping at the blanket.

    That’s when Kate registers the tremble against her shoulder.

    She looks beside her. Half of Yelena’s face is hidden beneath Kate’s black fluffy blanket, her hands balled into the fabric. Kate wouldn’t think anything of it, but the light from the TV screen reflects particularly brightly in Yelena’s green eyes. Hesitantly, Kate glances back at the screen in time to see the scene carry on. A whimper that doesn’t come from the speakers or Lucky on the floor makes her tense up and she looks back at Yelena beside her.

    Oh my god, is she crying?

    Eyebrows scrunched up, tears slip over Yelena’s cheeks.

    Yeah, she’s crying, dumbass.

    For some reason, Kate’s face decides to blush. She has never seen Yelena show this much emotion at once and it’s making her freak out a little.

    What do I do?

    She feels guilty for putting this movie on knowing the ending, but in all honesty, she had no idea that Yelena would react like this. In her head, the image that she has of Yelena is completely different to the one in front of her right now. A deadly, Black Widow assassin who has an arsenal of weapons on her body at all times, poised and ready to kill. At the same time, Yelena is also the woman swaddled in a fluffy blanket and who has a soft spot for dogs. How could have Kate ever predicted this?

    What does one do when there is an assassin crying on their shoulder?

    What do other people do when you’re crying?

    The memory of Yelena pulling her into a hug over the centre console of a car to console her plays in her head.

    Okay. Okay, you got this.

    Kate makes a humming sound in the back of her throat and uncertainly shifts on the couch.

    As soon as she swings her arm over Yelena’s shoulders, the blonde buries her head into the blanket and lets a quiet sob shudder through her body.

    Oh god.

    Her entire mental image of Yelena is shifting right before her eyes and it’s disorienting. It feels like Kate is dissociating.

    Biting her lip, Kate lightly pulls at Yelena’s shoulder. The blonde allows the pressure to tip her weight into Kate’s welcoming embrace, her face still hidden in the blanket. She squeezes her shoulders in what she hopes is comforting reassurance and places her chin on top of her head. Gradually, Yelena melts further and further into her body until Kate is tipping back against the couch and has a head of blonde resting on her chest.

    She hopes that her heartbeat isn’t too loud.

    They remain like that for the rest of the movie while Yelena’s breathing steadies out, with Kate awkwardly patting the smaller woman’s head every once in a while. She has never been the best at comforting others.

    “Y’know, I never pegged you as the type to cry at movies,” she says when the ending nears, hoping to dispel the thick air.

    Yelena sniffles loudly and emerges from her small shelter. “I would have to be inhuman to not cry at dogs dying,” she says shakily. The couch sways beneath them as Lucky pounces onto the seat beside Yelena and burrows himself into her lap. Yelena cups his face with both of her hands and brings it closer to hers. “Especially when the dog looks like this солнышко …” She presses a firm kiss between his doe eyes. “You're not gonna die, no you're not, you're going to live forever.”

    “I'm glad we agree on something,” Kate laughs. She reaches over with her free hand to wipe the lipstick stain Yelena’s kiss leaves behind on Lucky’s fur.

    Clicking her tongue, Yelena falls back into Kate’s side and purposefully shoves her with her body. “Your taste in drinks is still shit.”

    “Look, Pepsi is practically the same thing as Coke.”

    “No.”

    “The only difference is the label and branding, I swear.”

    “Your taste buds are broken,” Yelena scowls, casually rubbing at her face to rid herself of the tears. An ache spreads across Kate’s chest. The blonde is so confident even after crying into her. “They are two completely different things and you choose the worst out of the two.”

    “You liked my popcorn though,” Kate tries weakly, trying to hide how she admires how quickly Yelena composes herself.

    “Popcorn is always good.” At the reminder, Yelena leans forward to the bowl that had been discarded on the coffee table for another handful of the treat. “Nobody can ruin popcorn.”

    “Oh…” Kate shifts away slightly from the blonde so that she can dramatically look at her, ignoring the judgemental expression that Yelena fixes her with. “Oh, my sweet innocent girl…” She shakes her head and pouts forlornly. “Wait ‘til I introduce you to matcha popcorn.”

    “Ma-” Equally dramatic, Yelena pretends to gag. “Why would anybody do this? Who would do this? You Americans and ruining perfectly good things-”

    “Okay, first things first.” Kate doesn’t bother to hide the affronted frown and instead removes her arm from Yelena’s shoulders. “It’s not an American thing, I’m pretty sure it’s Japanese. Second,” she says, a smile beginning to push onto her lips when she sees the slow-spreading grin on Yelena’s face. Her nose is red, and Kate has to stop herself from saying anything about it. “It's pretty weird tasting, but it's not too bad once you get used to it.”

    Haughtily, Yelena twists her chin away from her and slips a piece of popcorn past her lips. “And you judge me for my mac and cheese,” she says before chewing.

    “Because I can't eat this!” Kate nudges the half-full bowl of sriracha coated mac and cheese abandoned on the edge of the coffee table with her foot. “I like my spices but this is so spicy it's inedible.”

    Yelena exclaims, “Bah!” and dismissively waves a hand at her. “What do you know? You’re weak.”

    What a cliche, Kate thinks affectionately, her smile spreading into a full-blown grin.

    “No, your taste buds are just jacked up.”

    “Shut up and drink your bad soda, you're ruining the movie.”

    “Mhm…”

    But the movie is already over, a fact that both Yelena and Kate ignore as they watch the credits crawl across the screen. The soundtrack plays in the background of their silence, which the blonde slowly begins to melt into. Familiar with Yelena’s touchy personality, Kate welcomes the added weight to her shoulder as the woman rests her head there. After a powerful debate between her left and right brain, Kate allows her head to rest atop the blonde’s.

    -

    Yelena tells her that she has business to attend to before she can fully dedicate herself to their ‘partnership.’ There’s a mirthful glint in her eye when she says ‘partnership’ and Kate knows that she’s trying to annoy her on purpose, but she throws the bottle of siracha at her head regardless of her intentions.

    While Yelena is busy doing god knows what (she wishes that the blonde would tell her where she disappears to all of the time), Kate takes the time to slow down. Allows herself to relax for the first time in three weeks since her mother called.

    Her therapist (they meet on Tuesdays) praises her for making a healthy decision, and Kate should reconsider her life choices if she’s being praised for something so rudimentary. But for once, she does what her therapist tells her to do and tries to learn meditation. She doesn’t know how this meditation thing is supposed to work, but she’s pretty sure that she’s not supposed to be slipping into an existential crisis. So she stops and does sit-ups instead because at least that will keep her mind busy while she’s meditating.

    Clint calls her on a Thursday (a day earlier than their scheduled weekly calls) just to ‘check up’ on her. She lets him know that “yes, Yelena did swing by. She thinks I’m a whore now.

    Thank you for hiring me a babysitter.” Laura makes sure that she’s at least trying to eat healthily and sends her a list of ingredients so that she can make herself a meal plan. Clint later texts her to make sure that she did go out and buy everything on the list. Kate sends him a picture of her standing in front of the copious amount of green products with a grimaced grin and a thumbs up.

    Miraculously sticking to her mental note, she does pick a fight with Eli about the placement of his hickeys and tells him that if he does it again, he will have to say ‘bye bye’ to his dick. Eli asks her to hang out, to which she explicitly states that she isn’t in the mood to do anything sexual with him, so they stay in and watch a movie.

    On Friday evening Lucky accidentally hits her in the face when he gets his zoomies, so she decides to teach him how to retrieve her bow in case of an emergency. He may be an excitable dog, but he is also very intelligent. Grills has taught her how to command him to do tricks and since then, Lucky’s repertoire has expanded from ‘sit’ and ‘lie down’, to ‘dance’ and ‘remote’. Kate treats it as a patience-exercise and they spend almost three hours trying to perfect the trick. By the end of the night, Lucky is exhausted and slumps at the foot of Kate’s bed like his bones are made of jelly.

    A week and a half have passed since Yelena left. Kate has tried to find the blonde’s phone number, but the lack of response lets her know that it’s the wrong one.

    That or Yelena is ghosting her.

    For the sake of her mental health, she concludes that it’s because Yelena doesn’t seem to have a set phone.

    Whatever… not like Kate cares or anything.

    Nope.

    -

    “If it isn't baby Antman - Ant Lady? Ant Girl-”

    A heavy sigh, and then, “How did I forget how insufferable you are?”

    “That's not very nice.” Kate pouts.

    “You reap what you sow, Katie Kate.”

    “Yeah yeah.” Kate taps the speaker button with her knuckle and rolls across her bedroom floor on her wheely chair. “So why is my dearest closest friend calling me?”

    “I wanted to ask what your Christmas plans were.” Cassie’s voice comes through distantly despite her being on speaker.

    “Uh... Well,” Kate says, straining as she reaches for a small metal bolt from one of the trays in her tool stand. “I mean Clint hasn't said anything yet but I know he's gonna invite me to the farm. He's a softie.” She checks that it’s the correct size and drops it back into the tray as she realizes it’s too small. “Doesn't wanna let anyone know how gooey he is on the inside.”

    “From what I heard from my dad, I can buy that.” There’s a crinkling sound. “Tough on the outside, marshmallow on the inside.”

    “Totally,” Kate says distractedly. As she picks out the correct bolt size, she registers that Cassie hasn’t followed up on that in favor of crinkling whatever snack she’s crunching on. She rolls back to her workbench and says, “How is Antman anyway?”

    “I hate that you refuse to call him anything other than that.”

    “It's a respect thing. You should really be calling me Hawkeye.”

    “You are too much of a dork for me to call you that.”

    “Hey!” Kate frowns, both in concentration and in disgruntlement. “Clint is very much a dork. People need to see that.”

    Except nobody does because he has a really bad branding strategy.

    Kate still can’t believe he turned down her idea to put him on a reality TV show.

    “I just don't think I can picture him smiling.”

    Precisely why he needs exposure.

    “Trust me, that man has a wicked sense of humor.” She recalls the moment she reached for an acidic trick arrow and pulled out an arrow that burst out bubbles on impact. Kate does an exaggerated shiver despite Cassie not being able to see her. “He's just an old man now and his back can't handle the retaliation from his pranks.”

    “So the Hawkeye’s are notorious dorks. Glad to know you're carrying on the legacy.”

    Kate perks up. “Speaking of legacies…”

    There’s another heavy sigh. “Hope and my dad are loosening up but…” A crinkle of a bag.

    “Still a lil’ rocky.”

    “Yeah? You’re still grounded?”

    “Why do you think I'm calling you?”

    “Uh- I don't know?” Kate pauses her activities and looks at her phone, affronted. “Because you miss me?”

    “Yeah yeah.”

    She’s even copying my speech patterns.

    “I know you miss me,” Kate teases, a grin on her face as she holds out the small device in her hand under the lamplight. “You probably have my face as your wallpaper.”

    “Technically true.”

    “See?”

    “Look, I'll show you.” Cassie’s voice rises in volume as she most likely reaches for her phone.

    “Glad to know I have the Cassie Lang as my number one fangirl,” Kate says as she puts down the small screwdriver on the table and reaches for her phone instead. She taps on the messaging app and moves to their chat. “That's great for brand- What-”

    Cassie cackles.

    “Cassie, what the fuck is this?” Kate grimaces as she stares at the disturbing image of a monster on her screen. It’s vaguely familiar, but she can’t pinpoint exactly what Sure enough, it’s a screenshot of Cassie’s wallpaper. “What am I looking at?”

    “It's a Demogorgon!”

    Ohhh…

    “I know that!” Kate winces as she uses her thumb and index finger to zoom in on the picture. “But why? This is hideous!”

    “Exactly,” Cassie drawls with a grin showing in her voice. In a higher pitch baby voice, she says, “he’s adorable.”

    “You…” Shivering, Kate exits their chat and puts her phone down again. “You are one fucked up bitch.”

    “Yuh-huh.” The crinkling of a bag of snacks returns and Kate focuses on it rather than the creepy image of the monster from Stranger Things. “You know you're lucky you got to see that. Showing you my vulnerable side or whatever.”

    “Oh god, if this is what our friendship is gonna be I would like a refund.”

    “Hey…” Cassie whines, muffled with whatever she’s eating in her mouth.

    “I'm kidding,” Kate half-sings, quick to reassure Cassie since she knows that the younger girl tends to assume the worst out of all situations. She finds that flattery and reassurance work best when teasing Cassie. “I still need to keep you around so me and Peter don't destroy half of New York.”

    “I don't know how to feel knowing that you two are running around loose without adult supervision.”

    “Like you didn't completely destroy that scaffolding.”

    “I put it back together!”

    “Mhm…” Kate leans in closer to the surface of the workbench, biting her tongue between her teeth as she uses tweezers to clip the small explosive to the pin. There’s a satisfying ‘clack’ as it slides into the small grip. She sighs and leans back in relief. “Ooh!” She jolts forward in excitement as she remembers that Cassie doesn’t know about her recent project. “I got my bomby pins to work!”

    “Oh god-” Cassie cuts herself off with a groan. “No. Who let you do this?”

    “They work! Seriously, I can show you-” Kate swivels in her chair to the small calendar pinned to the wall in front of her workbench, detailing the days when she has cases, therapy, hangout sessions with the LARPers and dinners with Jack. “When are you next in the city?”

    “I don't know,” Cassie says, uncertainty coloring her tone. “Senior year is kinda kicking my butt.”

    “Maybe you can catch a flight before I go to the farm?”

    Cassie hums, the uncertainty carrying through the low voice. “Do I really wanna spend my Christmas break with dumb and dumber?”

    “I’ll have you know-”

    “Okay, we both know I don't need a rundown of all of your great achievements. I know you're weirdly smart.”

    “Jealous?”

    “I wish I were there to hold you at the top of the Empire State Building.”

    “I have more grapple arrows now.”

    “Tch- after last time?” Cassie chuckles that annoying laugh she does every time she goes to purposefully irritate Kate. “I sure hope so.”

    “I hate you.”

    “I love you too~”

    Kate jerks her chin down in a sharp nod, prompting her goggles to come down to shield her eyes. Twisting her wrist to stretch the muscles, Kate adjusts the grip on the soldering iron. “Do you think I could make the boomerang arrows like…”

    “What-” Cassie shouts over the faint hissing sound that most likely is jarring to her ears through the phone. “-are you doing? What's that noise?”

    She shifts her phone away from where she’s working so that the mic doesn’t pick up on her work. “I'm borrowing Peter's tools.”

    “Uh-huh…”

    Kate focuses on soldering a small grip onto another bobby pin.

    “So I heard something.”

    She finishes pushing the grip down before leaning back against the chair. “What did you hear?”

    “Oh, nothing…”

    “No,” Kate whines. “Don't do this to me.”

    “Do what?”

    “That thing you do where you make me ask questions,” Kate huffs impatiently. “Just tell me.”

    “Okay okay.” Cassie rustles with something - Kate thinks she must have finished whatever she was snacking on. “I heard that your assassin friend is back in town.”

    “Oh god…” Kate groans and puts the soldering iron down onto its grip. “How does Peter even know? He's been busy with work.”

    “Apparently Eli told him.”

    Even though Cassie has never met Eli, he comes up during conversation in their group chat when Peter and Cassie demand updates on Fisk. They’re both too wholesome to be outright teasing - or scathing like Yelena - but there are certain uses of emojis whenever Kate mentions their stakeouts or restaurant recommendations that let her know they’re both aware of Eli’s relation to her.

    “Jeez, you guys are like the high society people that like to sit around and gossip at events,” she says.

    “Please,” Cassie scoffs. “I know you, Miss 'High Society' herself, goes crazy for gossip.”

    “Yeah yeah…”

    “So…”

    “So?”

    “Yelena~”

    Kate slumps further down in her chair and digs the heels of her hands into her eyes. “I regret ever telling you.”

    “Well, from the brilliant mood you were in that night you sang rather than told,” Cassie says with a teasing tone, and Kate is glad that she can’t see the shit-eating grin on her face.

    “Don't remind me.”

    It hadn’t been a particularly stellar night - the booze had hit her particularly strong since she skipped lunch and only ate half a bowl of cereal for breakfast. Kate barely remembers talking about Yelena, amongst all of the other crap that she said that night, but the moment that she woke up Cassie pestered her about her crush on the ‘beautiful Black Widow assassin’.

    “It's cute!” Cassie’s voice is abruptly girly, and despite the topic at hand, it makes Kate grin a little. “She makes you all blushy and stuff.”

    “I don't do 'blushy and stuff',” she huffs.

    “Yeah, you do,” Cassie deadpans. “With Yelena~”

    “If that's how you're gonna say her name every time I'm gonna have to hang up.”

    “I'm really liking this side of you.”

    “I don't know what you're talking about.” Kate ignores the heat on her face and ears as she goes to grab the soldering iron again.

    With a hum, Cassie’s tone changes into something a little less teasing and a little more serious. “I didn't know you could care enough about something other than your Hawkeye thing.”

    Kate rolls her eyes. “You're making me sound like an emotionless workaholic.”

    “Okay, I don't know about emotionless, but the workaholic part…”

    “Cassie, do we need to have a conversation? Because it sounds like you have a lot of pent up aggression.”

    “Stop trying to derail the conversation!” And then in a quieter, hissing tone, she says, “I know your little tricks, Katie.”

    “Uh-huh.”

    “So.” Kate sighs heavily at the conspirational tone in Cassie’s voice, already anticipating the ongoing grilling to come. “Anything new in the Yelena department?”

    “Not really, just that she came back to 'help' because of what's happening with Fisk and my mom.”

    “Aw, that's so nice of her-”

    “Cas, no.”

    “What? It is.” Cassie’s tone is dreamy. “It shows she cares.”

    “Yeah, but it also shows that she thinks I'm incapable of taking care of myself.”

    “I wouldn't say that. sometimes when you care, you wanna protect the people you love.”

    Kate fumbles with the soldering iron and shakes the tension from her shoulders. “Okay, I wouldn't say love-”

    “Yeah, maybe love wasn't the right word-”

    Sighing, Kate rubs at the perspiration building at her hairline with the back of her wrist. “I don't know…” She grits her teeth as her concentration wavers, willing herself to focus on soldering the grip firmly onto the slim width of the bobby pin. “It's just- the way that she talks to me, it's like…”

    “Like she's talking down to you?” Cassie supplies for her.

    “Yeah.” Kate blows a puff of air through her lips. “It's so irritating.”

    “Well I mean... she is a Black Widow,” Cassie says tentatively. “She's been doing this her whole life, meanwhile we're fresh out of the oven and already you're stepping on the Kingpin's toes.”

    Kate frowns. “I'm not that new to it-”

    “But this industry is her life, right?” There’s curiosity to the younger girl’s tone, as well as a softness to it that has Kate wondering if Cassie is actively trying to redeem Yelena in Kate’s eyes. It’s surprisingly endearing, considering that Cassie has never met Yelena before and she’s already trying to justify her actions to Kate. “Trained assassin and all that fun stuff…”

    “'Fun stuff'.”

    “I don’t mean to be insensitive, it's just-”

    Kate understands it. The need to make light of a situation that dark.

    “It’s a lot, right?” Cassie says. “I'm not sure if you read the files that were released but the Black Widow program was... messed up.”

    At the reminder of the Red Room, Kate refrains from pressing the solder into the iron just to control the spike of anger that surges through her. The protectiveness that she feels toward Yelena isn’t too disarming. There is a strange kinship between them forged mainly by the underlying impact loss has had on each of their lives.

    “I didn't read them,” Kate says finally.

    Even though she barely knows anything about it, Yelena’s past is a big part of why she is the way that she is. Kate may feel frustrated that Yelena isn’t more open with her but that is largely due to the part of her that drives her to know everything there is to know under the sun, paired with her poor relationship with patience.

    “What?” Surprise tinges the younger girl’s tone. “Why not?”

    “It feels... wrong.” Kate puts down the soldering iron and leaves the bobby pin to cool down. “You know?” She wipes her forehead again with the back of her hand. “Like I'm invading Yelena’s privacy?”

    “Okay... I get that.” Cassie hesitantly pries more. “But at the same time, like, if it helps you understand her better then why not? “

    “I know but it would feel like I'm cheating my way through building a friendship with her.” Kate purses her lips, trying to find an adequate way to get her point across. “Like, if all of your trauma was just out there on the Internet and I read it all. Everything. It would be... wrong.” She frowns at just the thought of invading Yelena’s privacy like many people in the past have most likely done before. “Like going behind her back.”

    “Yeah... okay,” Cassie says hesitantly, “yeah, I can respect that.”

    “Yeah. I don't know,” Kate sighs. “It just goes against my morals.”

    “I know.” There’s a small pause as they both try to process everything. “Still, Yelena is there because she cares, right? So like…” Cassie chuckles a little. “Take your win and leave.”

    Kate laughs, shaking her head a little. Trying to reason with all of the insecurities in her mind and convince herself that she needs to keep being patient with Yelena if she wants a friendship with her, and actually voicing the reasoning out loud for her to hear is much more convincing. Even Cassie’s encouragement to understand Yelena is comforting.

    “I hate that you're my voice of reason,” Kate grumbles, distractedly picking at the numerous scattered pages of paper on the table.

    “You need someone there to let you know when you're letting that Gryffindor pride get in your way.”

    “You are such a nerd.”

    “Uh-huh.”

    “Are we still on for that Harry Potter marathon?”

    “You bet your ass, Hawkeye.”

    -

    Archery practice is getting more and more competitive for her with the challenges that Clint is setting her. He gifts her a life-size dummy color coded with all of the different targets of the body that can kill, incapacitate or down someone and tells her to practise shooting within close range.

    Her ‘sweat and salty’ playlist blares loudly in her earphones, the bass rattling pleasantly in her brain. True to the name of the playlist, her body is perspiring only an hour into the workout. She ducks and weaves around imaginary attacks, the strain of her muscles as she reaches for the arrows at her quiver and pulls them back with her bowstring burning up her body. When her quiver empties, she retracts the arrows decorating the dummy and starts the process all over again.

    Another hour into the workout and her shirt comes off despite the outside temperatures dropping significantly as of late. When she slides it over the top of her head she feels the moisture accumulated in the fabric and grimaces. The combination of cardio and archery always has her working harder than usual.

    Her body is burning up from the exercise so the goosebumps that coat her skin barely faze her. It’s almost freeing, feeling the cold wisps of air that brush on her perspiring skin as she continues to practice.

    Just as another song begins playing, there is a harsh ‘beep beep’ that interrupts her focus.

    Startled, Kate takes an arrow from her quiver and slides it onto the shelf. As she spins around, her sore fingers rapidly pull back the bowstring. It must be the heat of her muscles and the world that she is wrapped up in, but her fingers release the string.
