    “I remember.”

    Kate squints at Yelena. There’s something about her too-innocent expression - wide eyes and pout curved around her straw - that irks her. It makes her feel like she has to defend herself. “He’s sending people after me and my mother,” she says, almost aggressively as the defensiveness jumps out. “I need to get him out in the open.”

    Humming, the blonde nods and absent-mindedly nudges Kate’s leg with her elbow. “And when you get him out in the open, then what?”

    “I get the police on him.”

    “And if he has people working for him within the police?”

    “The LARPers are already helping me find dirt on him, and Peter is working as a journalist so he won’t be able to slither out of the public’s eye so easily.”

    Yelena narrows her eyes. “He is a powerful man.”

    “I have a powerful team,” Kate snips back, narrowing her eyes in response.

    “Yes,” Yelena says forlornly, “the boy whose muscle went to his body instead of his brain is certainly going to help.”

    Kate rolls her eyes so hard that her body actually falls to the side, collapsing into Yelena in the form of a shove to convey her annoyance. “I know you’re not a big fan of Eli but I’m gonna need you to at least make an effort to play nice if you’re planning to help.” Glaring at her as severely as she can (which isn’t very severe because Yelena is wearing her clothes again, looking soft as fuck because she looks tiny in them and it’s throwing her for a loop) Kate holds a finger in front of her face. “I don’t need you two bickering while we’re trying to stop the Tracksuit Mafia from receiving their firearms.”

    The blonde backhands Kate’s finger away but she smiles placidly and quips, “You got it Hawkeye.”

    Blankly staring into Yelena’s mirthful gaze, Kate tries to find what exactly is making her feel so uneasy. Like her heart is falling out of her chest. Like her mind is melting under the weight of her attention. Like there’s a small wildfire crawling underneath her skin.

    Pull yourself together, Bishop! You cannot be this whipped for her.

    Clearing her throat, Kate hops off the counter and tugs at Yelena’s shirt (Kate’s shirt) to get her to follow. As soon as they sit down on the couch, Kate pulls up the files on her tablet and begins to dive in.

    “Okay, this is everything that we have so far…”

    -

    Living with Yelena is certainly… an experience.

    In all honesty, Kate doesn’t know how to describe what the chaos of the last four days are, but it is certainly eye-opening and Kate is learning a lot about her crush. So much so that she has started compiling a list of things that she now knows about Yelena that initially had her doing a double-take and her inner monologue going, wait what? A full list would be provided, but it would take far too long and Kate has a very bad habit of going over the word count on topics that she is passionate about. (Just ask her university professors.)

    Though if somebody held a gun to her head and she had to provide a list of the 'Top Three Most Surprising Things About Your Roommate Assassin', Kate would probably have to narrow them down to:

    1. Yelena has a sweet tooth. (It's somehow worse than Kate’s, which is very ironic considering all of the jokes she has made about Kate being on the road to diabetes.)

    2. Yelena is messy. (As much as she keeps the placement of her weapons organized, her neatness does not seem to translate to her living space. The mess in Kate’s apartment has doubled now, and she's seriously considering starting a chore wheel because of it.)

    3. Yelena is somehow more erratic than Kate is: one second she'll be monopolizing the TV remote, the next she'll be using Kate’s target to practise her knife throwing. Kate struggles to pinpoint what exactly the blonde thinks a routine is with the sporadic changes in activities.

    4. Yelena is a kleptomaniac. (This one is under Investigation; Kate needs more proof.)

    5. Yelena is an amazing cook.

    This is only barely chipping away at the iceberg of Things To Know About Yelena Belova, and they have only been living together for four days. Four very long, very confusing, very entertaining days.

    Good or bad, Kate hasn’t decided what she thinks of this living situation yet, but she’s glad that she’s able to see the inner workings of the Black Widow’s brain. It keeps her on her toes and adds a new dynamic to her life that she welcomes with open arms. The monotony of the previous three weeks had been slowly chipping away at the restlessness inherently embedded in her system, and Yelena’s very prominent presence in her life has certainly shaken everything up.

    It also helps that you have a devastating crush on her.

    And that.

    Kate is trying to come to terms with her ( blergh ) feelings for the blonde.

    Keyword being: trying.

    At the same time as she unpacks her feelings and addresses them in her head, she compartmentalizes them and tucks them away in a neat little box where she can feel like she's a little bit in control. Because while living with Yelena is helping her see just how unexpectedly adorably weird the blonde is, it's also helping her see that pursuing anything more than a friendship with her would most likely end in devastation for either one or both of them.

    And so Kate makes a list of the 'Top Three Reasons Why Dating Your Roommate Assassin Is A Bad Idea': 1. Yelena is the first woman Kate has realized that she is attracted to beyond the casual 'she's really pretty I wish I liked women' sentiment.

    2. Her goal to become an Avenger and be a better Hawkeye doesn't need to be muddled by her feelings for the assassin whose sister was an Avenger, and who also tried to kill an Avenger not even a year ago.

    3. Kate can barely handle herself when she's single, how is she supposed to handle herself in a relationship? The last thing she wants to do is put her baggage on someone like Yelena who probably already has her own thing to deal with. (See: ex-Black Widow under mind control who is living her own belated self-realization journey.)

    4. Sure they have chemistry, and on more than one occasion Kate has felt the sexual attraction reciprocated, but does Yelena even have romantic feelings for her? Can she have feelings for her? Is Kate just assuming her sexuality?

    And so, we as Kate Bishop's inner monologue conclude that pursuing a romantic relationship with ‘Roommate Assassin’ Yelena Belova over the awesome friendship that we have now, would be dumb.

    On a more serious note, the more that she familiarizes herself with what it means to be friends with Yelena the more she realizes that she doesn’t want to ever lose it. Her friendship with Yelena is quickly becoming something that she holds close to her heart. There's something there that feels natural. Kate doesn't even have to try and they're already compatible. It's easy, and simple, and it honest to god feels like destiny. ( That is so cheesy. ) If it comes down to it, she’ll take a friendship like the one that she has now over anything that could potentially end up with them being broken ex-lovers.

    That being said, it doesn't mean that Kate can merely logic away her crush.

    Does her heartbeat out of her chest every time Yelena invades her personal space? Yes.

    Does she still have intrusive thoughts about pinning the blonde to the kitchen counter and having her come undone at her touch? Unfortunately yes and it's making it very difficult to pretend that she isn't sexually frustrated.

    Do the deep, introspective conversations at midnight when Kate can't sleep make her feel weirdly domestic? You bet. She is a sucker for outlandish conversation that ultimately means nothing in the grand scheme of things, and Yelena, apparently, is the worst type of enabler and instigator. Sometimes it’s questions about what happened to people who were on planes when the Blip happened, other times it's theories about the afterlife and whether every being in the universe goes to the same place or if they have their own little Heaven to go to. Kate doesn't know whether to hate her for it or to embrace the feeling of free-falling when she looks at the time and finds that it's three in the morning and for once she feels like she can fall asleep now that the chaos in her brain is placated by the chaos of their conversations.

    All in all, Yelena’s presence in her apartment is strangely fitting. It fills a void within her life that Kate didn't know needed to be filled, and she can't say that she hates it.

    -

    One of the cases that she thought she could solve in a couple of days drags on longer than it should have, and when she realizes that she has to head out of the city to try to solve it, she does what any other rational and sane person would do: she screams into her pillow. Which is an interesting scene that Yelena walks into, and Kate has to assure her that the activity is more cathartic than it looks.

    Kate somehow convinces Yelena to try it out, which leads to a screaming match and ends with Yelena claiming one of Kate’s pillows as her own. Kate is more than happy to hand it off considering that the blonde has sufficiently claimed it by screaming into it. (Kate’s stomach hurts from the laughter by the end of the night, and judging by Yelena’s relaxed posture and flushed cheeks, she thinks that her new roommate feels the same. It’s a feeling that she doesn’t want to forget so she stores it in her mind and locks it away.)

    Reluctantly, Kate finishes the case and takes the time and space away from Yelena to once again, take her emotions and rationalize them in the context of friendship.

    When Kate arrives at the apartment again, exhausted from having pulled an all-nighter and caught up in trying to rid herself of the tension pulling her shoulders tight, she is more than ready to crash into her bed and sleep for days.

    Yes. Sleep. It is so underrated. Why don’t people do it more often? I should do it more often. Why don’t I?

    Insomnia. Nightmares. Messed up sleep schedule.

    …

    The groan that Kate pulls out of the pit of her stomach sounds like a wailing cat. She’s too tired to bother with the pleasantries of forming a sentence, like: ‘Yelena, I’m back!’ or ‘Home sweet home!’

    She drops her bag in the hallway and kicks off her boots which make her feet feel like they’re three seconds away from exploding from the pressure. Yelena says something to her, but it doesn’t register in her sleep-deprived brain. She slumps her way through the apartment, barely noticing Lucky bouncing towards her to loyally walk by her side as she trudges up the stairs. The texture of his wet nose on her fingertips as he reassures her helps bring her conscience back to earth, and she mumbles unintelligible nonsense at him that he definitely understands via telepathy.

    Her intentions had been to jump in the shower before anything else, but the sight of her pillows and covers crumpled up on her bed resemble soft, airy clouds in the sky and… well. No one should expect so much from one twenty-three-year-old girl.

    Soon to be twenty-four~

    Damn, I’m getting old.

    That’s Kate’s first coherent thought when she jolts awake.

    What… when…

    She sputters as she swipes the hair from her mouth, blearily blinking as she surveys her bedroom. It’s still dark outside, though the dull roar of the city is slightly quieter when there aren’t people shouting obscenities at each other across the street, meaning that it’s most likely well into the evening. Meaning that Kate’s takeout options are significantly smaller now that most of the places have closed for the night.

    Kate groans and sinks back into her pillows.

    Woe is me.

    This pillow smells really good.

    Kate burrows into the covers. The tension in her body melts as her muscles fall apart and decompose and Kate really feels like she could lay in her bed forever and wait for the earth to take her and turn her body into compost for flowers to grow.

    Maybe orchids.

    Hm…

    Yelena.

    She shoots up from the bed, heat already beginning to rise to her face as she realizes exactly what she was (albeit unknowingly) doing. Yelena’s scent is all over her pillows and duvet and covers and Kate had been burrowing into them and sniffing like an addict. She was only gone for a night and already the overwhelming scent of vanilla and orchids permeates her bed.

    Sighing, Kate forces herself into the shower (ignoring the new addition of Yelena’s many hair and body products) though forgoes her skin routine when her stomach grumbles in protest.

    The clattering of the metallic frame as Kate slumps down the stairs alerts both Lucky and Yelena of her arrival.

    They’re both curled up on the couch, watching her quietly. Lucky’s head drops to his paws, though he continues to trace her movements with a sleepy gaze. His body is nuzzled close to Yelena’s legs, which are folded against her chest with an open book resting on her knees.

    Dark emerald glitters in amusement as Kate yawns. Her golden hair burns in the warm orange light of a lamp that the assassin had dragged home just before Kate left for her case, and Kate has to rub her eye to make sure what she’s seeing is real. That it’s not an illusion.

    “Are those…” Kate gestures vaguely at Yelena’s person. “Reading glasses?”

    Perking an eyebrow over golden rimmed glasses, Yelena leans against the back of the couch as Kate continues to shuffle her way across the room. “Yes,” she says, voice only slightly hoarse. “You are so smart, do you want a cookie?”

    Kate scoffs, briefly pausing on her journey to the kitchen to look down at Yelena. “Screw you.”

    “Hm, you're very rude, Kate Bishop,” Yelena smirks, reaching out to tug at Kate’s sweatpants playfully. “No, ‘thank you, Yelena, for the delicious cookies’?”

    “Not when you're being a sarcastic asshole,” she says, though it lacks bite as Yelena hands her a triple chocolate chip cookie that looks way too professional to have been homemade.

    But there’s flour dusting the kitchen counters and an empty packet of chocolate chips on the coffee table as evidence for Yelena’s baking.

    It must be the warm lighting because Yelena’s eyes are molten and it’s making Kate feel horribly domestic. “Oh, Kate, you wound me,” she drawls with that ridiculously attractive Russian accent.

    “Thanks for the cookies,” Kate mumbles through a mouthful of heavenly chocolate goodness.

    Her eyes water from the relief of finally ingesting food. “And for looking after Lucky.”

    “We had fun.” Yelena continues to tug at Kate’s sweatpants, urging her to sit down. Which, in her drowsy state, Kate does. “There was a doggy date,” she says, shifting slightly so that Kate has space to sprawl herself comfortably beside her. “He has a new best friend. Thanos.”

    “What?” Kate frowns, thinking that she has misheard her.

    “Mhm.” Yelena nods with a soft smile on her face as her fingers card through Kate’s hair attempting to detangle the tresses.

    “Like-” She takes the time to swallow the cookie in her mouth, very aware that there’s most likely chocolate around her mouth. She nods as she does so, humming appreciatively as she really registers that Yelena has baked cookies for her - though not necessarily for her but still. It’s an adorable image to think about and Kate is kind of sad that she wasn’t there to see it. And then her mind snaps back to the topic at hand and Kate’s eyebrows furrow. “Thanos?

    Like the guy that literally blipped half of the universe? The fucker who killed Iron Man?”

    “Mm.” Yelena’s lips twist down as she tries to stop the smile on her face. “Beautiful bull terrier.”

    “No.” Kate’s jaw drops. “No way?”

    Laughing softly, Yelena continues to nod and detangle the knots in her hair, her fingers expertly twisting around the difficult parts. “I almost killed the man who owns him,” she rasps quietly, as if unwilling to disturb the rare silence of a New York apartment. “I was so close.”

    “You should have done it.”

    “Wow,” Yelena says as her eyebrows shoot up.

    Around another mouthful of cookie, Kate mumbles, “What?”

    “Nothing.” The blonde shakes her head almost affectionately, her eyes dancing across her face with open amusement. “I just thought you loved purple.”

    “I-” Kate blinks. Tries to process the joke because Yelena’s green eyes are glittering with that mischievous light to them so she definitely made a joke. And then she thinks about the pictures and videos people had taken of the big, alien guy who had destroyed trillions of people’s lives, and her eyes widen. “Fuck you!” She shouts as she shoves Yelena’s shoulder. There’s laughter in her voice, however, because Yelena is a dork who makes horrible jokes. “Oh my god…”

    Yelena giggles.

    Giggles.

    Keep it cool Kate. Keep it cool…

    Shaking her head, Kate tears the remaining bits of her cookie in half. “I'm gonna have to switch dog parks…” She hands one half to Yelena, who takes it from her hand with her mouth since one hand is busy detangling Kate’s hair and the other is holding her book open. “I'm not having Lucky around a dog named after the biggest fucker in the universe.”

    Yelena exhales through her nose, her lips curled up in a very amused grin as she watches Kate. “I have never heard you so angry,” she mumbles, still chewing the part of the cookie Kate handed her.

    “Yeah, well…” Kate shrugs, her momentary burst of outrage dissipating and leaving her feeling like jelly again. She sinks back into the couch, leaning her head into Yelena’s hand where it’s now brushing through the untangled tresses. It’s incredibly soothing, but she can’t fully enjoy it when her heart is skipping erratically in her chest at the same time.

    Humming, Yelena closes the book and places it on the coffee table. When she leans back against the couch, her head rests beside Kate’s and her eyes drowsily meet hers. “There, there.” The hand in Kate’s hair gently pats her head. “It's alright.”

    The perfect woman.

    Perfection.

    Why are we pretending like we don’t have feelings again?

    Left brain hits replay on her 'Top Three Reasons Why Dating Your Roommate Assassin Is A Bad Idea' list.

    Oh~ okay. Okay. Cool

    Your list is stupid!

    Kate clears her throat to distract from the overwhelming feeling that’s hitting her like a wave. “You wanna order some pizza?” She asks, her stomach tightening with hunger at the mere thought of her favorite food.

    “No,” Yelena says, “We already ate.”

    “I’ll get me some,” she says, hoisting herself up with the intention of grabbing her phone and calling the nearest twenty-four-hour pizza place.

    “No-” Yelena’s tone turns scolding as she reaches for Kate’s elbow. There’s a disapproving expression on her face - eyebrows raised and a ‘really?’ look in her eyes - that has Kate smiling a little. “I will heat up the leftovers and you will eat it and say thank you.”

    “Fine, but only because you're an amazing cook,” Kate chuckles, letting Yelena guide her towards the kitchen. “You would make a great housewife, y'know?”

    A startled laugh rasps out of the blonde. “Is that so?”

    “Yes.” Kate nods vehemently, walking herself to the space on the counter she always sits on. “You can cook, you can look after my child, you can kill someone with just your pinky finger-”

    “Kate Bish- op.” Yelena spins from where she’s pulling out a large bowl (that’s a new bowl) of food that has Kate’s stomach audibly rumbling. Her voice is low and shamelessly suggestive as her eyes dance over Kate’s features. The dark green shines in the kitchen LED lights and sends a shiver running down the archer’s spine. Lips lifting into an amused smirk, Yelena says, “are you implying that I am your housewife?”

    A grin that is more confident than Kate feels spreads across her lips. “The perfect housewife,” she says.

    The blonde stares for a moment. Says nothing and just stares . And then her smirk widens into a full-blown grin. “Thank you, darling,” she says, patting Kate’s knee as she moves towards the microwave.

    “No problem, sweetheart,” Kate swings back, a lazy smile on her face. “How was my bed while I was gone?”

    “Very, very comfortable.” Yelena nods appreciatively as she leans back on the kitchen counter across from Kate, and the latter grins at the way that the woman taps her finger against her mouth like she’s reviewing something serious. “I like where you placed it in the room - good feng shui. The material of your covers and pillows were good too. You have too many pillows though.”

    Gasping dramatically, Kate presses a hand to her chest. “I'm gonna pretend I didn't hear you say that.”

    “I learned that I am on the losing end of the deal here.”

    “Well, you are the guest.”

    “The guest is supposed to receive the best, no?” The blonde arcs an eyebrow. “Were you taught no manners?”

    Kate shrugs. “A hero never rests.”

    At that, Yelena’s face falls and the easiness in the air gets sucked out of the room. “Do not call me that,” she glowers, her tone abruptly dangerous. However, now that she is more familiar with Yelena’s softer side, Kate doesn’t cower away. Subconsciously, Kate has been keeping track of everything that she needs to know about making Yelena as comfortable as possible with her. One of the biggest benefits of living with the assassin is getting to know her mannerisms, her boundaries and her defence mechanisms.

    In a softer, but still light-hearted tone, she says, “A hero?”

    Predictably, Yelena says, “I am not a hero.”

    Kate frowns. “Of course, you are.” She is entirely honest when she says it because even though Miami was a psychological mess, it also helped her see a little of what Yelena does for the other Black Widows. “You're saving so many people.”

    Yelena scowls, looking away from her and towards the microwave timer. “I kill even more.”

    There was a shift within Kate after Miami. Getting shot twice and almost meeting death put things into perspective for her, especially with the added stress of Fisk sending multiple people after her. So she means it when she shrugs and says, “Whatever, all I know is that you're really fucking cool and you're a hero to me.”

    “Yes well,” Yelena’s upper lip raises in mild disgust, “Clint Barton is on your list of heroes so I am not too surprised.”

    The familiarity of Yelena’s hostility toward her mentor is strangely comforting. “I thought you liked Clint now?” She laughs, eyebrows raised.

    Yelena’s features melt at the sound of Kate’s laugh, but she keeps her expression stoic. “What on earth made you think that?” She asks, accent thickening around the words.

    “You didn't kill him?”

    “Just because I do not kill somebody doesn't mean I like them.”

    Kate chuckles. “It's a good thing you actually like me.”

    Purposefully hesitant, Yelena perks her eyebrows at her. “Hm.”

    Rolling her eyes, Kate kicks in Yelena’s direction but doesn’t try again when it doesn’t land. “I know you do.” There’s a mischievous smirk on her face that only deepens when the blonde’s eyes curve into a faint smile, pleased that she managed to navigate their conversation into comfortable territory. “Deep down in that secretive little assassin heart of yours, I know you like me.”

    The microwave beeps loudly, and Kate’s stomach grumbles at the reminder of food. “If it helps you dream at night,” the assassin says as she turns to retrieve the bowl.

    “'Sleep at night',” Kate corrects with a grin, unable to keep the amusement from her tone. “Ouch!” She flinches away from where Yelena has whipped her shin with a kitchen towel, mouth hanging wide open as she glares at her. “What the hell?”

    “Give me a plate,” Yelena orders with a casual wave of the hand.

    Hopping off the counter, Kate chirps, “Yes, dearest.”

    Yelena shakes her head but Kate can see the smile on her lips. The archer waits patiently for her to plate the food, and grins widely when the plate is handed to her with an, “Enjoy.”

    -

    When the day arrives to ambush the Tracksuit Mafia, Kate is feeling forcibly optimistic.

    Meaning that Kate takes half an hour that morning to do a light workout to get herself in the right mindset because she knows - she knows that things are not going to go exactly as she hopes. She takes Lucky on a short run to get the nervous energy out of her body, though Lucky doesn’t seem to be very enthusiastic about the exercise because he lags behind her a little. Even after the workout and the run, though, there’s an uncomfortable knot in her stomach. So despite her equipment being already prepared from the night before, she double- checks all of the arrows in her quiver and cleans her sword another time.

    Yelena seems to have a similar idea because she wakes up at the crack of dawn to begin her routine. Kate doesn’t have it in her to (discretely) watch her work out like she normally does.

    They barely exchange words outside of discussing what chores need to be done, the palpable tension as the nerves build too suffocating to have a conversation.

    Something about Yelena’s quiet mood tells her that she needs to soften the battlefield, so Kate goes ahead and meets Eli before they are scheduled to stakeout the suspected location. She picks up some coffee and a bagel and greets him with a toothy grin that has him rolling his eyes and slinging his arm over her shoulders. They talk about nothing at first. (Or the most recent basketball game Eli’s favorite team played, but that’s the equivalent of nothing to Kate.) After some coffee and food, they finally feel ready enough to start talking about the plan, agreeing on what needs to be done and what shouldn’t be done.

    By the time that they head to the transaction point (a blocked off backstreet in Chinatown), Eli is in a good mood and Kate feels a little more relaxed.

    Until she sees Yelena.

    Yelena is in serious assassin mode. The warm smiles and soft eyes are long gone. Instead, Kate is greeted by the Yelena that she experienced in Miami and on that rooftop at Christmas. A stoic expression and calculating jade eyes greet them as they approach her, and she feels Eli’s shoulders tighten beside her. Kate does her best to reassure him by placing her hand on his shoulder blade, but even she feels the shiver that Yelena’s intimidating stance shoots down her spine.

    Working with Yelena in Miami had felt like she was constantly trying to prove herself, though she supposes that her mental state at that point in time hadn’t been at its peak. Today’s Kate has a therapist now! Things should be better.

    And they are.

    Maybe it was Miami, or maybe it’s the roommate situation, but they work together like they’ve been doing this for years. Their natural chemistry shines through in their interactions. It fuels the way they bounce ideas off each other, and there are some side comments Kate makes that have Yelena breaking her ‘deadly assassin’ facade and glancing at her in amusement. Not only that, but Yelena is surprisingly receptive to what Kate has to say. It only takes fifteen minutes for Kate to realize that the blonde is making a conscious effort to be a team player, because there are moments where she opens her mouth to disagree with Kate or Eli but thinks better of it.

    Kate sends her a grin both in reassurance and gratitude.

    Eli, on the other hand, is feeling defensive. Kate understands, but it doesn’t mean that she appreciates it. His tone is more authoritative than usual, and he’s definitely overcompensating by interrupting them halfway through their sentences. Kate isn’t an expert, but Eli’s body language is mildly aggressive like he’s trying to remind them (and himself) that he’s the one with super strength.

    Eli is not in a good mood.

    It’s okay though because they allow Eli to have his room to flex his muscles and just make sure that they all know the plan.

    It’s okay. This is gonna be okay. We’re just gonna wait for the dealers to leave, and then we’ll apprehend the TSM guys. Easy.

    …

    We need to have a conversation about jinxing things.

    Things do not go to plan.

    Three hours into their stakeout, the dealers arrive. There are a lot of ‘bro’s being thrown around and Kate can’t really stop herself from smiling at the familiarity of it all, because the Tracksuit Mafia may be connected to Fisk, but Kate has to admit that they are her favorite criminals to have random, mid-fight conversation with.

    Everything seems to be going well: TSM have the cash, and the dealers have the firearms. It’s a smooth transaction.

    Until one of the TSM guys suggests that they do shots in celebration for a good deal. Kate really doesn’t think that the dealers will accept the offer, because it sounds like something only she would do and it’s slightly endearing that the TSM think that everyone else will be the same as she is with them-

    The dealers accept.

    They actually accept.

    Her heart breaks in her chest as she watches the bros break out into cheers and a pitiful rendition of ‘When I Was Your Man’ plays in her head.

    She hears Yelena mutter something in Russian in her earpiece.

    Me too, ‘Lena, me too.

    Kate clutches her bow a little tighter when the TSM guys start looking for something to drink, because Eli is tucked away behind the stack of liquor crates. How inconvenient.

    It’s a little nerve-wracking, but as long as he doesn’t move or reveal himself then they can stick to the plan.

    Eli jumps the gun.

    … I probably should have seen that coming.

    Probably antsy from waiting three hours doing nothing and anxious over potentially being discovered, Eli acts before anybody else can and shoves the stack of crates onto the TSM guy approaching his location. There are curses spewing from Kate’s lips as she pushes herself up from her crouching position, instinctively nocking an arrow. She has a feeling that the ranting in her earpiece is Yelena doing the same but in Russian.

    Shouting ensues as the dealers pull out their weapons and aim at the TSM guys. The TSM guys are busy trying to catch Eli. Eli is getting ahead of himself and trying to fight eight people at once.

    Everything has gone to shit.

    Kate releases the arrow. It pierces into a cardboard box and spikes flare outward. She can hear Eli shouting curses both in the distance and in her earpiece as he tries to dodge them (serves him right, dumbass), hoisting the body of a smaller TSM bro in front of him to serve as a shield.

    However, there are multiple cries of “Hawkeye!” and “It’s Kate Bishop bro!” and she knows that her position has been given away.

    The dealers, now catching onto the fact that TSM hasn’t double-crossed them and there’s a third party involved, follow the pointing fingers and turn to look at where she’s standing. Kate nocks a net arrow and hisses, “What happened to the plan, Eli? ” as she lets her fingers fly off the string. With a flurry and loud whipping sound, the tip of the trick arrow unfolds and unleashes the net, catching all five men in the web within seconds.

    “They were going to find- oof!”

    As Kate runs along the roof of the apartment building to change her position, she sees Eli slide across the street.

    With the super-soldier briefly incapacitated, multiple TSM guys redirect their attention towards Kate, shouting a mixture of greetings and curses that have her holding back a smile.

    There’s a blur of green and black and Yelena is pouncing on them before they can raise their new rifles at Kate. Not that the archer needs the help as she slides arrows and nocks them before any of her victims have time to drop. There are flashes of red electricity decorating the men around Yelena. Kate sees one of them go to smash a hammer over Yelena’s head (classic TSM move, to carry around a hammer and pretend like it’s part of his moving company ensemble), and she hits him with an electro-arrow before he even has the time to swing his arm down.

    The assassin must hear the buzz sizzling the air behind her because she turns as soon as she knocks out a female TSM member ( glad to see they’re diversifying ). When she sees the blue crackle of electricity and the arrow protruding from the TSM guy’s chest, she pointedly spins to look up at where Kate is standing.

    Feeling cocky and pleased with herself, Kate raises her hand and wriggles her fingers playfully at the Black Widow. “You’re welcome,” she says, knowing that Yelena can hear her through the earpiece.

    It makes Kate even cockier.

    It’s a little exhilarating, and the adrenaline prickles at her skin.

    The dealers are back in the fight after detangling themselves from Kate’s net, though her bird’s eye view lets her have the advantage. Eli is a super-soldier, so it’s not like he struggles as much as anybody else would when fighting off five armed people at once. And with Yelena’s help, the TSM guys begin to drop like flies.

    And then there’s a gunshot fired that is a little closer than Kate anticipated.

    “Guys, I think there’s someone on the roof with me,” she says in a hushed voice, as she flinches down from where she’s standing on the ledge of the building. The only answers that she gets are grunts from Eli followed by a series of gunshots, and an inquisitive sound from Yelena. Kate turns to look behind her, scouring the dark roof for its source. It’s only when she sees a bullet ricochet on the metal of the ventilation pipe and bounce up to miss her head that she thinks it’s a better idea to get down instead of risking a close combat fight.

    “I’m good. Just letting you know that I’ll be joining the party downstairs,” she breathes as she nocks a grapple arrow. “Save me a spot.”

    Missions with Cassie and Peter may have helped her grow more comfortable with heights, but her stomach still swoops out of her body and hangs onto the edge of the roof as she jumps off the building.

    This is where things get a little messy. ( *Messier. Things get messi er . )

(Cookie dough, of course, the best flavor.) She feels pretty cool, really, as she twists her hips mid-air like she practised and hears the crunch of the red brick when the grapple digs its grip into the wall.

    Fuck.

    Can we hear anything?

    Weakly, she looks down to see whether her knees can take the rest of the fall and releases the cable from her bow when she knows that she’ll be able to stick the landing. As soon as she does, absorbing the fall by rolling forward, she hears the faint ringing in her ears.

    Kate sees a shadow charging towards her and reflexively moves to swing her bow at them.

    But before they can reach her, they’re lifted off the ground and launched into a dumpster.

Kate doesn’t know because she can’t fucking hear and it’s making her panic a little because she accidentally told Clint that she had been ‘pleasuring herself’ a lot in sign language last week, and that had been a very awkward conversation that Kate does not want a repeat of.

    Oh, thank god, at least I can hear myself.

    His face is scrunched with concern as he looks over her body, his hands turning her and twisting her. She registers that he’s checking for any wounds and pieces together that the thing that had thrown her off-balance had been an explosion.

    Taking a deep breath through her nose, Kate tries her best to compose herself. Fortunately, Yelena has taken to muttering expletives in multiple different languages (Kate knows because she recognizes a French word in there), giving her enough time to relax her demeanor.

    Actively relaxing her posture, Kate rubs her forehead and speaks in a lighter tone. “I don’t need either of you deciding what’s best for me, and I especially don’t need you two to be at each other’s throats all the time. I appreciate your help, more than you’ll ever know, but-”

    “No,” Yelena cuts her off with a snarl. “I understand.” Shrugging Kate off as she begins to make like she’s going to walk away. “I get it.”

    “Tch- ‘Lena.” Kate reaches for her arm again, but the blonde is quick to dodge it. “Yelena. Listen to me.”

    Frustration building inside of her, both at her own inability of phrasing things in a comprehensible way and at the blonde for being so impatient, Kate hooks her fingers on her belt and tugs. She knows that Yelena is letting Kate hoist her around, and there’s a part of her that is entirely powered by the hedonist inside of her that feeds on the knowledge and hungrily prowls on the thick tension permeating the air. But Kate ignores it, because the last thing that she needs is to be thinking about how suggestive it is that she has Yelena backed up against a wall.

    Chest heaving, Yelena stubbornly glares up at Kate. But there’s an underlying layer of emotion behind the scorching burn of her wrath that lets her know that the assassin may be receptive enough to listen.

    The archer pushes onward, starting by saying, “I appreciate your help, okay? You’re my friend and I want you here.” Her voice is intentionally soft, and she knows that Yelena knows. But the sentimentality entwined in her words is real, and it’s spreading warmth over Kate’s chest. She frowns. “But I don’t want to see you fighting with my other friend.”

    “Friend,” Yelena scoffs.

    “Yes, friend ,” Kate says, rolling her eyes. “Because that’s all me and Eli are, okay?”

    Squinting, Yelena works her jaw. It looks like she’s holding herself back from fighting Kate on the topic, though she doesn’t seem to be doing a good job.

    Kate parts her lips, intending to continue insisting, but Yelena’s eyes drop to her mouth and her breath catches in her throat.

    It’s then that the feeling in Kate’s body returns to her. It’s then that she feels the warmth of Yelena’s body - of the warmth of her gloved hands gripping her waist like they’re searching for an anchor, and the heat of her exhaled breath through parted lips hits her chin. The proximity is intoxicating and Kate feels her body gravitate closer. The molten emerald is swallowed by the black of Yelena’s pupils and Kate can see herself in the reflection of the dim, orange light.

    She wants you. You want her. Kiss her.

    The hands at her waist slide to her hips.

    Kiss her.

    Kate’s eyes drop to Yelena’s lips.

    Kiss. Her.

    But there’s blood there from where Yelena’s lip has been split, and the crimson pulls Kate out of her stupor.

    Inhaling sharply, Kate decidedly backs away from Yelena, expending more energy than she would have liked by pulling herself out of her orbit. She clears her throat and awkwardly gestures. “I’ll talk to Eli about his recklessness because you’re right about that,” she says, bringing them both down to earth. Yelena’s spacy expression turns sour at the reminder of the situation, though she doesn’t seem anything other than irate. “I’m mad at him too,” Kate says honestly, and the blonde shoots her a relieved glance. “But you have to admit that you didn’t necessarily go about it the right way.”

    “He makes me angry,” Yelena supplies with a poisonous tone, shrugging her shoulders like she doesn’t regret a single thing about her behavior. Not that Kate blames her.

    “Okay.” Nodding, Kate recollects herself and waits until she knows that her voice won’t shake. “Okay, I can appreciate that. You don’t have to work with him anymore-”

    “I want to work with you.” Yelena narrows her eyes almost challengingly. Tauntingly. Like she’s goading her on.

    It has the hairs on Kate’s body rising.

    Is there a double-meaning to that? Are we reading into it?

    Does it matter? She’s your friend, you’re supposed to be staying within the friendship boundaries.

    There’s a determined expression on Yelena’s face, her lips pulled tight into a near-grimace and her eyes narrowed into a deadly glare. Kate knows, even at first glance, that the blonde has pulled up her defences. That even if she’s trying to bait Kate into… something, there is an anticipation to the way that her shoulders are pulled back to overcompensate for confidence. As much as Yelena wants to convince her that she’s the one in control here, Kate can see the tension in the muscles of her face and body.

    She’s scared.

    Kate is set alight from the inside by the realization.

    “We’ll figure it out,” she says in a hushed tone. There’s definitely double-meaning to that. She makes a show of backing away from Yelena, carefully watching every minute change in her demeanor. “How about you head home first and I’ll meet you there?” Kate grins as reassuringly as she can. “You can have all the hot water and we’ll order something nice.

    Watch a movie and make a girls’ night out of it?”

    Green eyes darting over Kate’s body, Yelena hesitantly relaxes her posture. “Okay.”

    “Okay?”

    “Yes, I already agreed Kate Bishop,” Yelena snaps, her hackles rising a little. But there’s a faint glint of mirth there as she says her name that has Kate’s grin turning genuine.

    Nodding resolutely, Kate backs away from her. “I’ll see you at home,” she says, gesturing for Yelena to go ahead.

    (Kate tries to pretend that she doesn’t see Yelena soften when she says ‘home’.)

    The good thing about her being hyper-obsessive about her career is that she does her best to prepare for everything.     Revved up by Yelena’s excitement this morning, and now by the attention from the kids, Lucky is going a little wild. Kate sees the manic look in his eye as he does that dance he does when he’s about to jump onto someone and preemptively picks the smallest kid up - Olive -

    by her armpits. Lucky barely misses Olive by a second, and Yelena immediately grabs his leash to tug him back before he hurts anyone else.

    Olive sits in Kate’s arms, a stunned expression on her face. Kate is worried that she’s going to burst into tears, but as soon as she makes eye contact with her, she bursts into a fit of giggles and screams, “Again! Again!”

    So Kate does it again. And again and again, until the other kids are begging to be picked up too.

    But there’s only one of her and three of them, so they have to take turns. Olive doesn’t seem to be very good with patience, because she turns to Yelena and holds her arms up to her in a silent request. Though judging by the determined look on her face, it’s less of a request and more of an order.

    Kate is a second away from putting a stop to all of this.

    To her surprise, Yelena breaks into a gentle grin and obliges. She bends her knees to her small height and swiftly picks up the small child in a practised motion like she’s done it countless times before. Kate briefly wonders if it’s in a Black Widow’s skillset to be able to play with children. Eyes wide with wonder as Yelena holds the small child close, the kid who isn’t in Kate’s arms immediately gravitates to Yelena like a moth to a flame. Whatever it is about her energy, the kids seem drawn to her. Yelena melts into a caring, big sister persona as Olive excitedly giggles. The kid’s happiness seems to spur her on, and Kate watches in amazement as the blonde spins them around to garner more laughter. There’s a delighted gleam to her open-mouthed grin and her raspy laughter.

    “She’s pretty,” the little boy in Kate’s arms says, clutching at Kate’s shoulders.

    “She is,” Kate says back, a little breathless. Absent-mindedly, she bounces the boy and smiles lopsidedly. “Isn’t she?”

    Yelena looks at them then, radiant and shining like the sun despite the overcast weather.

    A voice calls out, calling the names of the children clambering around Lucky and Kate and Yelena. The boy in Kate’s arms squirms and Kate takes the hint to put him down. A woman approaches them with a motherly expression on her face, the concern evident in her frazzled demeanor. She hugs the children close when they run to her and scolds them from running away.

    Olive is still clinging to Yelena’s hand, tiny fingers clasped around the blonde’s ring and pinky finger, garnering the woman’s attention. Her eyes dance between Yelena and Kate, and she smiles warmly. “Thank you for keeping them safe,” she says.

    “No worries,” Kate says, grinning brightly. She steps next to Yelena and bends at the knees slightly to ruffle Lucky’s side. “They seemed to want to play with the dog.”

    She feels Yelena’s hand on her back. Either providing reassurance or seeking it.

    The woman laughs slightly as the children excitedly garble about how cute Lucky is and how desperately they want a dog like Lucky. She nods and reassures them and then throws a glance in their direction. “You two make a lovely couple.”

    What.

    “Thank you,” Yelena says swiftly.

    “No, thank you for playing with them so well.” The woman shakes her head and extends her hand outward to reach for Olive. The little girl skips across to throw herself at the woman’s legs, clinging to her as a hand is placed on her head comfortingly. “They strayed from the group and I got so panicked thinking that something happened to them but I guess I got lucky and you two were here to look after them. Seriously, thank you so much.”

    Recovering quickly, Kate plasters her ‘make adults like me’ grin. “Oh no, don’t worry, Lucky here loves playing with children,” she says, straightening her posture. Annoyance prickles inside her. Aggravation at the unfortunate situation. Frustration with Yelena, who stands beside her so confidently after implying that they are a ‘lovely couple’. Fear and anger and insecurity. The fire builds the more that she thinks about the strange no man's land that they’re residing in and she throws her arm around Yelena’s shoulders. “My girlfriend is amazing with kids, it seems.”

    Yelena’s shoulders tighten under the weight of her arm. Kate ignores it, all grins and pleasant words.

    When their company leaves, Yelena shrugs her arm off her shoulder and spins to glare at her. The fire of aggravation in Kate’s watchful eyes is reflected in Yelena’s steel green ones as she grits her teeth. “Girlfriend?” She hisses.

    Narrowing her eyes, Kate spits back, “You’re the one that started it.”

    “I am only here to keep you from getting her killed by the Kingpin because she is running around New York City as a pretend Avenger is a lot more effort than just saying ‘thank you’,” Yelena says dismissively.

    Kate bristles. “That was mean.”

    That’s your comeback? Where did your wit go?

    I can’t think! There’s too much going on.

    The crease between Yelena’s brow deepens briefly before her glare softens and she rolls her eyes. She shifts the weight from one leg to the other, fingers playing with the Widow Bites at her wrists and keeps her features still to prevent any form of emotion from showing itself there. But Kate knows her better than that. She knows that the casual facade in front of her is exactly that: a facade. Kate watches as the frustration morphs into a conflicted, resolute expression.

    Green eyes deceptively bluer in the clouded light, Yelena readily meets her gaze and halts her twitching. “This life only leads to one destination,” she says in a low, sullen voice.

    Kate tuts and shakes her head. We’re back to this again, she thinks. While someone more reasonable might interpret Yelena’s protectiveness as thoughtful and kind, maybe even touching, Kate is growing to hate it more and more. “There are multiple destinations for this life. Protecting people. Doing the bad things that other people don’t want to do for the greater good,” she says heatedly. “Actual meaning in life.”

    “No,” Yelena cuts in sharply. “Death and pain and suffering.”

    “That was very Russian.”

    “I am serious, Kate Bishop.”

    “So am I,” Kate shoots back, lifting her chin challengingly, “ ‘Lena. ”

    Yelena simply stares. She stares and Kate knows that it’s an unspoken message to her, that this is something Yelena will never back down from. And she knows by the frustration that coils inside of her and the annoyance that prickles at her every time the blonde undermines her abilities by acting like she can’t protect herself. Like she needs Yelena to come back every time and act like her guard dog.

    Is that derogatory?

    A little.

    Maybe bodyguard instead, then.

    They’re both too stubborn to give up their stances and have been too stubborn throughout the entirety of their friendship. Kate isn’t expecting Yelena to back down now, so instead, she rolls her shoulders back and says, “Agree to disagree.”

    Yelena must come to the same conclusion because she doesn’t say anything else after that.

    They keep walking in silence and Kate can’t enjoy the smoothie that Yelena gave her when there’s a stale taste on the surface of her tongue. Lucky, sensing the sour mood between them, nudges at Yelena’s hand with his snout and licks her fingertips, which she almost gratefully accepts. The tense expression on her face soothes at Lucky’s innocent attention and the tension thickening the air between them dwindles into something bearable.

    “So,” Kate says, “I didn’t know that you liked kids.”

    Glancing amusedly, Yelena plucks the smoothie from her hand. “You make me sound like a strange person,” she quips, bringing the straw to her lips.

    Like a magic trick, the moisture in Kate’s mouth vanishes into thin air as her eyes watch dark lips trap the straw, pink tongue impatiently peeking out to meet the entrance and taste the smoothie. She swallows. There’s a fog clouding her mind as she tries to distract both her mind and body from the tension and Yelena’s tongue. Her voice is thick as she says, “You’re good with kids,” which is a statement that worsens her situation instead of benefitting it. “I wasn’t expecting it since, y’know, you look like you wanna dropkick any baby crying in your vicinity.” Yelena scoffs and rolls her eyes and she adds, “But you’re good with them and it’s really cute.”

    “Hm.”

    “What?” Kate bumps their shoulders together and takes back the smoothie from Yelena once she’s done with it. “Too domestic for your blood-thirsty vigilante heart?”

    “What?”

    Mango and orange and kiwi. That’s what Yelena’s mouth tastes like right now.

    Kate swallows the smoothie before hoarsely continuing. “Kids. Having them. Birthing them.

    All that jazz.” She shoots the blonde a curious glance. “You haven’t thought about it before?”

    Yelena’s face gradually falls as she speaks and Kate has a sinking feeling in her stomach that has her anticipating what her answer will be. “The Red Room made that choice for me,” she says resolutely. “For me and all of the other Black Widows.”

    Of course. Even the choice to have children was taken from her too.

    Biting her tongue, she meets the sullen green eyes and gently tilts her head down, wordlessly asking her to divulge.

    “It was part of the graduation ceremony.” Her voice has an artificial feel to it. Confidence in the facts yet lacking the usual fervor in the way Yelena speaks. “They sterilized us- gave us involuntary hysterectomies. Nothing would ever come between us and the mission. A person who does not have to worry about valuing the life of another more than they value their own life makes for a great killer .” Kate flinches at that. Understands Yelena’s tumult. “It was supposed to make the Widows more loyal. I was only fortunate that they subjugated me when they did. My dissent would have gotten me killed.”

    A wave of protective resentment crashes into Kate’s chest at the reminder of Yelena’s past. “I'm sorry,” she says bitterly, only wishing that things had been different. That somehow, someway, Kate would have been born early enough to live in the same time and place as Yelena and be there to shield her from all of the horrors of the Red Room.

    “Don't be,” Yelena breathes in a more empathetic tone. “It wasn't your fault. I am luckier than most, anyway.”

    “How so?”

    Poignant, Yelena’s fingers brush over the ridges of her Widow Bites. “Even if the Red Room hadn't sterilized me, I wouldn't have wanted to have children.”

    “And that's perfectly okay,” Kate reminds.

    “I told Natasha that.”

    Kate tries not to freeze at the sound of the Black Widow's name. Like Clint, Yelena rarely talks about her. However, unlike Clint, when she does talk about her it’s with pride and esteem, unafraid of displaying the emotions that Natasha’s memory evokes. Yelena doesn’t hide her grief. She tears herself open and exhibits her emotions with an air of confidence that Kate is almost jealous of. It’s haunting and debilitating and Kate slightly hates her for it.

    Sometimes it’s easy to forget that Natasha’s death is rather fresh, what with all of the chaos of everybody coming back from the Blip and trying to settle back into that life. Especially for Yelena, who was part of the Blip. Her death must have felt so abrupt to her. Kate empathizes with her for that, and feels the sorrow and grief in her smile when she looks at Lucky and says, “I told her I wanted a dog, instead.”

    Kate matches her smile. “And now you have Fanny.”

    “And Lucky,” Yelena says, looking up to meet her gaze. But there are hidden meanings laced between those two words and Kate hears the unfinished sentence loud and clear.

    “And Lucky, of course.”

    “Oh, I am very happy.” Kate throws her phone across the couch and rubs her temples. She feels Yelena’s presence pacing behind her and sighs, sliding the laptop higher in her lap. “I’m just… I got a case. It’s out of the country, in Paris.”

She seems to believe that Kate’s patriotism - which, unbeknownst to her, is practically nonexistent - will make it more difficult to bribe her into silence by the French.

    Tucking her phone into the back pocket of her jeans, Kate shuts her laptop and places it on her coffee table. “You wanna eat anything?” She calls to Yelena, who has disappeared upstairs to most likely change into her pyjamas.

    Admittedly, there is a shit-eating grin on Kate’s face as she watches the blonde descend the stairs that screams pure satisfaction. “I'm just surprised, Belova,” she says with a teasing tone in her voice, “didn't think someone as high and mighty as you would ever ask for pizza.” She gasps dramatically and presses a hand to her chest. “Of all things.”

    “Rude.” Kate reaches into the drawer before Yelena can and snatches them all up. “You're not getting any pizza.”
