She laughs nervously, knowing full well that if she keeps going any longer then they’re going to end up wrestling in the kitchen and Yelena will win. Kate’s ego does not need to be deflated today. “Okay okay,” she says, relenting. Pointing a finger in Yelena’s face, she adds, “But only cause I like you.”

    “I know,” Yelena says, preening.

    “We’re not gonna need these, by the way. Who do you think I am? I have the numbers of all the pizza places within a five-mile radius memorized.” Kate playfully smacks the pamphlets in Yelena’s face and immediately flinches away from the returning punch. “I’m sorry!”

    -

    Over the week where Yelena has become Kate’s… bedmate (?), Kate has noticed her condition progressively deteriorating. She smiles less and snaps more, her patience running thin despite Kate trying her hardest ‘to be on her best behavior and trying to accommodate to her worsening mood. Recently, Kate can hear her coming before she even arrives in a room, apparently not having the energy to pick up her feet and tread lightly like she usually does. There are shadows forming under her eyes that are often concealed by make-up, but Kate sees her face before and after a night’s sleep. Although still the most stunning person in the universe, Yelena looks like she has been dragged through the pits of hell.

    She hasn’t been sleeping well, and Kate knows that. In her deliriously fatigued state, Kate has caught her playing with the handcuffs and staring at them almost desperately. Kate’s heart aches every time that she thinks about that image, but the idea of having Yelena shackled to her bed because she’s afraid of what she could do to her disgusts her. It makes her shiver with distaste and has her angrily devolving into a furious ball of rage at the thought of the Red Room.

    But every time that she has even tried to sleep on the couch, Yelena adamantly insists that it’s not necessary. That she’s okay and that Kate should just stay in the bed.

    I doubt she’s that desperate to sleep beside me.

    And while she wants to be uselessly hopeful that the opposite is true, her instincts are telling her that there’s something deeper going on. That Yelena’s abrupt interest in tourism and sightseeing is less to do with genuine curiosity and more to do with the nightmare that started it all.

    So with the growing suspicion, Kate pays attention more. They’re in December now, almost marking a year since they met for the first time, but the mood is tense. Neither of them is able to relax with the impending feeling of doom creeping over the horizon, a fiery, blazing ball of fire with cataclysmic undertones. At least that’s what it feels like to Kate, who senses that there is something not quite right. And considering Yelena’s faltering facade paired with her misstepping more often than not, Kate is inclined to trust her gut.

    She just ended a call with Clint, of which half involved discussing the prospects of accepting the Paris case or not and the other half involved their plans for Christmas break.

    Yelena disappeared upstairs to get in the shower two minutes ago - midway through Kate explaining that she has adopted a nineteen-year-old superhero and she can’t go to the Barton’s because she doesn’t want to leave him in the city on his own - and she has left her phone on the coffee table.

    Her phone. On the coffee table.

    Her phone.

    Yelena has a phone? Since when did she get another phone? I don’t even have her number, what the fuck?

    Kate stares at it. It taunts her. She can see the light bouncing off the black surface and into her eye as if she were a magpie and the device a shiny piece of cutlery. (Which is a hilarious analogy because the only reason why her cutlery drawer is full is that Yelena has been stealing knives and forks and spoons from wherever it is she disappears during the day).

    Normally Kate would have more self-restraint. More of a sense of boundaries. It appears that those have flown out of the window alongside her patience.

    She needs to know if there’s anything on there to explain Yelena’s worsening condition.

    This is wrong. This is so so wrong. We are invading her privacy .

    She could be dying and I would never know. There is something wrong and I need to know before anything happens.

    Biting her lip, Kate hesitates. Her own phone hovers beside the blonde’s prepared to bypass needing a password.

    She’s keeping something from you.

    It’s at that moment that Yelena’s phone lights up and begins to vibrate in Kate’s hand.

    Shit!

    Startled, Kate drops the phone.

    Crap. Crap crap crap. What if Yelena has a secret alarm that lets her know when I touch her phone?

    Why would it be catered specifically to you?

    Because she knows I’m nosy!

    Precariously, Kate looks at the phone screen like it’s a grenade and it’s going to blow up in her lap. There’s an unsaved phone number lighting up the screen, and despite Kate not having picked up the call, it keeps vibrating persistently. Whoever is on the other end of the line must need to reach Yelena pretty badly.

    What if it’s an emergency? What if someone is dying?

    No one is-

    Fuck it.

    “Hello?” Kate says, the back of her neck prickling from the nerves.

    A woman’s voice answers with, “Hi, Yelena?”

    “No, this is Kate, her room-”

    Boop.

    Kate separates the phone from her face and looks at the screen to confirm that whoever it is has hung up the call.

    What the fuck?

    The sound of Yelena’s loud singing abruptly fills the silence in the apartment, alerting Kate that the blonde is finished with her shower. (That’s another thing that she has learned about Yelena: she’s capable of showering in record time, most likely a habit she carries from her past living conditions). Her voice carries through the room and had it been any other occasion, Kate would have smiled warmly and paused to listen to it. But she just got hung up on by a mystery woman who knows her name because she hung up the moment she introduced herself and her adrenaline is making her shake. There are a million theories running through her head and not enough time to process them all.

    Wiping the phone screen against her sweater to rid it of any fingerprints, Kate positions the phone back onto the coffee table exactly how she found it. Guilt poisons her breathing. She bites her lip and prays that her nerves will have calmed down by the time Yelena comes back downstairs.

    -

    The last thing that she expects when coming back home to the apartment after dinner with Jack is Yelena looking so… normal.

    Kate’s definition of normal may be a little different to the one that would reasonably appear in someone’s mind. She’s not sure that anyone else would walk into their bedroom and find their roommate looking like they’re having an existential crisis with music quietly blaring from a speaker and think: oh wow. She almost looks like a normal person.

    But that’s what Kate thinks. Because Yelena, despite her chaos and her mess and her amiable exuberance, still holds an edge of danger in everything that she does. In the way her wrist flicks when she spreads peanut butter on the bread with a butter knife. When she walks around the apartment so silent that Kate sometimes forgets that she even has a roommate.

    Even the way that Yelena eats, ingesting the food so quickly like she’s afraid that Kate is going to reach out and take it from her, her fingers curled a little too tightly around the cutlery. Yelena is dangerous in the sense that Kate is reminded of her mortality at the most random moments of the day.

    This is not Yelena being dangerous.

    This is Yelena being any other adult who is so tired that she has the bruises beneath her eyes to prove it but she can’t sleep (won’t sleep) so she has resorted to deteriorating on the surface of the bed.

    Never in their friendship has Kate empathized more with Yelena.

    “What are you doing?” Kate asks as she finishes climbing up the stairs, her hand gliding on the railing for support. The muscles in her thighs are shaking from exertion, having spent the majority of the night on her feet as Jack introduced her to the many, many potential clients for her business. It had gotten late and before she knew it, it was one in the morning and Jack was pushing her into the car of his personal driver and telling her to text when she go home safe.

    Yelena’s figure doesn’t move from where she’s staring at the ceiling. “Come here,” she says, barely audible over the speaker.

    Amused, Kate finally shrugs off the checkered blazer that has been hanging off her shoulders the entire night. “Okay.”

    As she approaches her bed - purple curtains still cocooning the space for privacy that they now both share - she hones in on the music lulling the air. The song that Yelena is playing on the speaker is as soft as she is. It’s a hollow sound carried by breathy voices singing lines that blend together into a haunting melody. Every rising melody stings a little harder.

    She’s in her emo hours, Kate’s inner-monologue muses.

    Parting the curtains with a smile on her face, Kate is wholly unprepared for the sight in front of her.

    Yelena’s face is bare, stripped clean of make-up. There are dark bruises under her eyes that dull the green of her eyes, but it doesn’t take away from her beauty. She’s still wearing Kate’s favorite archery shirt, and that may be another thing contributing to the whole ‘Yelena being normal’ thing because Kate still remembers the plethora of instances where she has had a mental breakdown over overdue essays and impending presentations in that very shirt.

    Except Kate has half a head on Yelena and the archery shirt was already loose on her build, so on Yelena, it engulfs her. It wraps her up in the blacks and purples of her old archery team’s colors, sleeves hanging low enough to brush her elbows and collar to expose her shoulder and collarbone. Despite Kate’s protectiveness of the shirt (2020 had been the only year they agreed to let the colors change to black and purple), she doesn’t have the heart to ask for it back. Especially not when it makes Yelena look like any other student (who also happens to look like a goddess descended from above).

    Kate’s heart swells in crescendo.

    “Are you feeling okay?” She breathes, still clinging to the mirth of finding Yelena in such a deeply introspective state.

    Yelena’s eyelids shield the emeralds and jades from Kate’s gaze as she hums, “Mhm.”

    Is she high?

    Probably not, but we’re not gonna rule out that possibility.

    Shaking her head as silent laughter shivers through her body, Kate fully steps into the purple cocoon. “Any reason why you're starfishing on the bed we're supposed to be sharing?” She asks, and if the teasing isn’t evident in her tone then it’s definitely evident in the way she grabs one of Yelena’s ankles and tugs it.

    Humming, Yelena lightly kicks Kate’s annoying hand away and lets her leg drop back to where it was: occupying the left side of the bed. “Extra hugs?” She says.

    “Dork.” Kate lightly nudges her leg, encouraging her to make room. “If you want cuddles you can just ask for them.”

    “I don't want cuddles,” Yelena says, eyes still closed and leg still refusing to move.

    Kate pauses where she’s hovering at the side of the bed, curls of her hair spilling over her shoulder as she hangs in midair. “Really?” She drawls, eyebrows raised.

    “Mhm.”

    “So if I went to sleep on the couch…”

    Fingers reach out to clasp around the front of her black dress shirt before Kate even has the chance to act on her words, curling into the thin fabric enough so that she can feel the slight scrape of her nails against her chest. Yelena’s eyes open, a dark, brooding, sacramento green. It’s disarming. Nothing in Kate’s life has ever prepared her for something as beautiful as the mutable color of Yelena’s eyes. Whatever Kate had been saying about this Yelena not being dangerous immediately flies out of the window because it feels like she could kill her with just one look.

    A startled laugh bursts past her lips anyways and she has to pretend like the pink on her cheeks is from the icy cold outside. “That's what I tho-”

    “If you finish that sentence I will make it so that you cannot walk for the next two weeks.”

    Kate purses her lips, the laughter ready to rise again. Yelena doesn’t seem to catch the double-entendre of her phrasing and it just makes it all the more amusing when she says, “Kinky.”

    Yelena’s right eyebrow raises in disapproval. “Kate-”

    “I'm kidding, I'm kidding.” Kate laughs soft and inoffensive, reaching out with her thumb to soothe the lines on the blonde’s forehead. Immediately her features melt back into a mellow expression and the hand in Kate’s shirt turns into less of a grasp and more of a gentle tug. Rolling her eyes affectionately, Kate reaches for the elbow of that hand and pulls. “Come here, you softie.”

    “Not soft.”

    “Mhm,” Kate hums, entertained smirk on her lips as Yelena does sit up to wrap her arms around her in greeting.

    She’s so soft. She must be tired. Was she waiting for me to get back?

    That line of thought is not one she wants to go follow. Mostly because every time she lets herself think about Yelena doing something caring - like staying awake to make sure that Kate makes it home safely - dejection falls over her. It’s difficult not to feel like Yelena is extra cautious with her safety because she doesn’t believe in her ability to keep herself alive. It has the insecurities of there being an imbalance in their relationship rising up again because the only reason why Yelena would believe that is if she thought that Kate’s abilities would never be able to match up to hers. All that Kate wants is for Yelena to see her as an equal. Someone she respects enough to trust that they’re capable of looking after themselves.

    Sighing, Kate releases Yelena from her hug and leans back on her hands. “So why are you listening to music in the dark?”

    “Music…” Yelena trails off, and her eyes wander the ceiling again like she’s searching for the right words. “...helps. Sometimes.” Kate watches her quietly, letting her find them on her own. “It helps with all of the... feelings?”

    “Ah.” Kate nods. “I get what you're saying, don't worry.”

    “Yeah, and…” The blonde mulls it over in the silence, sinking into the melody and swaying. “It's nice. To listen and feel it all.”

    Kate can’t say that she feels the same way when it comes to feeling feelings. Her preferred method is to allow herself an allotted amount of time to be emotional before she falls back on the structures of her mind. Though considering Yelena’s relationship with her own emotions, she can understand why she would feel that way. Why she would take special care to allow herself to feel. There is a certain beauty to the concept that intimidates Kate, so she awkwardly shifts on the bed and hesitantly asks, “You want me to give you space?”

    Shaking her head, Yelena turns to look her in the eye. “No.” Her lips curl up slightly, though anybody could have missed it if they hadn’t been intently staring. “Could you lie here with me?” She falls back onto the bed again, honey blonde hair splaying out beneath her head.

    When Kate still hesitates, her expression shifts into playfully imploring and she says, “Please?”

    “Of course I can,” Kate says. “Whatever you need, I'm here.”

    In more ways than one.

    Yelena hums.

    Sinking into the cloud of covers and pillows, Kate feels the tension in her body disperse. There’s something nice about the rising and falling of the melody that she can’t really pinpoint. Especially here, in the darkness of the violet safe haven, Kate can sense the gears in her brain slowing from their turning and turning and turning. The noise in her mind - which she has grown so accustomed to over time - quiets momentarily to allow room for the music playing on Kate’s speaker.

    For a moment, everything is slow.

    Yelena’s voice sings along to the melody, hoarse and rough but lovely all the same.

    She’s lovely. And sweet. Sometimes she’s sour but she’s always sweet.

    I adore her.

    What?

    I-

    Everything picks back up again.

    “What song is it?” Kate asks, turning her head to look at her.

    “I don't know,” the blonde says wistfully, an unreadable expression on her face. “It came up on Spotify.”

    “It reminds me of another group,” Kate says, and it feels like she’s trying to force the words out because they feel awkward and heavy in this atmosphere. “I think you might like them.”

    “Yeah?”

    “Yeah, they help me with my insomnia sometimes.”

    Yelena purses her lips thoughtfully, and Kate thinks that she sees concern show itself in a small frown on her face. But then she’s turning her head to look at her and the frown melts into a neutral smile that Kate knows she’s purposefully making encouraging. Like she knows that Kate is feeling a little anxious. “What is the name of the group?” She asks.

    “Sleeping At Last.”

    “Ha,” Yelena deadpans with a grin.

    It’s contagious and has Kate grinning back. “What?”

    “It's ironic, no?” Her eyes narrow playfully and Kate feels her eyes skip a beat because the warm orange light from the lamps in the room makes them gleam. “Listening to a group called Sleeping At Last when you have insomnia.”

    Kate laughs, shaking her head and fixing her gaze on the ceiling. “I guess so… dork.”

    “You like me.”

    “Yeah,” she says in a hushed tone. “I do.”

    There must be a draft because the room’s temperature drops at her admission. It’s slightly different to the one last year on Christmas Eve, where they had both been panting and trying to catch their breaths, Yelena rubbing at her sore wrist and Kate holding her bruising ribs. The only thing that Kate had known at the time was that Yelena was Natasha Romanoff’s sister, she wanted Clint dead more than she wanted appetizers, and she was infuriatingly charismatic.

    At that time, Yelena was nothing more than a part of the web her mother had weaved. Kate had fleeting thoughts here and there of what could have happened to the Black Widow, but she was still riding the high of the Hawkeye name being passed down to her so nothing much ever came out of that line of thought.

    Now…

    Kate feels a thrill of emotions and immediately grimaces.

    Yup. Feeling feelings doesn’t feel good for us.

    Bouncing up from her position, Kate turns to look at Yelena with a frenzied grin. “Come on.”

    Rightly confused and slightly spooked by the abrupt burst of energy, Yelena pushes herself up onto her elbows and frowns. “What?”

    “Come on,” Kate gripes, sliding off the end of the bed and tugging at Yelena’s ankle like a little kid whining for attention. The blonde curls her lip into an annoyed scowl, but Kate can see the amusement dancing in her eyes as she glares. “The music is better if you dance to it,” she says in explanation.

    Yelena frowns. “I don't dance.” But she slides to sit at the end of the bed anyway.

    “Really?” Kate turns to raise her eyebrows at the blonde because she wants to make sure she knows just how surprised she is by the revelation. Once she knows that Yelena has acknowledged her astonishment, she continues to grab the curtains at the end of the bed and drags them to the industrial steel beam at one corner of her bed. “Have I found the one thing that you're not ridiculously good at?”

    The blonde doesn’t say anything but Kate can feel her gaze on her as she carelessly ties the curtain around the beam. It’s placed in the centre of her bedroom for support of the floor above, considering that Kate’s bedroom used to be two smaller offices but she knocked the wall down to have more space years ago.

    “I don't have a good relationship with dancing,” Yelena says.

    Kate freezes. There’s a sinking feeling in her stomach but she turns to look at her anyway. “Why?”

    “In the Red Room.” Yelena’s expression is soft - almost apologetic like she knows that Kate’s anger levels shoot through the roof every time she mentions the Red Room. She probably does know. “They made us learn ballet when we were children. They said it was to make us tough - that we are made of marble.”

    “I'm sorry,” Kate breathes, “you don't have to do anything you're not comfortable with.”

    Yelena blankly stares at her, but Kate knows that she’s not focusing on her specifically. And then she’s saying, “I don't mind watching.”

    Eyebrows shooting up, Kate smirks and says, “Oh?”

    “Kate Bishop.”

    Kate laughs at Yelena’s disapproving glare, incapable of stopping the widening grin as Yelena holds her gaze. “Come on,” she says, still chuckling to herself as she tugs at Yelena’s wrist and pulls her up. “You don't have to dance but... just stand here.” She jostles the smaller woman until she’s standing in the space at the end of the bed, bathed in the orange glow of the lamplight and the blue tint of the moonlight. Kate leans in conspiratorially and says, “So I don't feel like a stripper.”

    “You would be a very successful stripper,” Yelena swings back, her own smirk now playing on her lips.

    “Thank you, ma'am,” she says, playfully curling a finger into her hair and pulling it in a very exaggerated seductive way.

    The song has already melted into another, this one a little less mellow and more climatic. When it comes to making her friends laugh, Kate will make herself look as stupid as she has to. In some ways, she considers it a sport. A competition to get them to break into laughter. There’s a part of her that lives off the exhilaration of making the people that she loves laugh and smile, even if it’s at her own expense. It’s one of the many things that she and Yelena have in common, which is surprising considering that Kate’s ego is pretty big and Yelena is a professional assassin, but she supposes that they make a good pair of clowns.

    Despite the slow tempo of the music and the cinematic feel of the music, Kate still puts her entire heart into dancing. She makes use of the clear space between her bed and the wall where Lucky will usually lazily spread himself, spinning and turning dramatically. Yelena watches her with a barely contained smile, but she seems hell-bent on keeping stoic. Kate maintains eye contact as she prances across the space to her, guessing the melody of the verse as she sings along in a terrible voice. The blonde rolls her eyes but she accepts Kate’s scrabbling hands at her forearms, very lightly holding her by the elbows. Kate makes sure not to move her, trying to resist the urge to twirl her around because she said she doesn’t dance. When Yelena still doesn’t break, she throws her head around and purposefully smacks Yelena in the face with her hair and pushes herself away. There’s an annoyed scowl on her face but her eyes are glittering in the blue and orange light and Kate can feel her grin making her cheeks ache.

    Drums crash and strings rise. The music swells and Kate can feel the notes coursing through her body.

    And then it halts and a hand slides around her waist.

    Kate’s breath catches.

    Fuck.

    As if she had perfectly predicted the structure and direction of the song, Yelena pulls her closer at the precise moment most of the instruments fade out. Their bodies crash together and Kate feels the swell of Yelena’s breasts pressing against hers and she really hopes that she can’t feel her heart beating through the layers of skin and bones.

    Yelena’s hand is warm at the middle of her back, the other sliding up from Kate’s elbow and tickling its path to her hand. All Kate can do is focus on the short puffs of her own breaths and the glimmering emerald in Yelena’s eyes. She feels the momentum of her body gently guided into a sway as the blonde spins them in place, the instruments in the song stripping bare besides a piano and the voice of the singer.

    “'Lena, are you sure-”

    “Yes,” Yelena cuts in, lips slowly spreading into a grin. “This is hardly dancing, Kate Bishop.”

    And fuck Yelena for having impeccable timing.

    Fuck her for being so unintentionally romantic and sexy and everything that Kate didn’t even know she wanted in life, because the moment she finishes saying her full name, all of the instruments crash back in like a wave and Kate’s heart pounds at her chest.

    Blood rushes in her ears. Sweat begins to build in the palms of her hands and she desperately hopes that Yelena can’t feel it.

    “Do you still remember?” Kate says, hoping to distract from the burning of her cheeks and the pounding of her heart. “Everything from the Red Room?”

    Yelena’s grin fades into a sorrowful smile. Kate spin kicks whatever part of her brain thought it was a good idea to bring this up right now. “Yes,” she says, confident yet mourning. “It's difficult to fall asleep sometimes.”

    Kate makes an ‘ah’ sound, fixing the arm wrapped around Yelena’s shoulders to play with the small baby hairs at the back of her neck. “And here I was thinking you were waiting for me.”

    “Ha,” she deadpans with a playful glare.

    Smiling cheekily, she pokes her tongue out between her teeth and accepts the flick on the forehead that it earns her. “No but really…” She lets her other arm fall at her side so she can grip Yelena’s elbow reassuringly. “Even though you were under mind control, you remember everything?”

    “Yes.” A pensive look crosses Yelena’s face. One that Kate has seen too many times to count now. “The memories, they are... blurred. It's difficult to separate the thoughts that belonged to me and the thoughts that belonged to them. Sometimes, it still happens. The lines blur and I can't remember which part of my brain is... me.” Her analytical expression morphs into one of disgust. “I did... so many unforgivable things. Things I would have never done if they never put me under.”

    “I believe that.”

    Yelena smiles bittersweetly in a way that is so self-deprecating that it makes Kate’s skin crawl. Like she doesn’t believe it herself.

    Kate lightly tugs at a small wisp of hair at the base of her skull, as if reprimanding her for her lack of confidence. “You're a good person, y’know? I can tell.”

    “I am…” The blonde clicks her tongue in frustration and mutters something in Russian. It makes Kate smile sweetly because it is truly endearing when she can’t find the right way to phrase what she wants to say. “I'm trying. I'm really trying. I really want us to be friends.”

    Did we just get friendzoned?

    Why does that sting?

    Quit it! We literally agreed to be friends!

    I guess that confirms it, then.

    Despite the internal war raging inside her head, Kate keeps the sweet smile on her face and laughs. “I think at this point, it's too late to go back.” The words feel heavy and laden with hidden meaning, but she keeps the teasing tone in her voice. She knows that Yelena feels it too, however, because the hands at her back flex and curl into her shirt, eyes dancing over her expression searchingly. “You're my friend, 'Lena,” she whispers. The music help fill the quiet pauses in their conversation. “Even if you're a jackass sometimes, nothing is gonna change that.”

    Satisfied with the teasing tone in her voice, Yelena tilts her head to the side. “Not even if I steal your favorite archery shirt?”

    “Don't push it,” Kate scowls, pretending to glare but failing massively.

    “I have not been able to choose my friends before.” Yelena’s voice is quiet when she says it. Full of emotion that Kate doesn’t have the mental capacity to decipher. “You are my first.”

    “And there are many more to come,” Kate says confidently, though the thought of having to share this side of Yelena - the soft, introspective, mischievous side of her - with somebody else makes her sick to her stomach. Not that she has any right to feel that way. Yelena doesn’t belong to anybody. Least of all Kate. “You're wonderful. Nobody would pass up the opportunity to be close to you, 'Lena.”

    I would never pass up the opportunity to be close to you.

    The blue in Yelena’s eyes shines under the moonlight and her voice lowers. “Be careful, Kate Bishop, or I will think that you are trying to seduce me.”

    And it’s flirting. Yelena is flirting. But this is what they have always done, and this is how they banter.

    We’re friends.

    “Seduce you?” Kate widens her eyes dramatically and looks around like she doesn’t know what she’s talking about. “With what? The truth?”

    “You flatter me,” Yelena says, carefully bumping their foreheads together in a subdued headbutt.

    “I'm just being honest.”

    Whatever playlist it is that Yelena found on Spotify, Kate has to commend her for her discovery. There aren’t many songs that are able to lull the ongoing anxiety and adrenaline in Kate’s life enough for her to fall asleep easily. It’s why she stays awake into the early hours of the morning, pacing and distracting herself in hopes of rest. Yelena’s dark circles show that she has been struggling just the same.

    Their height difference only stands out in moments like this, where they’re both barefoot, standing side-by-side or toe-to-toe. If Kate wanted to, she could lean forward and press her lips to Yelena’s forehead where her golden blonde fringe curls messily. But she doesn’t.

    Yelena leans forward and rests her forehead against her shoulder.

    Crap crap crap crap crap-

    WEE WOO WEE WOO WEE WOO!

    WHAT IS HAPPENING? WHAT IS GOING ON?

    Somewhere, in the very back of her head, therapy voice says: is this what falling in love feels like?

    Kate can feel the fatigue oozing from her body. There aren’t many times when Yelena allows her exhaustion to show itself - something about always being ready to go no matter how many hours of sleep she has in her system. Kate still remembers that story Yelena told her when she stayed awake for almost three days straight. So feeling her body - usually muscular, sturdy and strong - practically melt against her from the fatigue is startling.

    I know that she hasn’t been sleeping well because she’s worried about doing something to hurt me in her sleep, but she refuses to let me sleep anywhere else.

    Maybe we should-

    No. The handcuffs are out of the question.

    Kate’s arms wrap around Yelena in what she hopes is a comforting hug. She feels an overwhelming surge of protectiveness. Sighing, she steadily slows their swaying to a stop in time with the ending of a song and says, “Come on sleepyhead.”

    She hopes that they can find a solution soon.

Cassie blows the baby hairs out of her face and Kate wipes them out of the way for her.

But it would be hypocritical of Kate, who spent her entire childhood shortening her mother’s lifespan by being recklessly independent, to give her a lecture about disobeying her parents. So instead she swings her arm around Cassie’s shoulders and pulls her into a half-hug, half-headlock.

    Peter sounds genuinely excited to set up all of the lights (nobody is surprised at this) and Cassie is already struggling with two different balloons at once.

    Better get it over and done with.

He seems to appreciate the newcomers, but Kate can tell that he’s still drowsy because he doesn’t freak out over Cassie and Peter being here. Instead, he bumps his head against Kate’s leg for head pats, which she gives more than willingly.

    What the fuck-

    Get it off the wall!

The blonde nudges her hand away with her elbow as she puts what sounds like an entire bag full of liquor onto the counter. “We needed Eli to get your tequila,” she says, vaguely gesturing to Eli.

Eli watches the entire thing from the corner of his eye, a very satisfied smirk pulling his lips up. “I should have known,” Kate says, helping Cassie pull out the bottles of tequila, whiskey and packs of beer. “You and your text messages and calls.”

Cassie jolts in surprise at the sound, as does Lucky who has slumped in front of the oven. Kate snorts in laughter and hip checks the younger girl when she takes some of the beers to the fridge. “Yelena has been dragging me around the city,” she says in a strained voice as she bends at the waist. “I didn't have time to process your neediness.”

Kate tries not to frown when she says, “I think she's with her family,” because while Yelena didn’t tell her where she was going to be disappearing so suddenly, so close to her birthday, the blonde didn’t owe her anything in terms of disclosing private information on her profession.

    If that’s what you wanna tell yourself to feel better…

    Shut up. It’s not like we tell her everything about our cases. Confidentiality is part of the business model.

    That’s when Kate registers what’s happening. The lights and balloons and bags of liquor and food. Cassie’s emphasis on it being her birthday and the guilty expressions on Peter and Cassie’s face like they have done something to warrant Kate getting angry. Her brain, which has only just woken up from her post-work-out nap, finally catches up. And it’s not like Kate is opposed to impromptu plans and spontaneous activities, but she has been wound tight for the past two days since Yelena disappeared so she can’t help the spike in anxiety when she asks, “Wait- what party?”

    Idiots. They’re all idiots.

    They’re gonna be the death of me one day.

    -

    Apparently, the original plan had been to throw Kate a surprise birthday party. However, considering that Cassie had to fly in from San Francisco and Eli had to come back to New York from Baltimore, the timing was a little tight. Then there was the struggle with the venue because even though Cassie’s father is an Avenger he is not made of money, something that Kate is already aware of considering her extensive knowledge of the Avengers but Eli wasn’t. Peter’s place was already out of the question, considering that it’s more of a studio than an actual apartment so even with just the four of them it would have been crowded. The only convenient, price- and space-efficient place was Kate’s apartment, hence the three of them ambushing her with party equipment.

    They’re expecting guests, too. Cassie and Peter had taken it upon themselves to invite almost everyone that Kate is in regular contact with, bar the Bartons since it’s still school season and there’s going to be drinking at the party.

    “We don’t want the kids seeing the mess that you’re gonna be at the end of the night,” Cassie says with a teasing grin.

    Kate turns on Peter, whose cheeks blush pink as she chews him out for retelling the unfortunate night where she had dragged herself across the dirty streets of New York.

    That’s how she finds out that MJ will be coming too. Kate spends a good ten minutes using the piece of information against him to relentlessly tease, eventually garnering the attention of Cassie who is blowing up balloon after balloon. (Her face is flushed from the oxygen deprivation and Eli has to confiscate the balloons from her.) Under Kate’s and Cassie’s persistent probing and jesting, Peter crumbles and admits that he has feelings for MJ. Which is how they end up on Kate’s laptop scrolling through the girl’s Instagram page. However, much to their disappointment and Peter’s relief, MJ doesn’t seem to post too often, and when she does it’s usually either sketches or aesthetic photographs that look admittedly amazing.

    The atmosphere has Kate forgetting about Yelena’s recent absence.

    After that night, with the dancing and the talking and the friendzoning-

    You can’t be bitter about that Bishop, it’s pathetic.

    Leave me alone. Let me wallow in my self-pity.

    Kate figures that they needed some distance. Space to let everything sink in. Time to process away from each other and truly feel out the whole friendship thing. Or at least that’s what Kate has been doing.

    I have no idea what Yelena is doing.

    Maybe she joined the circus.

    Why the fuck would she join the circus?

    I think the question is: why not join the circus.

    So yeah, having the three of them here helps distract from thought processes like that. While there is still some residue of anger leftover from how their last mission ended, Eli is being cordial and is playing up his charm perfectly. Despite the rocky start, Peter seems to be opening up a little more to him now that he’s familiar with how he works. The edges of Kate’s anger soften when she sees Peter geeking out over the Bluetooth LED lightbulb he’s installing and Eli, despite the evident confusion on his face, is doing his best to nod along and ask the right questions.

    Although maybe, “So the bulb changes color?’ isn’t the best question, because it sets Peter off on a tangent about diodes and lighting theory and Eli looks like he’s three seconds away from ending himself.

    Kate, who has showered, fixed herself up and slipped on a simple, casual red dress (per Cassie’s adamant advice), swoops in to save him from potentially saying something dumb.

It has the entire apartment smelling like gorgeous food and Kate is glad that Peter insisted on the nachos idea because it’s one of her favorite party food. (Much to her mother’s dismay).

Her anger has mostly dissipated now into a small grudge that she will most likely hold onto for years to come, so it feels easier to be around Eli again. With this in mind, she sits on the space at the kitchen counter beside the stovetop, swinging her bare feet as she watches Eli tend to the sauce. He’s considerably less tense now that the atmosphere is lighter. Kate can see it in the slight smile on his lips. He’s a reckless dumbass but he’s not a bad person, she thinks as she is met with the cocoa brown of his eyes.

Eli is already looking back at the nacho chilli sauce by the time Kate glances back at him. He sounds juvenile when he asks, “Do you think he likes me?”

Yelena had been considerably upset that one of her babies was now sticking out of the ceiling and they wouldn't be able to retrieve it unless they used one of Katie's grapple arrows, and considering that Kate refused to further ruin her apartment, the only thing the blonde could do was sulk. Kate swerves out of the way as the sparkly gold balloon splutters and flies around the kitchen, swerving back around to smack Peter square in the face. Despite Eli's poor decision-making skills, his reflexes are sharp, so he's able to reach out and grab her by the waist before she can tip sideways and to the floor.     Oh shit-

    -

    Technically, I wasn’t wrong.

    "I'm sorry, I can't help it."

    Yelena doesn’t know what friendship is. Not really.

    It comes back to her in flashes, like a lighthouse spinning in the darkness of her memory. Remembering is blinding and disorienting and it makes her want to pull her hair out, but it doesn’t stop the strobes of light in her brain so she grits her teeth and pushes through. Like she was taught.

    Ever since she stepped out of the Red Room’s crimson fog, the dam holding back a lifetime’s worth of longing and yearning for intimacy broke. Natasha fed that desire for a little while. The little while she was still alive. And then she was gone and she tried to connect with the Widows - her other sisters. But it wasn’t the same. It doesn’t feel the same when every time she looks at their faces she is hit with the strobing lights in her mind.

    The closest she has gotten to friendship is the handful of times she has had to fake it for the sake of a mission. She remembers tasting the amicability behind pointless back-and-forth and the temptation in laughter and wondering behind the glass cell of her mind if the kind Spanish woman would have ever wanted to spend time with her - with Yelena and not Maria, her alias - had things turned out differently. But they don’t turn out differently. They turn out how they always do: a dead body, a smothered surge of emotions, followed by a new target.

    Friendship is not something the Red Room taught her. Sex and romance and acquaintanceships were dissected and examined and practised. They are all skills she has mastered and knows how to create and cultivate, but friendship is something that was strictly forbidden.

    The light of her memory flashes in her mind in the shape of her first kill and trembles.

    When she looks back at it - the night after she finds Kate Bishop slumped against the back of a 7/11 - she realizes that it’s her ignorance that has led her here. ‘Here’ being the top of the unexpected fence between friendship and infatuation. And she falls into the infatuation side too easily.

    Easy is not the word though, Yelena thinks. Yelena was very aware of the heavy air between them whenever she found herself meeting blue eyes across tables, cars and parking lots. The once-overs and stray looks exchanged, and Kate Bishop’s wandering gaze. Yelena’s own intrigued gaze when Kate is distracted by menial things like pretty cars and trees and strangers’ outfits. It doesn’t take long for Yelena to realize what it means. Especially when her heart clenches every time Kate rolls her eyes and smirks, or she says something so stupid that Yelena has to pause to look at her, or Kate’s playful and sarcastic glare softens into a warm type of fondness. And the Red Room taught her sex and attraction from a theoretical perspective, and she has experienced it from the confines of the glass cell in her mind. Yelena Belova, the Red Room’s best (right after none other than Natalia Romanova), and she still finds herself tipping over the edge. Knowingly tips over the edge.

    What good is all of her training in seduction when Yelena doesn’t see the line between friendship and something else before it’s too late?

    So Miami happens.

    Miami happens, and Yelena realizes how much her sanity hinges on this blossoming bond with Kate.

    She follows that trail of blood - like always, leaving a track of bodies behind her - and hopes to god that it has nothing to do with Kate Bishop. But her gut is telling her otherwise, and it’s not like she can ignore it because her gut is always right. So by the time she finds Kate Bishop bleeding out against the back of the nearest chain store, her mind is replaying thousands of images in her head like a lighthouse in the dark and it makes her head spin.

    But only one matters right now.

    Kate is too calm for the situation at hand. Her face is pale white, beads of sweat building across her forehead, and the wounds on her body don’t appear to have been properly patched up.

    Yelena knows that they’re staring at the face of death just by the state of her body.

    It’s terrifying.

    It’s even more terrifying when she can’t find it in herself to just… leave. Leave after making sure that Kate is okay and that she isn’t going to die in an uncool way and join the roster of faces that keep haunting Yelena. Instead, she stays even after getting Kate to swallow the pills and seeing her fall asleep.

    She doesn’t realize that by staying - by allowing herself to feel and worry and care - that Kate Bishop is added to the roster anyway. Just in an entirely different way.

It’s strange seeing it so dark when the warmth of the lights, the faint sound of music and the low voice singing along streaming into the living room usually keep her company. The absence makes her heart ache – her skin quiver beneath the surface. After all, the kitchen had been the deciding factor. Its open-plan design, with a perfect view into the garden from the window over the sink, so that they could watch the dogs run around while making dinner.

    You’re being incredibly domestic and it’s kinda gross.

    Too true.

    Uneasy, Kate huffs a breath. The sound prompts Fanny’s snout to push against the back of her thigh, her dark eyes peering at her.

    “Nothing, nothing,” Kate says, resuming the gentle massage between the akita’s ears. The TV glitters in the dark, the sound of Lucky barking as the squirrel escapes from the garden overlapping whatever movie is on. But still… Kate sinks further into the couch. “Just miss your mom, that’s all.”

    Fanny huffs again. She copies Kate and burrows into the pillows, eyes turning to watch the TV as if she understands the dialogue quickly swapped between the man and woman.

    At least the Tracksuit Mafia doesn’t know where she lives now.

    -

    Kate frowns. For the past ten minutes, she has been trying to make sense of the sore feeling in her eyes. In that time, all she has been able to chalk it up to is the fact that she has been staring at the same screen for the past hour or so, which is, in her father’s words ‘going to make her eyes turn into squares.’ Alternatively, it may be the migraine building across her forehead. It’s all up in the air.

    I'm gonna go blind at this point.

    She rubs her eyes with her thumb and forefinger. Clint had opened the document on the table for her to read, and she had done with a thirst to expand her intellect. However, upon rereading, her eyes had caught on one word and stayed there. In trying to make sense of it, she has turned the issue over and over in her mind. She zeros in on the word and picks it apart until she knows what has her attention hooked.

    As intelligent as she thinks she is, it's a wonder it has taken her this long to figure it out.

    Sliding her hand over her face (thankful for the make-up she didn’t put on that morning because of Past Kate’s laziness) until it covers her mouth, she finally says, “It doesn’t feel right.” Her hand restlessly moves to her ear, where she rubs her earring between her thumb and forefinger so that the shape of the arrow is practically branded into her flesh. Old habits and all that. “I don’t think I’m comfortable with calling us the ‘Avengers.’”

    Behind her, Clint grunts.

    Kate hears his sluggish approach. Shoes scuff the floors loudly in the compound, because of the weighty steel panels that serve as flooring and the vastness of the room. They should really put a rug down. It would really spruce up the place and I won’t have to fucking block out Clint’s pacing like it’s a white noise machine.

    “I thought that you of all people would have loved to be a part of that legacy,” Clint says.

    “Maybe on, like, a theoretical level but when I really think about it–” She inhales sharply. Holds. Then breathes out, “We don't really inspire the same kind of feeling that you guys did.”

    “Doubting your team, kid?”

    Kate bristles. “C'mon, It's not because of that.”

    “You sure?” Clint turns, pointedly, and puts his weight on one arm as he leans against the computerized table to look out of the wide windows of the office.

    Through the large, landscape windows they have a full vision of the tall training rooms. Kate is still wrapping her head around the upgrade from the little nook tucked beneath her apartment stairs to this, but the idea is certainly making itself comfortable in her head. She’s not going to be the one complaining that Maria Hill wants to spoil a bunch of noobs rotten with top-notch facilities and A-grade equipment.

    Below, in the training room, drills are taking place.

    ‘Drills’ is… one way of putting it.

    She was fortunately excused from the practice so that she could read this finalized document, which she is grateful for considering she now sees the mess that the team is making of the drills. It's a flurry of color and movement as they run through potential scenarios, some of which Kate helped strategize with Clint only three months ago. They’re still getting used to working together as a team, which was expected to… block their progress, but Kate may have been a little too optimistic in her hopes for them.

    But we’re improving, her inner monologue prompts, observing the way the team avoids an exploding wall.

    You only officially started two weeks ago. Give yourselves some credit.

    Someone is hit by a wayward projectile. From within the office, they can almost hear the pained shout through the bulletproof glass.

    Kate blinks.

    Spoke too fucking soon.

    Clint exhales heavily through his nose. “I wouldn't blame you if it was.”

    Grimacing, she watches as Cassie reappears from thin air with a skip and a hop, looking a lot less like she’s Bambi skating on ice and more like Ant-Man. Which is progress.

    What isn’t progress is the furious look that Eli gets on his face as he whirls on Kamala, who has accidentally broken a crate full of mock grenades with her powers and ruined the drill. The lights of the room flash red, signalling a type of ‘Game Over’ situation that feels a lot more world-ending when you’re in the room than when you’re observing from above. From above, it looks a lot like watching a game of basketball gone wrong, which considering the way that Eli commandeers the TV in the lounge room to watch his games, she now knows something about.

    "They're…” Kate grimaces as Cassie guides Kamala away from Eli, who is frustrated from failing at the same drill for the sixth time in a row, and shakily says, “a little rag-tag, sure, but I think we could whip them up into shape."

    Clint snorts. "That's hilarious."

    "What is?"

    "How you excluded yourself from that group."

    Kate gasps. "Shut up, old man."

    Clint laughs as he dodges Kate’s swatting hand, her fingertips catching on the fabric of his jacket. He crosses his arms over his chest, laughter softening into a smile that both looks and feels paternal. (And, surprisingly, it’s not as uncomfortable as it once was.) "Look,” he says, “if anybody asks I'll be the first to call bullshit, but…” As soon as Kate raises her eyebrows and allows the slow grin to spread across her face, Clint hesitates. At her ‘go on’ gesture, he sighs, “I’ll admit you've come far, Kate."

    Fuck. Am I about to cry right now?

    For the past five months, Kate has been tracking her development on a database, and later uploading the rest of her team’s progress, mostly for her own entertainment but the impressed face that Hill makes does wonders for her ego. (Never mind the judgy comments Cassie makes every once in a while.) So it’s safe to say that it’s not as though Kate isn’t aware of her own improvements. And if she’s being honest with herself, she has intentionally been using it to push herself more and more and more–

    Yelena isn’t too happy about that.

    Kate thumbs her earring, pressing the tip of the arrow to the flesh of the fingerpad.

    Yelena is off travelling the world. And she trains me just as hard as I train myself so.

    There’s a soft, gushy warmth that sits on her chest. It smooths the sharp edges of her grin and blunts the tips of her arrows (metaphorically – I would never let my arrow tips get blunt) .

    "Thanks, Clint," she says, quick and short.

    "But we both know you have a long way to go." He must say it because the air was too thick for either of them to sit with comfortably – warmth and gushiness are not huge hallmarks of their relationship.

    Kate clicks her tongue and smirks. "Best archer of my time, Hawkeye."

    "Oh,” Clint says, “I'm sure of it, Hawkeye."

    -

    Her quiver is strapped to her armor tight. It’s a recent modification, triggered by that one time she scared away a perp, which had been incredibly embarrassing for the self-proclaimed best archer of her time. Equally quiet are her boots. Rather than modifications, that can be attributed to her practising that soft-footed walk that Yelena does around the house that has been weaponized to jump-scare Kate around corners. (Or she did around the apartment – she hasn’t had the chance to be around the house very much.)

    "Alright, what are we betting?” Out of any of them, the one not being quiet is Eli, of course. “Does Hill kill us when she finds out or not?"

    Kate smacks his arm so that she can level him with a glare.

    How many times do I have to tell this man that his deep ass voice means he can’t be quiet unless he whispers?

    Cassie does a quick surveillance scan beside the metal container they’re pressed against.

    When she ducks back and messes with the controls of her suit, her fingers tremble. "All of my money is on us getting suspended, at least,” she hushes. Her teeth scrape her upper lip. “Do you have She-Hulk's number or do we need to dive into Bishop's pockets to get us out of jail?"

    Kate pops her lips. "I wanna say that we're not that screwed but…”

    Taut, invisible strings pull the air around them, the tension palpable despite the vast space of the bay. None of them had anticipated that the nice, calm little stroll would turn into this. Not even when the stroll cut through the same area that happened to be marked with an urgent, red symbol on the map. A map that Kate isn’t supposed to have access to. Except sleep hadn’t been showing up and the nauseating feeling of insomnia had been too disturbing, so… obviously, she had to go for it. She needed a break from the disturbing case files an agent had handed her.

    Hacking the system seemed like a fun challenge to keep her mind busy.

    That was strike one.

    Hill is never happy with her snooping. Says that she can be quite intolerable when she wants to be, which is a kind way of putting it and so it hasn’t discouraged her from doing it again.

    Of course, Eli catching wind of the admittedly dangerous weapons being moved into The Bronx had been strike two.

    Strike three was letting Cassie tag along.

    Miraculously, Kate had convinced Eli not to engage the traffickers. They were only going to have a look around to gauge how bad the situation was.

    Like all good things, however, that quickly came to an end. The situation simply had to be crappier than they originally thought, which meant that Eli lost his cool and snuck into the cargo bay. And when Kate saw one of the organizers kill a trader (who they had watched end a call with his son before entering the bay with the promise of pizza) in cold blood, she lost her cool, and that had been Cassie’s permission to go all in too.

    A collective anger had swept over them, then. Wrapped up in their emotions, they crept further into the bay and surveilled how many crates had been slipped out of the metal containers. When there were only three men to take down between them, the situation seemed positive. But then Eli had heard the name of somebody he knew and the comment had struck him like a match, incensing the already high emotions that had gotten them there in the first place. He misstepped and sent a piece scattering across the floor, the sound earsplitting. A forcefield encased the warehouse, trapping them in like three little rats that were stupid enough to fall for a trap. They have no contact with headquarters now.

    At least the cliche, red lights and blaring alarms are a nice treat.

    Kate rubs her ear.

    Eli grits his teeth so hard that it carries over the sound. "How did we get here?"

    "By car?” Kate says.

    Footsteps thunder into the bay, where there are weapons with highly reactive materials that could make this whole place go ka-boom. They must have noticed that the guards stopped answering their walkie-talkies. Even worse, they must have realized somebody (Kate) tried impersonating them.

    Cassie continues to mess with the controls on her suit, muttering, "Fuck fuck fuck," under her breath.

    "Why are we here?"

    "Cool it with the existentialism, Bradley,” Kate scowls. Now that their backs are against the wall, he starts to regret. Pulling an arrow from her quiver, Kate shifts her weight on her feet and angles herself around the corner of the container, intending to get a visual of the people running into the bay. “I think backup is coming."

    "For them or for us?" Cassie’s smile quivers.

    They exchange a glance.

    The majority of the footsteps soften into a dull murmur, leaving the guards in position to counter their ambush. Eli spots the apprehension in both their expressions and clenches his fists. "Crap."

    "I am so getting kicked out of the project," Cassie says.

    "It's not that serious, Cas,” Kate says. If she’s honest, she’s not too sure whether it is that serious or not, but there is no point in dreading what’s to come later if it’s not certain there will even be a later. She spins on her team, determination planted squarely in her stomach. “All we have to do is wrap this up as fast as we can, kick some bad guy's ass and slip back into HQ before anyone notices we're gone."

    Eli scoffs. "Oh yeah, real simple."

    "We are so screwed,” Cassie says.

    Kate purses her lips, their lack of optimism dampening her own. "Okay, listen."

    The plan is quickly concocted. It doesn’t appease her perfectionism, but Kate is almost sure that the practice and relentless case studies she’s been reading will pay off because it’s pretty beautiful, given the circumstances.

    Cassie shrinks at will to perch on one of Kate’s arrows. When her grip is tight enough that the momentum won’t leave her lying in the dust, Kate releases the arrow.

    The reaction is immediate. A round of gunshots shakes the container. The acoustics of the cargo bay amplify the sound until it shakes her eardrums and prompts her to stroke her earbuds and turn the noise-cancelling on. The setting is enough to muffle the overwhelming explosions and gunshots, but not enough to prevent movement close to her – less ideal in close combat situations but perfect for this long-distance combat.

    I should probably stop thinking of this as a simulation.

    She notches an explosive arrow next, nodding to Eli who flicks the safety of his pistol.

    “Distraction in three,” Cassie says.

    Kate pulls the string back, ducks her shoulder, and on the third count releases her breath.

    As she rounds the corner, the large clump of guards aiming their rifles at her is the first thing she sees. In that split second, several rifles go off.

    The majority of their bullets slam into the metal surface of the container. One skims her ear. Two burrow in her side.

    In the next second, the arrow lands and explodes, knocking multiple men off their feet.

    Though perhaps more significant is the tall stack of containers that groan and tip – over and over with a deafening grumble. The cluster of men have to disperse or else they’ll be crushed by its weight, leaving them vulnerable to Eli’s pistol.

    She doesn’t see how many men are caught in the fall. The impact of the two bullets spins her body mid-step, interrupting her initial trajectory behind the pile of crates piled beside the container. For a moment she’s left sprawled on the ground, unprotected and with no cover to shield her from potential onslaught. She’s fortunate that Cassie’s distraction progresses from knocking cargo containers like a stack of toys to snapping rifles in two.

    Breath caught in her lungs, Kate scrabbles onto her feet.

    From her peripheral, she sees the snout of a gun pointing in her direction. Her suit may be bulletproof but there are only so many bruising/potentially bone-breaking shots she can take before one catches her in the head.

    And that would not be a cool way to die.

    Her bow scrapes the smooth concrete floor with a sharp metallic shriek. The crates may be full of dangerous weapons, but if she’s learned anything from numerous TSM stints it’s that they won’t purposefully destroy their goods. Hopefully. So she puts her figurative eggs in one basket and ducks behind them. Pressing her hand against her side, she feels the welt of a bullet with the pads of her fingers. The minor disturbance pulls it from the padding of her suit and sends it clattering on the floor with a faint tinkling sound.

    “There are guards around our exit point,” Eli says.

    There’s not enough time to let herself feel the pain, she needs to move.

    “I’m going to get some high ground,” Kate says, “Cas, can I get a distraction?”

    “On it.” A burst of shouts erupts immediately after. Kate doesn’t know what Cassie has done to prompt a reaction like that, but she has a feeling that it would make for a good clip.

    “You’re good to go.”

    “I’m going to try to clear the exit,” Eli says, “but if any of you– ack!”

    Her hands falter from where they’re notching a grapple arrow. “Bradley, you good?”

    No response.

    She shoots the arrow to a beam that shoots horizontally across the bay. Her muscles tighten as the grapple digs its legs into the metal, anticipating the tugging motion that’s to come.

    Vibrations shoot through her hands and up her forearms. The motor attached to the bow whirs to life, reeling the string in and effectively tugging Kate upward toward the beam. Her stomach swoops, giving out as she flies mid-air.

    Shit–

    A grunt pushes out of her stomach.

    I don’t know how Peter does this every day.

    The movement is clumsier than she would like – she’s no Spiderman – though it manages to get the job done. She would try to make herself feel better, but she’s seen Yelena do some crazy aerial stunts and she’s not superpowered.

    With her skill level, she may as well be.

    Using her elbows to hook onto the beam, Kate hoists herself up. Below her, chaos rages on.

    Someone must have seen her leap – not that she tried to be very discrete about it – because the squeal of bullets whizzing past her makes her jump. Kate does a two-step backwards, anticipating the bullets that shoot into the air she would have occupied had she moved forward.

    The balancing sensation is reminiscent of gymnastics competitions. If gymnastics competitions mean life or death situations.

    Her feet are sure as she runs on the beam. She heads into the chaos with determination in her steps, already doing a quick head count of enemies that are visible from her vantage point. She trusts that Eli has the exit situation handled, so considering there may be thirty guards versus one Cassie, her abilities will be better used to try to save her best friend from certain death. Though now that she watches her weave through groups of armed guards, she seems to be keeping her head above the water, appearing and reappearing like they practised in training.

    Kate ducks behind a beam that protrudes upward toward the ceiling. “Nobody touch the container near the west wing,” she says.

    The electro-arrow strikes true – right in the centre of four or five men huddled behind the metal container. Blue shards of electricity jump from the metal, shuddering through the men that were leaning on it for support.

    “I’m having some trouble with the exit,” Eli pants through the coms. He sounds winded, which, although not uncommon, is a little unsettling considering the super-soldier serum running through his veins. “Also, I just got my ass handed to me by a ninja.”

    “What?” Cassie says. “Did you hit your head?”

    “No, I swear.”

    Kate frowns. She has no idea what that means, but she knows that she can start picking off a couple of stray enemies scattered around the bay.

    A thrill shivers down her spine at the thought of the new trick arrow in her arsenal. The grin on her face is entirely fuelled by the memory of the conversation she had with Clint that first Christmas. The arrow tail is unfamiliar between her fingers, and the length of it as she notches it onto her bowstring is a new sensation. But fuck, if this isn’t a full-circle moment.

    The boomerang arrow hurtles out. And immediately deviates from its path, swooping around and smacking a man’s rifle away from Cassie’s direction. While the arrow tours around the cargo bay, she uses a normal arrow to take out a guy peeking from the side of a stack of crates. There’s no time to check whether he’s still moving or not. She takes out another man several feet beside him and has just enough time to shoot a warning arrow at one attempting to sneak up on Cassie.

    In her palm, her bow trembles. From her peripherals, she sees the blur of movement racing toward her.

    With a step, she slides out from behind the vertical beam. Raising her hand, she unfurls her fingers – then clasps. The boomerang arrow quivers from the abrupt stop, but the satisfaction of the success means she barely registers the sting in her fingers.

    Kate takes off running across the beam again.

    “Okay, uh…” Eli says with a strange voice, “I think the ninja is on our side.”

    She ducks behind another beam.

    Below, on the east side of the bay, a figure in black begins to gradually take men out. It draws the guns away from Kate and Cassie, and toward them. In the brief moment when she isn’t under fire, Kate inhales a series of sharp puffs of air, feeling the ache that throbs with her every expanse of her lungs. There is most certainly a bruise beginning to grow on her side.

    Time to put my game face on.

    Kate moves her hand to her quiver, observing the figure. The movement triggers her memory almost immediately. Her heart stutters.

    Her feet are moving – carrying her east – before she knows what’s happening. In her haste, she teeters, giving her pause as she regains hold of her balance before she can go tumbling down. Since her pulse is already haywire, the near-death experience doesn’t faze her. If anything, her hands flying out to the side make the motion of pulling an arrow out much easier.

    She stops mid-beam.

    And sticks an arrow into the shoulder of a guy shooting at the figure. The ‘ninja’ stops to watch the man scramble to the ground.

    Kate scans their surroundings.

    A net arrow is next. It catches the three men running across the chaos – away from Cassie and toward the new intruder – and bunches them up until they’re left flopping like a pile of wild fish.

    Kate – with her pulse fluttering in her throat and an amused grin spreading across her lips – watches as the woman in black tilts her chin and looks up. Looks up in her direction. Her face is shielded by the full-face mask and the goggles, but Kate knows that she’s being stared at right now. Maybe if she takes the time to observe her body language she’ll know the expression on her face, but all that matters is that she’s looking at her. The warmth that spreads through her body is too familiar for it not to be true.

    Yelena picks up the rifle the man dropped.

    There’s another approaching her from behind with a pistol. Kate decks him with an arrow just as Yelena swings around – before she can pull the trigger on him.

    Kate already knows that there’s a competitive glare hardening on Yelena’s face.

    “Hawkeye, can I get some help here?” Cassie’s voice trills through her earpiece.

    Kate turns on the balls of her feet. The brief disappointment at having her interaction interrupted is immediately wiped away by the sight that greets her. It’s no wonder that Cassie asked for help because there are about ten men fighting her in hand-to-hand combat at once, and she seems to be losing.

    “Tear-gas arrow, incoming.”

    The second Kate releases the arrow, a bullet lodges into her shoulder. Her heart leaps to her throat as the impact pushes her backwards. She loses hold of her balance.

    Oh shit.

    “Kate!”

    Fuck. This is embarrassing. The worst way to die, for sure.

    Eyes stinging, she grits her teeth and tries to reach for a cable arrow.

    This is the end. Goodbye world.

    There’s a great groaning sound as Kate collapses onto the ground. It’s earlier than she anticipated. She was sure that she still had another second to hit the concrete.

    The impact catapults the air from her lungs, blurring her vision with tears and small dancing spots.

    For a brief moment, Kate thinks that she really did die.

    Then she’s rolling onto a harder, colder surface, her entire body aching with exertion. There’s a sharp, stinging pain in her shoulder from where the bullet has caught her, and a duller, pressing sensation on her side from the earlier shots. As her cheek presses on the cold floor, Kate realizes that Cassie must have enhanced herself to make it in time for her fall. That the thing that caught her a second earlier had been her hand, massive and soft. Softer than hard concrete, anyway.

    Above them, the ceiling of the cargo bay quakes, Cassie’s enlargened form having broken the supporting beams and leaving it to keep itself up.

    Arms shaking, Kate pushes herself up.

    Footsteps clap closer to her. Then, there’s a hand sliding across her shoulder blades and another helping her up, hooked too forcefully under her armpit to tickle. The hands are warm despite the chill of the bay, and familiar around the strap of her quiver. “Kate,” Yelena says into her hair, close to her ear. “Kate, can you breathe?”

    Kate groans.

    “Good. You’re good.”

    Affronted, she grunts what sounds like, “Lena.”

    With a warm palm, Yelena cradles Kate’s cheek and guides her face so their foreheads meet. Kate inhales deeply, smelling the familiar scent of leather and the underlying sweetness interweaved in it. Every ache – every bullet-shaped bruise on her skin – feels less significant with the pressure of Yelena’s hands on her again. "I'll see you later, моя любовь ," Yelena says, pressing her mouth to the high of her cheekbone.

    The next moment she’s gone. Which is a classic Yelena move and shouldn’t really surprise her anymore, considering they have known each other for at least a year now, but it does.

    Because she misses her and it feels incredibly cruel to see her for a second, only for her to disappear the next.

    Kate clambers to her feet. Her bow has scattered a few feet away, so she feels the ache that shoots up her spine as she scoops to pick it up.

    Cassie jogs over, sidling up next to her and immediately wrapping her left arm around her shoulders. "Was that Yelena?"

    “Yeah."

    "I thought she was out of the country for the next couple of months."

    "Yeah."

    "God, I haven't seen her since New Year's.” They trudge together to the exit, where Eli is tying up the last of the men he was fighting. “She's looking good."

    Kate thinks back to Yelena’s waist and how snugly it had fit in her arms when she had held it. Long strands of blonde had fallen around her face and hooked beneath her chin like the golden frame of a painting. She thinks that her cheeks had been pink. Warm and heated from the exertion of a fight. She’s so caught up in the brief, blurry memory of Yelena that all she can say is, "Uh-huh."

    Cassie laughs, but it hardly matters. Not now, when her heart is pounding in her chest and that disquiet that has been bothering her all this time is finally flooded with the sweet relief of Yelena.
